# Native on-foot VR (replacing Explorer Cam) — design, 2026-10-13

## Status
- **Build 334925 retarget (2026-10-23): DONE for the branch's 99 addresses, built, not installed, not flown.** Elite moved 332841 -> 334473 (2026-10-20) -> 334925 (2026-10-23, `VersionInfo` 2026.10.23.334925, stamp `0x6ADA8048`); every on-foot RVA, 44 byte pins (4 rewritten: the weapon-UI and reticle `E8` rel32s and two stack-cookie loads) and 5 vtable slots now match the 334925 exe. One real struct shift: the look processor's component grew 0x30 ahead of +0x4A8, so its yaw/pitch/basis/gain offsets moved (`0x5F4 -> 0x624`, `0x5F8 -> 0x628`, `0x5B0 -> 0x5E0`, `0x7D8/0x7DC -> 0x808/0x80C`). The processor also gained a conditional extra term, which only a flight can judge (the F22/F24 gain lines). The identity pair is untouched (main's retarget brings it; merge that before any flight). Method, pins, tables: `docs\native-onfoot-retarget-334925-2026-10-23.md`; every old -> new row: `docs\native-onfoot-334925-address-table.txt`. **Eye-dump crash (10-23): FIXED in source, rig added, not flown** (the UI point sampler freed d3dcompiler_47.dll under a live blob; section "Eye-dump crash" below).

The provider logs the first version 2 accept and any version 2 size refusal; the runtime logs its first publish, each ladder step-down and the negotiated version; the d3d11 side reads the now head at every marker post (any mode) and logs its first read and the first valid head.
- **F33 (BUILT, NOT INSTALLED; `fix.native_on_foot_body_align` = off by default, live).** Body align: the seated origin moves so the game's first-person camera sits at the third-person body's pov, so the first-person arms meet the torso. No game writes.
  - **Runtime.** Frame output version 8 (`eyeAlign`, a seated-space translation, and `eyeAlignSerial`; version 6 and 7 callers read zero). The runtime folds it into the seated origin as a translation through the look compensation's route (`seated.replace`), with no origin generation bump and no currentOrigin write, and a rebase (a new seated origin) resets the applied value. The same seated space carries the render, "now" and layer poses.
  - **Controller (d3d11, `alignTick`).** The error is (cam - HMD) - pov3P in body axes (right, up, forward), minus the trims. cam is the job camera; pov3P is the head joint (neck census) plus Explorer Cam's rest offset, through the AMC matrix. HMD is the eye position of the game's list-B view (world axes). The integral takes 0.2 of the seated error per frame (5 cm at most), clamped to 0.6 m; a critically damped second-order filter (tau 250 ms) follows it; the published value moves at 5 cm per frame at most. The guard: when the error has not fallen to 90% 2 s after the offset started, the offset goes to zero for good until the key is toggled. Held while ADS, and while an input is missing or the AMC sample is over 100 ms old.
  - **Deviations from the spec, for the flight.** (a) HMD_body comes from the list-B eye position in world axes, not from the runtime's physicalHead mapped into body axes: the list-B view already gives world axes, so no seated-to-world transform is needed for it. (b) The body-to-seated rotation uses the list-B view C (world from view) and the render head R, as F31's H_M does; sign and order unverified. (c) fp_root is logged "unread": the mode-2 root cross-check is not wired. (d) The head joint is the neck census head (as the P1 probe reads it), not a skeleton slot read in the hook. (e) The rest pov offset comes from Explorer Cam's skeleton walk when that has derived one; otherwise the pov is the head joint itself plus the trims. The once-a-second line's `pov:` field says which ("derived" or "head joint + trims"). The walk does not run on foot, so the fallback is the usual case until it is wired there. (f) The runtime folds the applied offset into lookSeated; with look-to-aim on, the applied value is rotated with the look so the commanded seated offset is kept.
  - **Keys.** `native_on_foot_body_align` (off | on, live); `..._trim_right_cm`, `_trim_up_cm`, `_trim_forward_cm` (hidden, clamped to +-30).
  - **Risks.** The shoulder gap when looking down; the lean decouples (the HMD's own lean is in the error, so most of it cancels, but the trims and the pov are fixed by the rest offset); the stance swing moves the eye about 20 cm; option A (the body's own placement) later.

The visor is correct with first = left (Sean, F30 swap test), and the layer swap was persistent under first = right (F30 07:38:51). **Next flight (F31, when Sean says):** no `eye rule cockpit (first = left)` change lines; no stand-down at a mode edge (or the `re-armed at native render-mode edge` line when one happens); the visor correct with `remlok_swap_eyes = 0`; the 5 s `H_M vs C: angle` line at rest near 0 degrees and rising in a turn; `t' (written)` and `forward under H_M` side by side for the marker stutter.

## Eye-dump crash: d3dcompiler_47.dll unloaded under a live blob (2026-10-23)

- **Crash.** Pressing eye dump in native on-foot VR killed the render thread with 0xC0000005 at `d3d11.dll+0x358A35`, inside `EyeDrawSnapshot::pointSampleUiTexture` (`src\d3d11\eye_draw_snapshot.h`), reached from `captureCandidateInputs` for a large single-mip RGBA8 weapon-UI candidate texture (Elite's minidump of 14:08:47). The crash sentinel read `fault-page state=free ... covered-by-unloaded=d3dcompiler_47.dll`.
- **Cause.** The sampler compiles its 384x384 point-sample kernel with `D3DCompile` from a `LoadLibraryW(d3dcompiler_47.dll)` handle and called `FreeLibrary` right after, while the `ID3DBlob` `code` (and `errors`) were still alive. Their vtables live in that DLL. `code->GetBufferSize()` then read an unmapped vtable, and so would the `ComPtr` destructors. It bites only when nothing else holds the DLL; `shader_swap.cpp` and `gpu_pass_profile.cpp` keep their handles forever, which hid it whenever one of those features had run.
- **Fix.** The handle is a function-static, loaded once under the sampler's mutex (a failed load retries) and never freed, as in `gpu_pass_profile.cpp`. The blobs are scoped to the kernel-build block, so they are released with the DLL mapped on both paths; a missing `D3DCompile` export or a failed compile returns false without touching a blob. No other `LoadLibrary(d3dcompiler...)` in `src\` is followed by a `FreeLibrary`: the two in `shader_swap.cpp` never free theirs.
- **Rig.** `tools\eye_draw_snapshot_unload_test\` (build.bat `:rig_eye_draw_snapshot_unload`): its own process, `d3d11.lib` only, no `D3DCompile` of its own. It asserts the DLL is absent after the WARP device exists, runs the sampler on a 1024x768 RGBA8 grid three times (first compile, kernel reuse, a second device that recompiles) and spot-checks seven output texels each time. `eye_draw_snapshot_test` could not catch this: it links `d3dcompiler.lib` and calls `D3DCompile`, which pins the DLL. Against the unfixed header the rig dies with 0xC0000005 reading an address no loaded module covers; with the fix it passes.
- **Ruled out:** the sampler's shader or the 1024x768 source as the fault (the same dispatch passes once the DLL stays loaded).

## Build 334925 retarget (2026-10-23)

HRC, pose-evaluator and AMC offsets read unchanged by class scan.
- **Struct shift (applied).** Look processor component, +0x30 from +0x4A8: yaw delta `0x624`, pitch delta `0x628`, basis `0x5E0`, gains `0x808`/`0x80C`, proven by the aligned processor's single `movsd [rdi+...]` yaw/pitch store.
- **Not provable offline.** The processor's new conditional term (a call, a test on `comp+0x4B8`'s sub-object, a virtual call, an added term). It could change the gain F22 measures or the sign F24 checks; the next flight's look lines decide.
- **Source changes.** About 200 literal rewrites in `native_on_foot.cpp`, `native_on_foot_core.h`, `native_on_foot.h`, `explorer_cam.cpp`, `explorer_cam_core.h` and `native_frame_test.cpp`; the 4 pins; the look-component offsets; build text in refusal and log strings and the `edvr.ini` sentence. ## F79 implementation (2026-10-17)

The reach pass now applies the shared body correction only when the candidate satisfies every active arm disk. On an infeasible pair, it logs the rejected midpoint separately and applies zero, leaving each arm's own reach clamp authoritative. The 49.5 cm example is a synthetic fixture matching the measured candidate magnitude, not an exact replay of the later equipment-transition event.

Pistol support contact now selects the weapon-facing radial side from the right attachment relative to mean K2. F77/F78 attach projection is +50.07 mm; the selected edge is +41.12515 mm from K2. Captured regression fixtures cover both sides and rigid transforms. The existing mirrored curl remains: bone-proxy minimum is 1.948 mm versus 1.251 mm for authored left curl, but these distances do not verify skin clearance.

For the laser assault rifle, a coherent item/serial and fresh pose use joint-51 local anchor (3.614, 131.150, 70.473 mm) transformed through the current rig. Across 43 fresh rifle samples, residual is at most 0.00885 mm; the 22.1777 mm half-quad plausibility bound admits rifle residuals of 4.918–5.289 mm and rejects 48 captured pistol rows at 50.159–59.860 mm. Raw P14 remains the immediate fallback. The correction changes only the transverse sight origin, preserving depth and body/view offset. It is evidence for this rifle asset; rendered bolt alignment does not prove collision-ray equivalence.

F79 full validation and Epic VR install completed before this F80 work; the build receipt is preserved with the F79 release artifacts.

## F82 synchronized diagnostics and neck reach (2026-10-17)

The UI probe observes the existing attachment getter call and joins its camera/attachment snapshot to the final submit by render frame, item serial, renderer, part, instance and QPC. ## F82 flight review (2026-10-17)

The user reports hands are much steadier during normal movement across tools and weapons, while jumps still judder. With the rifle, the left shoulder pulls inward and weapon draw is delayed from empty hands and during tool-to-rifle changes; observed swap rows show the left hand dropping out before it reacquires. Preserve the live INI and +2.5 cm view offset.

The pistol optic capture contains 14 matched, fresh pose rows; its left elbow stays 23.58–23.68 cm below the shoulder and the right hand matches its commanded grip within 2.23e-5 mm. The sampled segment is about 0.56 s, all ADS-off, with one missing pose and one stale final pose. It supports the elbow-down pistol pose in this brief sample, not behavior through a head turn or jump. The repeated F82 laser-rifle 55/55 body-pin counter is not pistol evidence.

For the rifle, 12 matched optic rows show crouch weight 1 and zero applied reach; the left wrist misses its target by 7.417–8.275 cm while the right remains effectively exact. A physical-rest target counterfactual is arm-feasible across the 12 rows and passes a held-foot distance check, but assumes a single -21.5-degree local pitch and does not establish per-row pitch or anatomical skin clearance. Camera-local conversion and anchor offsets are consistent with captured transforms, but the GPU UI projection and eye route remain unverified, so no UI transform defect is established.

## F83 diagnostic tracing (local implementation, 2026-10-17)

The jump lattice samples every second source pixel for SkinE, motion vectors and EngineSlots across all 16 scheduled eye0 frames. It preserves the integer slot words, caps the run at 320 MiB, and joins only when scene frame, render frame, epoch and both eye capture sequences agree. Each row reports not reached, refused, or written; a late DO_NOT_WAIT refusal has no capture sequence or payload. Existing one-shot full-resolution SkinE captures remain available for edges and pixels.

The UI writer annotation uses the proven synchronous CPU submit path and exact active instance TLS at the existing 434D149 writer observer. It records the native render frame/QPC and joined getter identity beside the exact 336-byte record, before the generic record/byte caps; the 1024-slot and 336-KiB reserves have explicit overflow. The issue observer runs inside the existing draw bracket after marker/UI state changes and before restoration. It records effective target view, viewport/scissor, VS b0 identity/range and a deferred bounded copy; PS texture inputs retain source resource/view identities and explicit refusal metadata. The eye draw reader labels unavailable payloads instead of treating them as projection evidence. B10/DB89 and 025B/46F are candidate shader families only. `eye_run_ledger.py` checks full-record equality through the indexed t33 stream. A writer record matching multiple draw/eye uses is `exact_many_draws`; only distinct renderer/part/instance identities claiming a shared GPU tuple are `ambiguous`; zero matches are `unmatched`. A flight is still needed to verify capture coverage and compare both eye projections during head motion.

## F84 airborne and UI tracing (local implementation, 2026-10-17)

The manual eye-dump request still starts its 16-row capture immediately. When issued on foot while grounded, it also arms one 30-second companion request. A read-only native AMC root/velocity snapshot arms the companion on a fresh airborne classifier sample (`|filtered vy| > 0.8 m/s`), with a 100 ms freshness limit; feet lift alone is not a trigger. If the player is already airborne when the manual run starts, that run records the native trigger and no duplicate is queued. The companion waits through an active eye/final/lattice run, draw census, or object ledger; if the jump ends while busy, it waits for the next fresh airborne sample. Stale snapshots, busy deferrals, start refusal, timeout, on-foot exit and shutdown have explicit log states. The companion uses a unique stamp, links its parent stamp and trigger/root/velocity timing into the final JSON and lattice manifest, and starts a new census only after old census/object-ledger work is idle. Its normal 16-row pipeline writes through the same final-eye, optic and lattice completion path as a manual capture.

For the next flight, press the manual eye-dump key while grounded, wait about two seconds for that immediate run to finish, and jump within 30 seconds. A jump during an active capture is logged as deferred; a later jump is then needed if the first one lands before capture becomes free. Use the paired manual and `airborne_companion` stamps to compare pose, instance/palette, SkinE, motion-vector, engine-slot and final-eye rows around the jump. The state-machine and full-image sampling WARP tests live in `tools/eye_draw_snapshot_test`; the focused `eye_draw_snapshot` rig and Python schema/reader tests pass.

UI candidate storage now reserves 768 rows for all 16 frames, three eye buckets and 16 candidates per bucket, plus 384 non-UI rows. It preserves the exact issued target and shader identity; 025B's valid VS camera binding is b1[270..275], while its lack of b0 is recorded as unused. UI images prioritize B10 t0 and 025B t2. Small existing mips are retained whole; otherwise supported RGBA8 sources use deterministic full-source point sampling to 384x384, with source dimensions, format and coordinate/color semantics in the v15 file. Per-frame UI imagery has its own 6 MiB allowance; non-UI per-draw allowance remains 20 MiB. This remains capture-only evidence, not a UI behavior change. No flight has yet validated these F84 runtime captures.

## F81 flight review (2026-10-17)

The pistol sample at 12:19:23 kept the left elbow 19.00–21.03 cm below the shoulder, with exact hand targets and full write weights. Its 0.525 s duration is too short to establish behavior during a substantial head turn; the right elbow stayed level.

Armed `pose_state` never reached `Matched`: the validator treated nonsemantic `M3P[15]` padding (`NaN`) as a required matrix element. The affine basis and translation, joints, final torso, G and Froot were finite, so the raw rows still support affine analysis, but the logged pose join must remain invalid. Captures 12:19:40 and 12:19:46 each have 15 matched and one stale exact scene-eye rows. In the first, rifle neck-to-eye displacement is 9.3–9.9 cm up and 1.7–2.8 cm forward, with 12.02–13.20 cm applied reach matching the neck-target displacement; both wrists meet their targets. In the second, wrist target errors are about 1e-8 m. Eye-relative motion ranges are 3.74/2.50/0.89 mm left and 1.40/0.79/0.73 mm right; adjacent-frame maxima are 1.50 mm left and 0.75 mm right, and neck motion is at most 1.83 mm. The skin ledger recomputed 780,995 palette rows (maximum difference 2.38e-7); 739,890/739,890 prior rows were bit-exact across 18 pairs with no identity swaps. A captured neck matrix matches its ledger matrix to 6.7e-10, but the frame-label domains are not fully resolved, so same-draw identity is not established. Forcing crouch weight to 1 in the captured geometry worsens clearance: neck target moves 5.10–5.56 cm upward, while left-head reach deficit grows from 9.8–10.94 cm to 14.94–16.18 cm. That counterfactual does not support a fix. The standing observation at 12:16:37 had raw/normalized eye height 1.260/1.249 m; at 12:17:06 it was 1.614/1.620 m with the same calibration, a +33.8 cm normalized difference. The baseline is real, but its cause is unknown.

Four UI captures (96 rows each) show `distance_scale=false`, `facing=0`, and unchanged dimensions, scale and anchors across 384 rows, ruling out distance scaling for these samples. In capture 12:20:02, the icon shifts 110.57 px while the optic shifts 143.38 px, changing their separation by 32.81 px; depth parallax remains possible. The submit record lacks the updater camera and raw attachment, and scene-frame/render-frame differ by one, so these records cannot prove attachment motion.

Before another flight, fix the padding validity check and instrument GPU palette/motion-history identity plus the exact UI updater camera and raw attachment (a verified mid-function capture after the attachment getter can record these without another virtual call). Then capture controlled head-turn and crouch/jump samples. No further behavior change is supported by this flight.

## F78 flight review (2026-10-17)

Build verified before analysis: Epic `edvr_gfx_20261017_100939.log`, `v0.20.0-rc.2-217-g95a90224`. Captures: rifle ADS/firing `101234`, pistol `101251`, Arc Cutter `101312`. Review only; F78 remains installed.

Attachment: F78 retains the Arc Cutter through an 82.97-degree relative-angle change at 4.9 cm separation. P1 6410 is still attached at 38.1 cm; 6411 releases at 41.5 cm, with fresh inputs, unchanged identity and the explicit free-hand policy disabled. At 6412 the pair is close again (22.6 cm), but reacquisition history has restarted and weight is 0.845. This is the distance-release path, not recurrence of the angle-release bug. Around 6411 to 6415, shoulders move forward about 48 cm and the reach solver reports infeasibility with a 49.5 cm forward correction. Both hands still write while the left weight falls. The event is at 10:13:19, seven seconds after the Arc Cutter dump, and may involve the subsequent equipment transition; it is not proof of the cause of ghosting in the dump. Rifle item 7's sampled attachment and write authority are stable after acquisition.

Arc Cutter capture: current skin recomputation passes 559,037 rows; previous palettes match the preceding current data bit for bit (529,614 rows, 18/18 pairs), with no job identity swaps. Actual chains are L C35/U40/F45/H58/A67 and R C36/U41/F46/H59/A66. Render 24732 to 24733 (FP/G generations 6038 to 6039), same local job src27333/dst27334/bind620, moves model-space wrists 43.83/44.80 mm and neck 42.03 mm; removing neck translation leaves 4.33/3.52 mm. FP world proxy and eye translate about 273 mm together, agreeing within 0.031 mm. These measurements alone do not prove physical hand judder. The local body's world record is absent: neither frame's census pool contains palette 27334, eyemesh ends at 24732, and 24733's ordinary drawstate has constants but no instance/t33 buffers. Pose event history also does not include the exact dump frame; the flight reports 24 per-item suppressed events overall, not attributable to a particular item. Future capture must retain the actual local body's draw-associated world record, instance and palette identity throughout the image sequence.

Ruled out: the old 5-degree release rule as the recorded F78 Arc Cutter release cause (attachment survives the angle change and releases on the 41.5 cm sample); generic previous-palette swaps/staleness for the captured Arc Cutter rows. The exact visual ghosting cause remains open because model/world cancellation cannot be reconstructed.

Pistol: SelectedWeapon changes at 10:12:44.412; first policy row P1 3803 at .425 already has valid cup, blend 1, effective left weight 1 and targets 2/1. Its identity reset removes preceding history, so the brief correct pose is not captured. At .925, the cup palm differs from authored FP palm by distal/thumb/normal +50.0/+2.3/-76.0 mm and 158.37 degrees; right anatomical palm residual is zero. The commanded contact lies +27.6 mm right, 29.9 mm down and 6.1 mm forward of right K2 in body axes. The image confirms the displaced open support palm. This is the selected target, not failed convergence or delayed attachment. Actual indexed geometry now also excludes the watched 154F rigid family as the pistol: 37 first-right-eye instances have nearest origins 8.55 m away and nearest decoded vertices over 6.9 m away. The nearby six-vertex 025B quad is not the pistol butt. The open replacement-contact question here was later answered by F79's measured contact side; F80 uses the natural anatomical target for pistol posing.

Rifle: all six optic candidate draws have valid retained indices/vertices. The dot lies on a 44.355 mm square quad. Fresh matched R01/render 21948 gives dot (411.90,741.15), visual bolt endpoint (371.63,705.93), a +40.27/+35.22 pixel difference; R02/render 21949 gives +41.81/+34.07 pixels. Ray/quad-plane reconstruction places the dot at +3.407 mm right/-3.503 mm up/+331.487 mm forward of P14 for R01 and +3.481/-3.577/+336.661 mm for R02. The reconstructed dot remains at the same quad-local location within about 0.09 mm. P14 forward agrees with the rendered bolt within about 0.41 pixel; eye-to-P14-line errors are 0.393/0.606 mm. Thus the geometry supports an optic-anchor correction, not a new body-offset compensation. Preserve the working pistol ADS and manual offset. First row R00 is stale and is not used to establish this P14 relation. The rendered bolt remains distinct from a verified collision ray. T2 is explicitly refused: a 3525x3133 RGBA8 single-mip texture exceeds the per-attempt budget; the retained quad and dot image suffice for this reconstruction. Ignored reproducible helper: `build/f78_optic_review.py`.

Measured rifle correction candidate: quad-local anchor approximately (0,0,0.115), or 5.10 mm along local Z from the quad centre, with approximately 0.1 mm image/reconstruction uncertainty. For live matched optic anchor A, P14 position P, forward unit F and existing depth k, use `E = P + k*F + (I - F*transpose(F))*(A - P)`. Fresh-frame mean transverse correction is +3.44 mm right/-3.54 mm up, with no forward change. Reprojection predicts about 1.5 px residual at the captured 7.85 m range. This is evidence for this rifle asset only; production needs a reliable matching optic transform/identity or validated equivalent rig-local anchor. A universal P14 offset is unsupported. No production correction was made in this review.

## F77 flight review and F78 work (2026-10-17)

Evidence: Epic `093903` matches F77 `e7a4f120`; pistol `094115`, rifle firing/ADS `094245`. Capture `094154` is fists. The corrected optic frame identities work: `094245` has 15 matching rows and one stale row, with complete callbacks.

Confirmed: the off-hand detector uses its acquisition steadiness test as a continuous release test. At 09:42:43.460, 90 readable rifle pairs remain 33.3–35.8 cm apart but produce eight decision flips and 58 ramping calls; left grip error reaches 33.3 cm. At 09:42:48.361, a 5.3-degree relative rotation produces six flips, four actual free-hand/no-write calls out of 50, and mean weight 0.72. The right arm writes all 50 calls. F78 retains an acquired attachment through relative rotation while current inputs, identity and the existing separation rule remain valid. No angle or distance threshold increase.

The left weight also changes the body's reach allowance: `(1 - offW) * 1 m` is added to its radius, and the resulting shared horizontal correction moves both shoulders. Frame 10512 to 10513 has less than 1 mm right-target movement but 7.0 cm shoulder and 8.6 cm elbow movement as left weight rises 0.922 to 0.996. This supports the shared correction mechanism; F77 lacks the per-frame reach fields to attribute that event completely. F78 records actual write authority, target mode, support validity, radii and correction in the bounded deviation history. F77's `valid` flag also required the stable solver, so an unavailable deviation row did not necessarily mean an unwritten arm.

Ruled out: stale source/refused writes as the attachment loss in those intervals (fresh FP/G, explicit off-hand-free reason); duplicate Animate execution there (one call per P1); GPU skin palette corruption for pistol `094115` (all hard checks pass, previous-palette continuity exact). These findings do not exclude other uncaptured transitions.

Pistol reconstruction identifies the local 185-joint job `src39015/dst39016/bind1540` by reproducing its actual contact command, +35.390/-41.1255/-0.000689 mm in right K2 axes. The 91-joint FP job is `src38786/dst38787/bind248645`. The synthetic grip moves the left wrist approximately 37 mm right, 24 mm down and 82 mm forward from ordinary anatomical FP retargeting. Fresh R01 (frame 15694, epoch 2, sequence 10745) projects actual support K2 to (616,1038), authored FP K2 to (91,1064), and authored thumb tip to (95,837); the visible butt spans roughly x110–300/y810–890. This confirms a large policy displacement, but neither the authored open palm nor the synthetic contact establishes a correct grasp. No guessed placement or orientation adjustment is justified. Retained 154F candidates all have zero skin palette base and do not recover the local hands.

Rifle dot ownership is now proven by the issued draw's before/after ROI: VS 025B4B9FF54622ED / PS 46F92DC71BF8DFA5, right draw 32, render 22759, ordinal 617 adds the localized white dot. R00 dot (412,733) versus rendered bolt endpoint (378.77,695.87); R08 dot (416,727) versus endpoint (381.15,687.57). P14 forward agrees with the visual bolt within about a pixel, and eye/P14 perpendicular error is 0.0047/0.0175 cm. This is rendered-shot evidence, not a verified collision ray.

F77 candidate geometry is incomplete because the candidate flag wrongly selects unpacked stream addressing: VB1 retained bytes starting at zero, while the quad starts at base 2955444 × stride 40 = 118217760. Normal captures have no retained payload there. T2 was also declined by the bounded input budget. Available depth belongs to the left eye/background, and the left optic draw adds no visible dot, so neither depth nor stereo salvages the anchor. Repair the actual capture path and test it through WARP; F77's manually populated round-trip fixture did not exercise the faulty capture branch. Do not change ADS based on the screen-space error alone.

F78 implementation: the off-hand angle test now acquires an attachment, while valid same-item/rig pairs within 40 cm retain it. Acquisition uses unique P1 frames; duplicate frames do not advance history or weight. Identity changes clear authority; frame gaps restart acquisition history without dropping an already valid attachment. Release keeps the physical elbow policy through the existing weight ramp. Scanner/Energy Link freedom, Arc Cutter handle targeting, pistol cup geometry, ADS and user settings are unchanged. New bounded policy rows distinguish successful writes from solver selection, record support/target transitions and the shared reach correction, and retain existing per-item/global event caps. Raw qualification changes alone do not consume the event budget. The missing off-hand reason label is also supplied.

F78 capture implementation: candidate per-vertex streams use actual indexed min/max windows with signed base and original binding/start offsets. Version 11 records requested versus retained texture mips and refusal descriptors. Each of the first two optic attempts has half the existing 24 MiB material budget; oversized arrays retain a fitting coarser mip chain across their view slices. Older captures remain readable, and incomplete optic geometry is reported explicitly per draw. Actual WARP capture tests exercise the observed large base, nonzero offsets/start, positive minimum indices, signed base handling and oversized T2 arrays; manually staged fixtures alone are insufficient. Focused pose validation passed 417 checks with zero failures. The full F78 validation build passed; DLL promotion and Epic verification are next.

## F76 source work (2026-10-17)

F74 landmark evidence: left/right K1 palm dimensions are 2.4 x 1.7 cm; K2 candidates are about 4.9 x 7.0 cm. F74 armed torso applied 34.7 degrees while the recorded raw shoulder angle was near 0; F75 preserves authored stance and logs root/yaw and shoulder motion for review.

F75's elbow branch changed while the hand target, torso yaw and G age were all exact/zero. The approved solver change transports an unconstrained physical anatomical pole between successful shoulder-to-hand axes, keeps the previous solved radial only as a singular fallback, applies the existing acquisition weight, and commits state only after the full span write succeeds. The candidate chooser evaluates the outward and below constraints analytically; it uses nearest feasible angle, then minimum summed violation when no feasible arc exists. Free one-handed off-arms and unarmed paths retain their previous policy.

The left support-cup anchor moves from the wrist plane to the measured mean-K2 plane using `contactCentre + distal * supportLength / 2`; the ulnar edge, width, gap, palm orientation and finger retarget remain unchanged. Existing bounded grip completion diagnostics record W, mean K2, right attach and cup target in body and right-attach axes, plus the contact axes and source ages. F76 adds bounded, event-triggered pose history independent of F68g: 8 pre-samples, trigger, up to 8 post-samples, 8 events per minute, with 20-degree radial or 5 cm shoulder-relative elbow triggers; these values select diagnostic samples only.

The native-frame tests cover independent dense-circle constraint selection, basis invariance, reference-parallel/antipodal transport, fallback recovery, lifecycle resets, K2-plane anchoring under nonidentity transforms, and deviation-event thresholds, caps and resets. The separate synchronized optic capture records only actual right-eye image callbacks and preserves mismatched source values with freshness labels. F76 full build, clean promotion and Epic VR install passed; the flight is reviewed below.

### F76 flight review (2026-10-17)

Verified `edvr_gfx_20261017_084224.log` against `2eea460a`, version `v0.20.0-rc.2-215-g2eea460a`. Environment: SteamVR/OpenXR, headset identifier `aapvr`, 90 Hz, 2016-square input and 4032-square output per eye, DLSS preset K (DLL version unmeasured). No code changes or install during this review.

Pistol: `eye_084515_FinalCrisp_R00.bmp`, R15 and L00 show support fingers and raised thumb beside/right of the grip. At 08:45:07.249 the K2 contact target is +35.4/-41.1/0 mm in right-K2 axes, reached at full blend with 0 mm/0-degree residual and FP/G ages 0/0. Wrist swing/twist are 96.8/173.7 degrees. This proves the F76 target executed, not physical skin clearance or a natural pose. At 08:45:15.665 the pistol is ADS-off; +2.5 cm body/view offset is active at 15.707.

At that measured pose the target is 27.0 mm right, 30.2 mm down and 6.7 mm back from mean K2 in body axes. The retained -41.1 mm ulnar-side offset has body direction (+.657,-.736,-.162). The F76 wrist-plane to K2-plane change itself adds approximately 23.6 mm right, 6.5 mm up and 66.4 mm forward at this pose; it is not a lateral recenter. Actual weapon-base/skin contact remains to be derived before another placement change.

Elbows: rifle item 2/rig 2 event 2 at 08:44:31.707 moves the right elbow 34.3 cm relative to its shoulder in 11.3 ms, radial angle 108.2 degrees, with unchanged hand, about 1 mm shoulder movement, 0.2-degree preferred-pole change, full weight and source ages 0/0. Event 3 at 33.575 reverses by 108.0 degrees/35.0 cm. Left events 6-8 show 104.6-120.4-degree flips with hand motion at most 5 mm. Later right events reach 165.7 and 174.1 degrees. Events involving acquisition or large target/shoulder motion are separate and do not establish the same cause.

The direction reversal is recoverable without raw clavicle X: event 2 has elbow-minus-shoulder X +24.2 cm then -6.0 cm, both below shoulder and both zero constraint violation. With the logged 6 cm elbow-out setting and the actual-written-pose violation formula, these require outward signs +1 then -1; event 3 requires the reverse. `native_on_foot.cpp` derives that sign from `g_pfzAnim[C][12] >= 0`; pin stage 0 translates every joint, including the clavicle, before this read. Thus the constraint depends on model translation. The per-event raw clavicle coordinate and relative contributions of animation and body-pin translation are not retained.

Coverage at 08:45:25.127: 4668 fresh samples; valid written L/R 4157/4668; 16 events, 36 cap-suppressed, 11 coalesced, 9 continuity resets, 1 invalid post row, zero ring drops. The cap uses absolute QPC minute buckets. Pistol item 3 starts at 08:45:06.743; no later pistol event was retained, which cannot certify stability under suppression.

Rifle: all 16 `084458` optic rows identify ADS-active laser rifle. Callback coverage is 16/16, with no missing/duplicate rows. Automatic classification is 14 unmatched/2 stale because two counter domains were compared: temporal scene is zero-based, while the draw ledger's prospective render frame is one-based. Canonical proof uses `draws_084458.bin` crop[k], the SAME optic raw row[k], and matching image epoch/sequence. ADS/FP/root-read/eye identities equal that render frame at all 16 indices, and camera rows match the same canonical draw to under 1e-7. Correct fresh images are R01-R14; R00 and R15 are stale (about 110.6 and 1906.6 ms). The earlier shifted-row numeric join is discarded. F77 records the explicit render identity at scheduling rather than assuming a fixed counter offset.

For image R08, temporal scene 18181 corresponds to canonical render 18182/epoch 9/sequence 9772 and optic raw row 8. In the native 1400-square crop the visible dot is approximately (417,755), the exact bolt far endpoint projects to (381.99748,718.95943), and P14-forward at 10 m projects to (380.88694,718.38300). The dot is about 35 px right and 36 px below the endpoint. The ADS summary at 08:44:59.049 reports perpendicular mean/max 0.02/0.04 cm and pin discrepancy 0.01 cm. This confirms the rendered dot/effect discrepancy and rejects a failed eye lock; it does not identify the collision ray. Pistol `084515` and repair tool `084542` are ADS-inactive, not ADS validation captures.

Further F77 investigation: skin frame 19322, local 185-joint job src 40435/dst 40436/bind 625, independently reproduces the pistol contact target within 0.011 mm. Actual left posed K2 differs from the carried rest landmark by 6.94 mm (centroid 3.47 mm); FP right differs by 6.78 mm. Ruled out: this posed/rest proxy mismatch as the gross lateral-placement explanation. The target's frame permutation is 120 degrees and its placement is a policy, not a measured pistol-butt contact. The pistol eyemesh contains 4096 draws/11528 dropped, an empty supplemental geometry section, and no late candidate VB/IB data after the stream budget was consumed. Candidate skinned VS `154FB5A453F3D2E9` is not uniquely identified as pistol.

Rifle candidate VS `025B4B9FF54622ED` / PS `46F92DC71BF8DFA5` draws a six-index quad after the rifle glow strips. Its VS uses ordinary rigid-pool world-minus-eye projection; PS uses mesh UV/material sampling (b2/t1/t2/t3), with no view-direction pixel math. This excludes camera-dependent pixel math only for that candidate. Packed VB1/index data and sampled alpha are absent; supplemental geometry was unarmed, not budget-declined. A bounded before/after contribution capture must identify the dot draw before deriving its world texel anchor. No numerical pistol or ADS correction is justified yet.

## F77 changes (2026-10-17)

The elbow-out sign now comes from anatomical plan side (left -1, right +1), preserving the established model-X half-space while removing dependence on translated clavicle position. The regression translates each arm across model X zero and checks the same shoulder-relative solution and outward/below constraints. Existing pose-event rows retain their fields and add the sign plus actual/required outward distance. The event budget is eight per active item serial per minute and 32 globally; separate counters identify item/global suppression. These thresholds affect diagnostics only.

FinalCrisp scheduling records explicit native render identity beside its unchanged temporal scene, epoch and sequence. The callback uses that stored identity, including for delayed or missing callbacks; zero/unset render frames cannot pass matching. No fixed counter offset is assumed. The source samples and freshness failures remain available in the manifest.

The targeted candidate sidecar reserves independent capture capacity for the observed hand/held-item shader family and exact suspected rifle optic pair. Both are candidates until the geometry/contribution evidence identifies them. It retains bounded draw inputs and frames, with explicit refusals and missing data, so environment geometry cannot consume this budget. Limits: first three mapped render frames, 192 draws, 64 MiB pool data, 32 MiB vertex streams, 24 MiB material inputs and 8 MiB ROI. The first two optic draws receive alpha-first mip/material/sampler capture and centered 512-square before/after regions; at the measured 2016-square source size the dot falls inside that crop. Issued, skipped and unknown draws remain distinct; incomplete pairs cannot establish shader contribution. Pistol contact policy, rifle aim, body offset and floating UI remain unchanged.

Validation: `build/F77-full-build.log` ends with all gates passed; 413 native-frame checks, zero failures; 164 pooled jobs plus the serial checks and installer resource validation. The new nonempty C++ fixture round-trips shifted packed VB/IB windows, frame domains, material payload, sampler and ROI pairs through the Python reader. Full-pass input SHA-256 is `d21f6de1ba7faaa3aedacf8092279fe77b91fe7b1f4bfe34890e9e77dc074513`. Earlier attempts stopped on sandbox temporary-directory permissions, the Status line limit, and stale v9 fixture expectations; those gates pass in the final run. Parent reviewed the source and full-build result; Luna owns the clean DLL promotion and sanctioned Epic VR install without `--ini`.

## What "finished" means

Explorer Cam goes. Pressing nothing, the commander leaves the ship in native
stereo, with a usable HUD, inventory, weapons and menus, and returns aboard
unchanged. Six workstreams, in dependency order:

| # | Work | Size | Risk |
|---|---|---|---|
| W1 | Activation: hook the setter's vtable slot, swap 5→3 only when admitted | small (CodeHook pattern, ~300 lines + rig) | low; addresses verified |
| W2 | EDVR frame path: re-key on-foot gates from "journal on foot" to "eyes are stereo" | medium; ~6 gates | medium; regressions on the panel path |
| W3 | HUD: port the on-foot UI elements into projected 3D space (three stages, below) | **large**; the bulk of the arc | high; RE + several flights |
| W4 | Contexts: inventory cursor, radial menus, maps, contacts, SRV, elevators, Apex, loading, intro video, deaths | medium; mostly per-context admission | medium |
| W5 | Remove Explorer Cam (6 files, ~5.7k lines, 8 keys, F5 hotkey, F8 page) | small | none, once W1-W4 fly; keys quoted and asked first |
| W6 | Body and view: show a full body like Explorer Cam's, with a ~200 ms damper on the view (not body yaw) | medium | medium; the neck census decides the damper's shape |

### W1 — activation

Install pattern exactly as `skin_entity_hook.cpp:22-26` / `explorer_cam.cpp`:
PE timestamp + image size identity (`explorer_cam.cpp:410-423`), SEH-guarded
prologue compare (`sehCheckBytes`), refuse-and-log on mismatch. Hook the
vtable slot (`vtable_hook.h:115-146`) rather than the body: the entry is a
0x21-byte .pdata stub chained into two more ranges, so a 5-byte detour is
fine but the slot is simpler. The handoff's rules carry over: forward once,
same object, caller thread; never call the setter ourselves; never write
+0x20/+0x3694/+0x3698; unknown identity → passthrough.

Admission (the handoff's own warning: an incoming 5 alone is not an on-foot
detector). Swap only when the journal's `Disembark` arrived, or Status.json
says OnFoot, within the last N s; never for the intro video, galaxy map or
loading screens. W4 grows this list; F0 measures what the natural 5-requests
actually are.

Note from the disassembly: the setter dispatches by mode class (jump table
0x28724EC: 1 → 0x2872467, 2 → 0x287247E, 3..6 → 0x287248C), so 5→3 stays
inside the same backend class. That is consistent with the handoff seeing no
resource transition fault.

### W2 — EDVR's on-foot gates

From the code map, these key on "on foot" and assume a mono panel:
`vr_world_route.h:4-17` (route owns the frame on foot);
`native_temporal.cpp:384,453` (eye shift suppressed, screen draw taken);
`ui_layer.cpp:2366-2372` (journal on foot + screen-depth draws);
`temporal_pass.cpp:1545-1553` (footSplitNow); `native_frame.cpp:307-311`
(deferred pacing on foot); `vscreen.cpp:988-996,5819` (16:9 panel shape);
`transition_flash_eye_base.cpp:285-315`. Each needs a "native stereo on foot"
branch that behaves like the cockpit. The F2 skin join is NOT coupled to
Explorer Cam (`engine_velocity.cpp:2496-2502`); it stays.

RemLok, F9 (2026-10-14): in native stereo the RemLok lines are ALWAYS drawn, even in a
breathable station. F10 census_skip found no separate overlay draw: the on-foot lines are not
the cockpit overlay, and they likely sit inside a post pass (see the F10 journal). No code
change for RemLok in this build. The helmet-state handling may be another cinema-only path.

RemLok (F0 log, 10:50-10:54): in native stereo on foot the helmet lines show
the replicated nasal-line bug that the cockpit fix solved (`fix.remlok_lines =
outer`, angle 46, `remlok_fix.cpp`, matched by shape N:3 i=1 no-DSV PS0
1024x512 in `vscreen.cpp` ~2467). The startup line says `remlok lines: outer`,
but the GPU census reads `RemLok overlay -` in every 30 s window, so the match
never fired on foot. Hypotheses: (a) the on-foot overlay is a different draw
(texture size or shape); (b) the eye-draw verdict path is not reached on foot
in native mode, because an earlier on-foot gate turns it away. Discriminator:
a `dump_draws` pair on foot in native mode, helmet down and helmet up, diffed
with `tools\diff_draw_census.py`. No code change for this in F1.

### W3 — the HUD: port the on-foot UI into projected 3D space

Goal (Sean, 2026-10-13): the on-foot UI elements (radar, left panel,
inventory, radial menus, reticle, weapon label, prompts) placed in the
headset as projected 3D surfaces, not one flat HUD quad. Three stages:

1. **Does the game already place on-foot UI in 3D behind a gate?** The
   handoff restored the reticle and weapon label in both eyes in a later
   experiment, and the game re-requests cinema every frame in native mode (H4).
   F1's caller RVA locates the requester. Question: is there a 3D placement
   path, behind a gate, that the requester can reach?
2. **Element inventory from a `dump_draws` pair (cinema against native).** Per
   element: its draws, its source target and its screen rect, and whether the
   elements separate cleanly. Done offline from the dumps.
3. **Per-element placement and interaction.** Each element is body-locked,
   world-locked or head-lazy, at its own depth, composited by EDVR. Interaction
   maps a cursor or pointer onto the placed surfaces for inventory and radial
   menus. Placement choices are Sean's call after stage 2.

The machinery to composite a 2D layer into the eyes already exists (the UI
layer path, `ui_layer_math.h:8-13`, `kScreen` family at :583-587). The
handoff's runtime comparison saw the HUD resources still produced in mode 3,
with their usual PS-SRV composite consumers absent.

Fallback if stage 1 finds that the game skips the HUD render entirely in mode
3: find what skips it, starting at 0x28540B0 (mode 3..6 AND settings+0x2AE0==1
predicate, no direct callers).

### W6 — body and view

Sean: show a full body like Explorer Cam's, and damp the view. F1's census
shows the third-person body exists in native mode with zero view masks (the
F0 flight, 10:50:56: the local AMC with 15 of 54 parts instanced, including
EVASuit_Torso, EVASuit_Arms and EVASuit_Legs). The neck census (F1) says
whether the view rides the animated neck. The head joint and the camera are
not yet in one space, so the census logs both with their spaces named.
Plan: a ~200 ms damper on the view that follows the neck bob, not body yaw.
Read-only until W6 is designed.

## Proposed first flight F0 (one flight, several hypotheses)

Build on the branch, behind one temporary key `fix.native_on_foot`
(default off; listed above; removed when W1 ships as behaviour):

- the setter hook, logging every call (`native on foot: incoming=5
  effective=3 source_after=3 onfoot=… disembark_age_ms=…`) and refusing
  loudly on identity mismatch, so "never ran" is distinguishable from
  "ran and passed through";
- a once-a-second line from the runtime: submit shape per eye
  (stereo vs panel), so H3 is measured, not guessed;
- a counter per W2 gate showing which engaged while eyes were stereo.

Flight: VR in the ship, key on, disembark at a station, walk, draw a weapon,
open inventory, a radial menu and the map, board again. Then key off,
disembark again (cinema) and take `dump_draws` on foot in both modes for the
W3 census.

Decides: H3 (gate counters); W4's admission list (every natural incoming
mode and its context); W3 step 1-2 (dump comparison, offline).

## Ruled out

- ruled out: back-face culling hides the native torso, because CULL_FRONT on the matched 37080 torso draw changed nothing (F18).
- ruled out: the F52 audit's world rows are the main scene's camera, because they came from the first-person body pass (VS 7B0DC42D383F694C / PS 0DF03E64DF9DBEF1), whose camera is head-locked (F52 flight, log 163043); F53 takes them from main-scene passes only.
- ruled out: the povCamera joint as the body anchor, because it is a root child that follows neither the stance nor the upper-body twist: the 3P neck sat 21 cm right of the eye (F52 flight); F53 anchors the neck with the rest geometry.
- ruled out: the live dh as the FP raise, because it bobbed 0 to 12 cm with the pov joint's animation (F52 flight); F53 makes it a constant, off by default.
- ruled out: gating the marker patch on the scene rows agreeing with the temporal pass's rows under 1 px (F53), because the two are not meant to agree (the gate refused about 45% of frames) and the 16-frame image dump of log 034818 shows P through the scene rows following the displayed world within 5 to 10%; F62c removes the gate.

- ruled out: a mode branch that hides the HUD in 3 but shows it in 5, because
  all 17 static readers of settings+0x3694/0x3698 test the 3..6 range, never 3
  vs 5 (re_modes scan, 2026-10-13). Indirect readers are not covered.
- ruled out: the cinema request is always 5, because flight F0 (10:15:48,
  Epic, Pimax OpenXR) logged incoming=6 at disembark, with the VR world route
  engaging 2 s later. This rig's HMD mode is 4, and the main menu also runs in
  4 (10:14:01, incoming=4). The pairs are 5/3 and 6/4.

- ruled out: the on-foot RemLok overlay is the 3-vertex draw with PS 0x99C21CEB7A699821, because that is the tonemap (HDR-to-display) pass (tonemap_admit.h:39); the F1 census candidate was misidentified, caught before flight.

- ruled out: the HUD is gated on the cinema re-request loop (H4), because under the gate method the game requested HMD itself with no re-requests and still drew no HUD (F2 13:09:43-13:12:23).
- ruled out: EDVR's UI layer hides the HUD (H5), because fix.ui_quality was off from 13:25:06 through a native window 13:25:44-13:27:03, and still no HUD.
- ruled out: the HUD is gated on the stereo leaf predicate 0x28540B0, because it returns 1 in cinema (mode 6) too, from the same callers (F6).
- ruled out: a per-item eye-index match drops HUD items in stereo, because the eye-index query's only caller runs once per mode change, not per frame (F6).
- ruled out: the 0x1A36453 world-placement patch is needed, because with it off the HUD shows head-locked in camera space (F11).
- ruled out: vs:53211E8C072CD02E is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10; flight 2026-10-14 08:20, 24888 draws dropped, the lines stayed).
- ruled out: vs:4AEC439CEC7FFDCE is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10; flight 2026-10-14 08:20, 24888 draws dropped, the lines stayed).
- ruled out: vs:5E417E9DF2E7F9E6 is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10; it also drives the smoke).
- ruled out: vs:3064D7F445192FDD is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10).
- ruled out: vs:B75A6FF2CA9FA5D6 is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10).
- ruled out: vs:D1281DF454A153AD is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10).
- ruled out: vs:D95905C18B7FAD93 is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10).
- ruled out: vs:CFA91824129ECBBC is the on-foot RemLok line's draw, because skipping its draws did not remove the lines (F10).
- ruled out: T slot +0xB0 is the HUD gate, because it is the counter R+0x780>0 and nothing in the HUD reads it (static).
- CORRECTION: the game code does test the selector mode in {3, 4} at about 50 sites (the "all readers treat 3..6 alike" claim held only for settings+0x3694/0x3698).
- CORRECTION: F4's "native serves a superset of cinema's views" is withdrawn (V is an OR of content masks).
- ruled out: the HUD view node is missing in HMD mode, because 0x28A4B90 built slot 0/1 nodes once at startup and never re-ran across mode switches (F5).
- ruled out: the cinema flag W+0x38 gates the HUD, because nothing reads it (static, 4 methods).
- ruled out: the HUD is gated by a per-group enable byte (+0x30), because all 25 group vtables read 1 in cinema and native (F4).
- ruled out: the HUD is dropped by the per-item view-mask test, because that test selects views (one-hot masks) and native serves a superset of cinema's views (F4).
- ruled out: the view masks exclude the 3P body from the native view, because its masks are a superset of the FP body's (F3 14:06:20).
- ruled out: forcing the avatar fade opaque shows the third-person body in native, because the fade was 0 from 13:10:55 and the 13:11:52 eye dump shows only the game's first-person body (arms, half torso with an open neck, legs), no third-person parts.
- ruled out: "the game camera never takes the injected aim" (the F66 review's section 5), because the F67 flight's rendered view (the right eye's rows) turned with it (pitch -66.6 deg against -103 asked at 13:25:30); the F62b-F66 ratio of -1 came from camera C, which is the left eye, not the render camera (F68a).
- ruled out: F67a's view-minus-weapon loop as an ADS aim, because its actuator turns both sides of the error; the weapon ran to -75 deg (F68a).
- ruled out: a stale FP snapshot as the cause of the armed arm stutter, because the snapshot's oldest age was 16-31 ms and the hands met their computed grips at 0.00 cm; G paired the FP root and the AMC matrix of different frames about 40% of the time (F68c).
- ruled out: the game's ADS disengage as the F66 stop (ADS stayed on 1.7 s after the guard tripped); a marker scale mismatch (sigma 1.00000); a two-slot pose source (F66g).
- ruled out: G built across frames as the cause of anything in F68, because the FP P1 call came first in 15,606 of 15,606 frames (the F67 rule would have paired the same frame); F67's 15-16 ms "older" ages are the 15.6 ms millisecond tick (F68c).
- ruled out: F68b's NOT found as "the reticle is hidden in ADS", because key 10 was never tagged at all (0 of 3,616 posts; F18 writes exact zeros, which the tag refuses): F69e.
- ruled out: the armed stance's forward lean (23-25 deg) alone as the head-in-torso cause, because F67's same rifle with the same lean kept the eye 11-13 cm above the neck.
- ruled out: the camera (E) or the calibration as the head-in-torso cause, because unarmed standing reproduced (-0.2, 15.5, 8.2) with target minus neck 0.0 cm.
- ruled out: the animated legs' reach as a crouch test, because the rifle stance reads 0.70-0.89 standing (F68e's 0.80 threshold): F69a/F69d use the camera's height instead.
- ruled out: the feet-high test as a jumping signal after the pin, because the pin lifts the feet 8-16 cm in every weapon stance (every armed second filed as jumping): F69d bins jumping on |vy| alone.

- ruled out: a sight, scope or eye joint to put the ADS eye on, because no such name exists in the exe or the logs (only povCamera, handAttach, head, foot): the FP animation's own camera joint 14 is the animator's eye point (F70 RE, dumps 152230 and 152353).
- ruled out: a per-weapon ADS camera table in the code, because the exe has none; the ADS pose is the FP animation's, with joint 14 as the camera, and it is still computed in VR (F63's snapshot of P14 is fresh every frame) (F70 RE).

(2026-10-23, the hands/UI/reticle review, section 7, "New (this flight)"; flight `edvr_gfx_20261023_140448`, build 6ADB8D37)
- ruled out: G age or pairing as the judder at speed, because G was built from the same game frame in all 400 traced frames; the 15 ms "ages" in F76 rows are GetTickCount64 ticks.
- ruled out: the reach rule as a per-frame mover in this flight, because its correction was 0.00 cm in all 400 traced frames and the policy rows applied 0.0 during the sprint.
- ruled out: the FP weapon's own sway against the camera as the judder at a jog or in a jump's rise, because the grips moved at most 0.14 cm/frame median (max 0.44) relative to the eye at a jog and 0.2-0.5 cm/frame through a 22 cm pin lift.
- ruled out: the F69d standing/jumping bin as a weight step, because `pc.state` feeds only the log bins (native_on_foot.cpp:12050, 12170).
- ruled out: an Arc Cutter left-hand target switch, because the policy rows keep targets 1/1 throughout. The 5.58 vs 0.02 cm F67f figure follows the game's FP attach separation (16.6 vs 14.4 cm), which the game blends over about 9 frames.
- ruled out: the weapon UI drawn at zero disparity, because the same B10B composite draws key 10 within 0.01 px of P's per-eye projection before any patch.
- ruled out: the reticle at the far plane or at zero disparity, because its anchor is at the game's fixed 5.0 m and lands on P per eye (0.01 px before F62c, 0.00 after).
- ruled out: a moving CPU anchor for the weapon UI (F83: under 0.0002 cm in attachment space).
- ruled out: the F70d attachment-basis patch as relevant to item 2, because every part in this flight is original-facing 0.

(2026-10-23, the F86 review, section 10; flight `edvr_gfx_20261023_202639`, build 112d1d2f)
- ruled out: the renderer placing the 3P body with a matrix other than M3P, because record minus M3P was 0.00 cm in all 20,353 joined frames, including a 239.7 cm hitch step.
- ruled out: F67f's hand error after the solve as a judder measure, because it is taken in the pose's own frame. Dump 001 read 3.92 cm flat while the drawn hand moved 0-10 cm.
- ruled out: carrying the body by F_root's translation alone, because it leaves 15 cm at the wrists in dump 007's 20-degree-a-frame turn, against 0.0 for the fitted carry.
- ruled out: head pitch as the knee bend, because the LOCAL-pitch rotation was valid in every call and predicted the eye drop within 0.3 cm at -53.7 degrees.
- ruled out: lost tags or K refusals as the first-equip flicker, because tag finds equalled tagged submits (36,968) and there were 0 refusals.
- ruled out: a per-eye patch error as the charge-port dot offset, because the dots sit on P at 0.00 px. The offset is the parallax of an anchor 5 cm deep, seen 34 degrees off the plate's normal.
- ruled out: head rotation moving the prompt dots, because their plate offset changed under 5 px over a 1700 px sweep.
- ruled out: F85b or a record scale as the ADS readout shrink, because K^-1 U is a rotation and the scale is identical hip and ADS.
- ruled out: F85a's `vy` as a jump detector, because it read world Y: walking gave 1-3.7 m/s with zero up-step.
- ruled out: F85a's FP half as built, because the FP AMC's job makes no 0x4C86270 call and MGMC saw 0 submits on 334925.
- ruled out: the game's VR layout as R4 (the label's leader up-left of the socket), because unlayered (frame 0 eye 1, two dumps) the leader dot sits on the game's own dot as in flat.
- ruled out: F86c lengthening the flicker, because F85 missed for 28 s (19.3%) and F86 for 30 s (18.7%).
- ruled out: F86 causing the head-down refusals, because they equal F57's no-main-scene frames, which F85 has too.
- ruled out: an origin shift as the R1 drop, because there was no space-change event or generation change and the pitch moved -9 deg with it.
- ruled out: a quad shrink in ADS, because F86e's ADS quad equals the hip quad (14.41 x 7.21 cm). **Amended 2026-10-24 (F90): this measured the wrong part.** F86e followed part 29501 into ADS, but in ADS the game draws the readout into part 29500 (15.03 x 5.01 cm), whose quad maps 16/9 the texels per cm; the shrink is that switch (the F90 lines below).
- ruled out: the nearest of five rays as the weapon UI's readout depth, because it sat 4-11 cm in front of the surface and hit the scale floor in 62 of 135 s.

(2026-10-24, the F90 review, section 7; flight `edvr_gfx_20261024_064508`, build 30cc64e6, Pimax OpenXR)
- ruled out: the game drawing smaller digits into the UI texture in ADS, because the ADS cluster is the hip's texel size (33x26 against 34x25 samples) in dumps 064843396001/064851427002.
- ruled out: a UV-range change in ADS, because VS b2 row 6 and the quad's vertex UVs are bit-identical hip and ADS for every part.
- ruled out (refines F87's "any shrink is texture"): the shrink is the game's switch of the readout from part 29501 to part 29500, which maps 16/9 the texels per cm.
- ruled out: the pistol readout lying behind the gun in stereo depth, because its disparity puts it at 0.223 m against 0.27 m for the gun under it. "Behind" is along the barrel (past the slide's rear end).
- ruled out: F86d `panel` and F86e `at`/`angular` as eye distances since F88, because they are measured from K's origin, 7.6-13.3 cm from U's in this flight.

(2026-10-24, the F91 review, section 6; flight `edvr_gfx_20261024_080707`, build eff7b9de, Pimax OpenXR)
- ruled out: the part name id as an identity, because the same parts are 29501/29500/29498/29495 in F89 and 29510/29526/29524/29553 in F90.
- ruled out: the slot as an identity, because `f85bSlotOf` assigns slots in first-submit order, which matched only because the rifle came first in both flights.
- ruled out: F90 changing the rifle's ADS readout, because A applied to 0 parts and the readout box is 85x75 px in dump 080918297001 against 85x74 in 064843396001.
- ruled out: the ADS sight lock oscillating with the head still, because the F89 traces step 0.01-0.04 cm a frame, monotone, and `ADS eye point` perp max is at most 0.05 cm in still seconds.
- ruled out: the hold, the F88 weight and the hands in ADS, because the hold reads `frozen (ADS)`, w is 1.00 and the F87a hand residual is at most 0.02 cm.
- ruled out: DLSS or the game's sway creating the rifle's motion in steady ADS, because rifle minus world is at most 0.6 input px.
- ruled out: the game itself entering ADS for tools, because the journal ADS flag is 0 on all 35 tool seconds.

(2026-10-24, F92b and F92c; flight `edvr_gfx_20261024_092147`, build e0975724, Pimax OpenXR)
- ruled out: the game quantising the injected look, because applied(N) = asked(N-1) to 0.0028 deg rms and 0.001 deg asks are applied as asked.
- ruled out: the sight lock as the still vibration, because its error steps 0.05-0.18 mm rms in still windows with one 0.2 mm reversal in 22, and 4 of the 5 LOCK verdicts are a press's first window (the eye moving 20+ mm onto the sight).
- ruled out: the game's ADS sway or recoil recovery, because r is the game's last step with nothing left over and the rifle-in-view step (0.008-0.015 deg) matches the tremor's size.
- ruled out: the F91 'none' verdicts as a fourth cause, because they were the fit's frame misalignment.
- ruled out: a leaked private b0 (M2), because those draws used the game's b0 in every frame.
- ruled out: the F87 premise that the label group is the 3600/624-index camera-local draws, because they decode as about 1 m shells carrying the HUD texture.

(2026-10-24, F93b and F93c; flight `edvr_gfx_20261024_102707`, build ed903ba3)
- ruled out: the sight lock's own loop as the vibration, because the plant with either sight input reproduces the flight's -0.41 only when the hold lands two frames late, and white measurement noise through the loop gives -0.5 at lag 1 and nothing at lag 2.
- ruled out: lowering the hold's lag to one frame, because a one-frame-differenced random walk has the same step rms (0.081 mm either way).
- ruled out: the head aim as the remaining vibration, because it stayed with head aim off and r is flat with it on (r step 0.0002-0.0025 deg, 8 H3 still windows).
- ruled out: the 16-frame dumps for per-frame motion, because the capture stalls the frame thread.
- ruled out: the HUD placement as the label offset, because changing the HUD values did not move the labels.
- ruled out: the 3600/624 camera-local draws as the label, because they replay as about 1 m shells carrying the HUD texture.
- ruled out: a leftover private b0, because those draws used the game's b0 in every frame.
- ruled out: the reticle as the dot's anchor, because it follows the aim, not the model point.

(2026-10-24, F93d and F93e; flight `edvr_gfx_20261024_102707`, build ed903ba3)
- ruled out: the activity insert hook as the camera gate, because it is a temporary training probe and only gives events.
- ruled out: the F88 up offset as the bent knees, because the pin ignored its 7.5 cm every second.

(2026-10-24, F94a; flight `edvr_gfx_20261024_124854`, build f358d3ce)
- ruled out: the hidden head taking the stance's height (F93e) as the cure for the bent knees, because it worked as built (fists: 13.7 cm taken, hips 0.0, legs 0.996 written against 0.996 animated) and the pilot then reported "no neck": the view, not the body, stood at the wrong height (the game's camera 4 cm over the upright stance's neck, the animated eye 15.5 cm over it).

(2026-10-24, F94b; flight `edvr_gfx_20261024_124854`, build f358d3ce)
- ruled out: frames left uncorrected as the charge port label's vibration, because from 12:54:46 to 12:55:18 every draw of its key (1) was moved (left as placed 0 in every second, designed-offset draws equal to key 1's patched draws); all 929 uncorrected draws fell in 12:54:40-12:54:45, when the reticle was the only other marker (references 0).

## Archived Status lines (F48-F62a)

Moved verbatim from the Status block on 2026-10-23 (the block is held to 60 lines). Each was written when the build was made; the later entries in the Status block and the journal say what flew and what was retired.

- **F48 (built, not installed):** `fix.native_on_foot_body_align_anchor` = pov (default) | neck (F46) | clavicles (F45, ruled out). pov places the 3P def_c_povCamera_joint at the FP root, its height held at the standing value (captured at key-on and at rest). The trims default to 0 again (sliders unchanged). Census: the FP AMC's whole part table, the FP parent array, the 3P parents of 15, 34, 35, 36, 44. Temporary: the anchor key goes when the arc picks an anchor.
- **F49 (built, not installed):** a post-hook on frAnim Animate (0x741520) for the local avatar's evaluator only. `fix.native_on_foot_pose_freeze` = probe (default; measures, writes nothing) | on (holds the torso-side subtree of the hips at the rest pose while standing idle, blended over 0.3 s) | off. Hidden, live, TEMPORARY. Open: whether the skin reads the span the hook writes (the t2 row packer was not found).
- **F50 (built, not installed):** the marker fix's delta is now -(m - c), m = X0 through the marker's b0 minus P through the eye's world rows, c a per-eye baseline (30 slow-head frames, std under 0.5 px; measured about +0.036 NDC in y); gate 0.05 NDC; the 1 s line's honesty check says `not constant: masking` when c moves with pitch or roll. `fix.native_on_foot_body_align_vertical` = off (default: body height locked, up trim ignored) | on (F48). Hidden, live, TEMPORARY. Next flight: c ready in both eyes, the residual near 0 px at every head speed, no masking line; feet on the ground in body mode.
- **F51 (built, not installed):** pose freeze: the local X is chosen against an Animate X census (P match fallback); the gate is now no locomotion (weapon out freezes, ADS excluded). The body-mode raise of the view and the FP AMC records (`..._body_raise_fp`) was RETIRED in F66 (never shipped on). Next flight: the `pose freeze (F51` lines.
- **F52 (built, not installed):** the marker fix per key: every post-stub marker node is tagged (key 10, mode game and no-pose posts in place), 256 tag cells, and every tagged B10B draw is patched with its own per-key, per-eye baseline. New reference key `fix.native_on_foot_marker_ref` = temporal (default: the temporal pass's camera rows for the eye at the world rows' projection, while it has them) | world_first; hidden, live, TEMPORARY. Next flight: the `probe (F52)` lines: the world rows' source draw, world vs temporal in deg and px, keys -> records, B10B draws NOT tagged per eye.
- **F53 (built, not installed):** the pose freeze's member cap 128 -> 192 (the plan was refused at 185); body anchor default `neck` with the rest geometry (FP side + (0, -0.155, -0.161) m); the constant raise (`..._raise_cm`) RETIRED in F66. Waypoint, measure only: reference rows from main-scene passes only, cb1 census, 1 px trust against the temporal pass (untrusted frames counted), target ndc_P + e (e = 36.3 px, measured), key census, untagged draws' records, marker crop in the eye dump. Next flight: `F has 185 members`, `F53 trust`, `scene reference`, the neck-after-d line near (0, -0.155, -0.161).
- **F54 (built, not installed):** the held weapon as a MotionGraphModelComponent (MGMC) record. Always on in VR, read only: a post-hook on the MGMC submit job (0x43ED820) lists each MGMC with a non-zero mask against the FP and 3P handAttach joints; a pre-hook on 0x4C837B0 tallies its callers (AMC excluded). The FP weapon raise it fed was RETIRED in F66; the census stays. No new key. Next flight: the `weapon (F54)` lines: which MGMC sits at the hand.
- **F55 (built, not installed):** walking shift: d's horizontal source is the 3P rest point (neck) captured at idle (not moving, no ADS, head steady 1 s) plus its offset, so the torso leans with the legs. Experiment (c), the FP shoulder drop (`..._fp_shoulder_drop_cm`), was RETIRED in F66. F53/F54's flight ran with vertical ON, anchor pov, trims up 6.5 forward 8.5. Next flight: the `walking shift (F55)` and `body (F55)` lines; `body vertical: OFF` at entry.

Hidden, live, TEMPORARY. Flight 1: probe plus one nudge. Recommended body setting for this mode: Body height +9 (the menu slider; default unchanged); body_align_vertical stays off.

## Journal

- **2026-10-13, F0 flight 3 (Epic, Pimax OpenXR, build v0.19.1-25-g15eb62b5,
  10:36).** The 6 reached admission and was refused: `reason=selector_invalid`
  on both disembark requests. Cause: our check. It read owner+0x60 and
  compared it with the selector. But the selector is embedded in its owner at
  +0x60, and owner+0x60 holds the selector's vtable (the constructor 0x27FBFC0
  writes it there). The check now reads the selector's vtable, requires it to
  be the setter's (image base + 0x52E9608), and compares owner+0x60 with the
  selector by address only (d4875a12). Every setter line now shows `sel`, `vt`,
  `owner` and `owner_back`, so a refusal shows which part failed.
- **2026-10-13, F0 FLOWN OK (Epic, log edvr_gfx_20261013_104913.log, build
  v0.19.1-27-g88bba7b0).** From 10:50:56 the game's `incoming=6 effective=4
  source_after=4 admitted=yes owner_back=yes` was substituted every frame.
  Sean: full native stereo on foot; he can interact, open menus and talk to
  NPCs. NO HUD. The body shows arms and legs but no torso. Key off at 10:53:54,
  and cinema is back by 10:53:59 (the world route owns the frame). While
  substituted the game re-requested 6 about once a frame (14273 setter lines in
  three minutes, 4 MB). Gate counters at 10:54:06 read frames=16461
  world_route=1838 ui_layer=545 foot_split=28708 deferred_pacing=0
  panel_shape=26663 transition_flash=629. They are contaminated: the flag stayed
  set after the key went off (a forwarded 6 did not clear it). Fixed in F1.

  distance slider keeps the angular size by design, so it cannot spread or narrow the layout. Size
  is the control for spread: F13 adds it as the HUD's basis scale at the same depth.

  byte, with its rel32 target at 0x434ECC0. A stub passes the instance and node to a read-only probe,
  then calls the real function. The probe writes nothing to the instance. The rel32 is one 4-byte
  store inside a single 64-byte cache line, so it is single-copy atomic on x86; a qword CAS was not used.
  Reticle setting: "Reticle follows: aim | head" is approved in principle; it is built once the
- **2026-10-14, F15 built (202d39b9, NOT FLOWN).** The body RE, static, from the F3 census and
  the visibility job: the local third-person AMC already passes the job in the eye views (view
  filter 1, alpha 1.0), and its masks are a superset of the first-person AMC's. Hypothesis: the
  3P body is drawn but hidden behind or inside the FP body, and backface-culled from inside.
  E1 tests it: `fix.native_on_foot_hide_fp_body` (temporary, default off) zeroes the four masks of
  EVASuit_Arms, EVASuit_Hands and FirstPersonSkeleton on the local mode-2 AMC, after the fade and
  after headHideStep (explorer_cam.cpp fpBodyStep; the frame side is explorerCamFpBody). Nothing is
  restored, and alpha, the view filter and the flag bytes are not touched.
  Identification: mode 2 (the creation parameter at +0x14). The code's note at isLocalPair says only
  the local player has a first-person avatar, so mode 2 is taken as local. The census row for that
  AMC carries its distance from the local 3P AMC; expect about 1.65 m.
  Log lines: `native on foot body: first-person body hidden (... on AMC 0x...)` once per transition;
  `... shown again (<why>)`; `no first-person AMC (mode 2) reached the fade hook ...` after 5 s; and
  every 5 s `first-person masks after the zero: ...`, read straight after the write. The frame
  thread's own census read can land after the game's next-frame rewrite, so the readback line is
  the evidence that the zero happened.
  Reading the flight: (a) readback 0 on the three parts and the body shows in the eye views: the
  hypothesis holds. (b) readback 0 and nothing shows: the 3P body is not hidden by the FP body;

  see the FP AMC; read the census. (d) a name missing from the table: logged once, that part is skipped.
