- **Ruled out:** the F59 marker series not firing in flight, because log 034818 has three armed/complete pairs and the eyes folder 62 crops per run (under `_mk_`).
- **Rig.** native_frame_test (216 checks): the raised factor (0 at 0.5 and 0.2, 0.5 at 0.75, 1 at 1.0), the disengage ramp (1 at 0.15 m, 0.5 at 0.25, 0 at 0.35), the lateral weight and the 0.12 m clamp, the trims subtracted once (no fit d_head - trim; full fit d_head + pull - trim), the ADS lateral blend, the bend limits (8, 8, 6; -173 deg roll to -6) and the 0.35 m suspension, the full-3P rest offset, k_reach and the defaults. The F60 Sd case now passes k_reach; the F59b residual case uses the full-3P offset.
- **Not done.** Not flown, not installed. The F60 flight's before-residual was 55..58 cm with the fit engaged and about 32 cm without; if the fit's new gates do not bring it under 35 cm the bend stays suspended (watch the suspended count). Whether the fit engages with a weapon raised depends on the grip gate holding above 0.5 (the F60 lines' last gate was 0.0..0.2 in most seconds, 0.90 once); that is the flight's question. The crop centre is still the drawn marker when its read-back is fresh, else P. Temporary keys added: `fix.native_on_foot_body_fit_lateral`.

### 2026-10-16: F62b (the ADS head-aim: the headset steers the aim while aiming down sights, the view cancels the game's turn)

- **What changed.** New key `fix.native_on_foot_ads_head_aim` = on (default) | off, hidden, live, TEMPORARY, with two hidden live sign keys `_ads_head_aim_yaw_sign` and `_ads_head_aim_pitch_sign` (1 default, -1, 0 = that axis off). It applies only in native on foot with the full-3P body (body align = body, hide_fp_body and body_unclip on), the look hooks armed, look to aim not engaged and the F61 calibration off, while ADS (`journalAds`), with the weight wA (0.15 s in, 0.3 s out). Each frame (`adsAimTick`, before `lookPublishTick` and `alignTick`): (1) the headset's heading and elevation step since the last frame (the head in natural LOCAL) times wA is queued as look input through the F22 update hook (rotate-abs and pitch-abs = old + ours for one call, restored after), divided by the measured gain (the processor's delta over the whole input; fallback 1.0); (2) the processor hook splits its deltas (comp+0x5F4 yaw, +0x5F8 pitch) into ours and the player's (linear, as F22), and ours goes into the seated rotation: the yaw through the look total (`lookPoll`, about the head), the pitch through a new overlay about the seat's right axis through the head (`adsPoll`), clamped to +-60 deg; (3) the position lock wA (h - h_entry) (LOCAL) rides the eye alignment; (4) the body: F60's fit weights go to 1 and the head's target is ignored by wA (`adsFitDetach`, past F62a's disengage and 12 cm clamp), only with a fresh weapon-fit measurement. Release: the yaw is kept (a rebase); the pitch unwinds over 0.3 s by injecting the opposite while the cancel follows what the game applies, any remainder is chased 0.2 s and then dropped (logged); the lock and the body go with wA. A trip, or the feature no longer applying (the key off, on foot ended, not full 3P, look to aim engaged, the calibration on), returns the seated rotation to zero through the reset serial, the kept yaw included. The hook paths allocate, log and lock nothing (atomics; guarded reads and writes).
- **New interface: frame version 10, input version 3.** The d3d11 half needs the headset's own motion, which the seated render head cannot give while the seated space turns under it (rebuilding it from the reported head plus the published cancel is a loop with a frame of latency each way, marginally stable). Version 10 is a ladder step like 7: input version 3 adds `localHead[12]`, `localValid` and a reserved word after the version 2 words (`EDVR_NATIVE_FRAME_INPUT_SIZE_2`); the answer's retired `reservedYaw` carries the ADS pitch. The runtime asks 10 first and steps down to 9 (input version 2) for an older d3d11.dll; an older runtime's version 2 input is still read; a mismatched pair is refused. The runtime locates the head in LOCAL at the display time only for a version 10 ask.
- **Runtime beside it.** `seatedPoseFor` composes the ADS overlay (axis `seatRightAxis` of the seat's yaw, so a pitch after any yaw stays a pitch) under F61's; zero is the old pose. `lookPoll` now pivots about the head less the applied eye offset: about the head itself, `eyePoll`'s re-applied LOCAL offset moved the reported head by (I - R^-1) e. With no eye offset (every case before F62b's lock) the pivot is unchanged; look to aim with view-mode body align gets the same correction (never flown together).
- **Conventions, as the rig verifies them.** The seat convention: yaw + = left, pitch + = up. F61: a seated +a moves the camera by -a (gain 1, sign -), so a seated +a cancels an aim turn of +a. The game: yaw delta + = right, pitch delta + = up for signs 1 (both ASSUMED). A head yaw of +10 deg asks the game for -10 deg; the applied -10 deg is a seated yaw of +10 deg; the camera's net turn = aim + head - seated = +10 deg, the head's. The lock's offset is dH (LOCAL) = D (-e_body) in F40's map, so the camera's body motion diag(1, 1, -1) dH + D s is zero.
- **What it logs.** Per second while engaged or releasing, `ADS head aim (F62b): <state>, weight`: the head's yaw and pitch (LOCAL head, input version, steps, skips), injected (seat convention, the queued input, the gain measured or fallback), applied (ours) and the player's own, cancelled, the seated yaw carried and pitch (in flight, clamp hits); a second line: the camera - head residual (the camera's turn since the base, cb1 270..273 when fresh else C, less the head's turn and the player's own aim turn; ratio per axis: about 0 the view follows the head, -2 that axis's sign is inverted, -1 the camera does not take the aim there), the lock (cm, LOCAL) and the head's distance from entry, the guard's figures, the hook's writes, processor calls and refusals, the signs. Transitions: engaged, release, release complete (pitch unwound or dropped), stops (why), RUNAWAY GUARD TRIPPED (why), guard cleared; every 5 s in full-3P ADS when not engaged, why. Runtime trace: `ads_head_aim_runtime` (the first LOCAL head; every 5 s while the pitch is not zero). d3d11 log: `local head (F62b): frame input version 3 accepted`.
- **Guards.** More than 270 deg of seated yaw in 3 s; |camera - head| at least 8 deg and half the head's turn since the base, grown 4 deg over 0.5 s without falling 1 deg below its peak; a refused look-value restore. Each trips the feature off (the seated rotation to zero) until the key toggles, a sign key changes or on foot re-enters. The seated pitch is clamped to +-60 deg and the injected pitch is held inside it; the queued input is capped at 20 deg of turn.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test (230 checks): the sign and gain chain above (a measured gain of 0.5 doubles the input from the second frame; an inverted sign reads a residual of -2 x the head), the gain fallback, the LOCAL head's steps (left +, up +, the 180 deg seam, jumps refused), the 270 deg guard in 3 s (not over 4 s), the residual guard, the 60 deg clamp, the ramp (0.15 s in, 0.3 s out), the release unwind (20 deg to 0 over 0.3 s, the view still), the lock through F40's map, the body detach; version 10 with input 3 answered with the pitch and the LOCAL head, 9 with input 2 without, mismatched pairs refused. openxr_native_test: the ladder (a version 9 provider steps 10 down and a pitch it scribbles is not believed) and the overlay (after a 90 deg seat yaw a +8 deg pitch about the seat's right axis is -8 deg of reported pitch, no roll, the head in place).
- **Honest status.** Built, not installed, not flown. Nothing in the chain is measured: the injected look input reaching the aim in ADS (the pitch-abs write and the +0x5F8 delta are F22's static reads, never flown; the yaw path spun in F22-F24), the game's two signs (ASSUMED; the sign keys and the residual ratio are there to find them in one flight), the gain and the linear split, whether the game camera takes the aim's pitch in ADS (if not, the pitch ratio reads -1 and the guard trips: pitch sign 0 is the yaw-only fallback). The seated cancel's gain 1 and sign - are F61's result as the arc's analysis reports it; the F61 entry's verdict line above was not updated here.
- **Not done.** The cancel lands a frame or two after the game's turn (the runtime applies at the next WaitGetPoses), so a fast turn shows a residual of rate x latency; nothing compensates it. While aiming and moving the runtime replaces the seated space up to three times a frame (yaw, lock, pitch). Any stop returns the kept yaw to zero (the key off, a trip, leaving on foot), which turns the view by it. Temporary keys added: `fix.native_on_foot_ads_head_aim`, `fix.native_on_foot_ads_head_aim_yaw_sign`, `fix.native_on_foot_ads_head_aim_pitch_sign`.

### 2026-10-16: F62c (the marker patch without the trust gate; the waypoint series about this frame's P)

- **Evidence (log edvr_gfx_20261016_034818, the F59 16-frame image dump `eye_035139_mk_*`, marker fix off).** The drawn marker lags the displayed world by about 1.75 frames: 60 to 130 px at 4032 while the head turned at 115 to 185 deg/s, about 0.8 px per deg/s at 4032. P through the scene rows (cb1 270..273) follows the displayed world's motion within 5 to 10% on the pre-UI crops. So ndc(P through the scene rows) + e is the patch's target. F53's trust gate refused about 45% of frames, because the scene rows and the temporal pass's rows are not meant to agree (ruled out above).
- **What changed (the marker patch, `fix.native_on_foot_marker_fix`, off by default).** `drawtag::patchDecide`: a draw whose key is matched in this eye and in the other is patched on every frame where the eye's scene rows are this frame's, P has a find, both w > 0 and |dx|, |dy| < 0.1 NDC (was 0.05). Otherwise the last good delta is held for the one frame after it; otherwise the draw is refused. The trust gate is gone; its comparison stays in the F52 line as a measure. The patch measures against the scene rows (`g_cpuWorld`) whatever `fix.native_on_foot_marker_ref` says, so that key now steers only the F52 measure lines. e (36.3 px) and the one-frame hold are kept; the learned baseline c stays logged only.
- **What changed (the waypoint series, read only).** Crops are 1024x1024 (`drawtag::kMarkerDumpCrop`), centred on THIS frame's P through the eye's scene rows. P now carries its probe frame, and the capture's frame must equal it (age 0; a frame's draws and submits come before its Present). An eye-frame with P off the eye (w <= 0, or NDC outside -1..1) or with no P this frame is skipped. The CSV adds per frame and eye: the head's speed in deg/s; ndc_P with its w, key and age in frames; ndc_marker (the game's rows, read back) with its age in ms and in frames; the fix's state; whether the fix patched the eye this frame (fresh or held), with its key, draw count and dx, dy in NDC and px. The eye run's crop clamp is now `drawtag::cropOrigin` (same rule).
- **What it logs.** With the fix key on, once a second per eye, `marker fix (F62c)`: the patch-applied fraction (draws bound at the issue over decisions), the fresh decisions, the refusals (scene rows stale, record not matched per eye-frame, both-eyes rule not met, w not positive, |d| over 0.1 NDC, no P, other: ranged b0, no CPU b0 rows, no CPU o_hud), the one-frame hold's uses and the bind refusals. The series' end line counts the skipped eye-frames (off-screen, no P this frame, no P yet) and the patched ones; the self-check names the skipped crops.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test: a frame with the scene and temporal rows 5.0 px apart at P fails F53's rule and is patched onto P (scene rows) + e; the 0.1 NDC limit (0.0999 patches; 0.1 and 0.1001 are refused with nothing to hold); the one-frame hold (once after a good frame, not twice; unmatched and both-eyes refusals never hold); the 1024 crop about P at age 0 (centre (2520, 1814.4), box (2008, 1302) in a 4032 eye; a one-frame-old P, w <= 0, an NDC outside the eye or NaN skip; the edge clamp, flipV, a right-eye region).
- **Not done.** Not flown, not installed. With the fix key off nothing drawn changes: the decision runs only with the key on. The series holds 64 staging crops of 1024x1024 (256 MB at 4 bytes a texel) while it runs, and its write at the end stalls the render thread longer than the 512 series did. If a frame's submits ever came after its Present, every crop would read "no P this frame" (the CSV's P_age_frames shows it). The dump's P is the newest find of any key, as in F53 and F59; the patch columns name their own key. `fix.native_on_foot_marker_ref` no longer steers the patch; it is a removal candidate when the arc closes (not removed).

### 2026-10-16: F63 (the held match, the CPU identification, the icon offset's bias; the ADS sight lock and why-lines)

- **Why (F62 flight, log edvr_gfx_20261016_054928, on disk in the Epic edvr_logs).** The patch was accurate when applied (4 to 12 px from P up to 100 deg/s, against 60 to 130 px unpatched) but applied in only 30 to 60% of frames in motion: the read-back match went stale (8 frames) whenever the marker composite's stream was not copied. The cost lines show no slot, buffer-full or lost refusals; every uncopied draw was in a frame without a find in the pool this frame or the last (tags in flight at 25..58 of 90 Unmaps a second in motion, 90 of 90 still). Settings: body_align was OFF in that flight, so F62a and F62b never engaged; a settings issue, not a code bug.
- **Ruled out:** the stream-copy ring or the per-frame staging cap as the cause of the uncopied draws, because the F62 cost lines count 0 no slot, 0 buffer full and 0 lost read-backs in every second.
- **What changed (marker, `fix.native_on_foot_marker_fix` on only).** (1) The match is `drawtag::HeldMatch` (place in the eye, record) held 90 frames from its newest confirmation; every B10B draw at the held place is patched with no read-back that frame; a contradicting read-back or CPU identification (the held place read another record while the key was tagged elsewhere, or another record than the held one) drops it; the both-eyes rule accepts a held match for the other eye. Without a find this frame the patch takes the key's newest P (within 90 frames) and the held record as this frame's pool Unmap wrote it. (2) Staging 8 slots of 2 MB (were 6 of 1 MB; headroom, not the cure). The untagged near-camera composites (five of six B10B draws per eye in F62, at places that move with the marker's, so not identifiable by place) are not copied when the CPU identification names a record that is neither a find nor a held record. (3) **CPU identification, primary:** the marker composite draws' instance stream (one stride-8 vertex buffer) is shadowed at its Unmap, before the real one (per eye 8192 entries about the eye's newest marker composite entry, streaming loads under SEH); at a draw its entry (offset / 8 + StartInstanceLocation) names the record, and a find of that record at the pool's newest Unmap names the key in the same frame. The read-back stays as verification. It depends on the stream being CPU-mapped (WRITE_DISCARD or NO_OVERWRITE); the settlement arc saw a 1,540,096-byte DYNAMIC stream mapped that way, but this flow is not measured: an UpdateSubresource write is counted, not shadowed, and the hold stays the fallback. (4) The patch aims at e less a bias b, the EMA (alpha 0.02, clamp 0.02 NDC) of the read-back residual of fresh patched frames against the target they used (`drawtag::BiasEma`).
- **What changed (ADS, no key; full-3P body mode with the F62b head-aim only).** (5) The ADS head-aim's "aiming down sights but not engaged" line prints whenever the key is on and ADS is true (no longer full 3P only), and names the body key that is off (`full3PWhy`); the weapon fit and the spine bend print the same line in ADS when not engaged. (6) The sight lock: FP joint 14 (FP def_c_povCamera_joint, index 14 per log 054928) joins the FP Animate snapshot (before any drop write); P14w = P14_model x F_root (the whole F_root, no minus d); E_R = cam + halfIpd R^ (R^ the FP root's row 0 made perpendicular to the FP forward and signed to the body's right; halfIpd the right eye's x offset from native_temporal, s->eyes[1][3], else 0.0315 m); e = P14w - E_R less its part along the FP forward, body axes through the AMC rows, seatedFromBody(e, 0); `g_adsSight` through alignFilter (0.02 a frame, 5 cm a frame, 12 cm clamp) while the head-aim weight is above 0 with a fresh snapshot, decaying (about 0.3 s) otherwise; added to the eye alignment. Guard: |e perpendicular| growing 1 cm over 0.5 s (AdsResidualGrowth's pattern, in metres) holds it off until the next ADS engage.
- **What it logs.** Per eye a second (fix on), `marker fix (F63)`: matches by the CPU identification and by a held match, the hold's P/X0 fallback, contradictions at the draw and at read-back, confirmations, the residual of fresh patched frames against P + e (mean, max px) and the bias. `marker fix (F63): CPU identification`: the stream, Unmap copies and their time, faults, other map types, UpdateSubresource writes, draws named (a find, the held record, untagged; copies skipped), no shadow this frame, outside the windows, and the read-back's entry 0 agreeing or not. The per-frame burst names each draw's CPU record. The waypoint CSV adds resid_x_px, resid_y_px (against P + e) and e_used_x_px, e_used_y_px per frame (nan until the read-back lands). `ADS sight lock (F63)` once a second: P14w - cam (body axes), half IPD and its source, e perpendicular after (mean, max) and before (estimate), the applied lock, the weight, the refusals; GUARD TRIPPED and guard cleared lines.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test (242 checks): the hold (90 frames patched with no read-back, held at 90 and not 91, a contradicting read-back drops it, older evidence and another place say nothing, a move); the bias EMA (alpha 0.02, unpatched frames ignored, the closed loop settles on ndc_P + e, the clamp); the stream window; the sight-lock e (level and pitched 30 deg: right, up and seated; the forward part removed), the right axis's sign, the half IPD; the 12 cm clamp; the guard.
- **Not done.** Not flown, not installed. The CPU identification's premise (the stream mapped by the CPU each frame) and the read-back's agreement with it are the flight's first question. The read-back residual compares the patched rows with the rows the decision used, which F50 measured at 0.00 px; if the 0.6 px a frame drift lives in the reference itself, the bias stays near 0 and the CSV will show it. The sight lock needs the FP snapshot (taken while the arm IK is not off, or the bend is on in full 3P); R^ ignores head roll (about 5 mm at 10 deg); the before-lock figure is an estimate. No key added.

### 2026-10-16: F64 (the eye height; the marker gate at 0.4 NDC, no held delta, the newest rows within a frame, why the stamps differ)

- **Why (F63 flight, log edvr_gfx_20261016_065348, on disk in the Epic edvr_logs).** The CPU identification works; the patch applied in 82 to 92% of frames and lands within about 8 px of P when fresh, but the fastest frames still flick 100 to 305 px. The worst real lag (about 0.15 NDC at 90 deg/s) was over the 0.1 NDC gate, and the main refusal was "scene rows stale" (06:56:16, eye 0: 86 of 90 decisions; only 4 eye-frames had main-scene rows that second, while 880 world draws were refused as the first-person body's PS). Standing, the eye sat 4.2 cm below the 3P pov point; the spine bend hid it by leaning.
- **Eye height (`fix.native_on_foot_eye_height_cm`, new; slider "Eye height" on the Native on foot page, 0..15 cm in 0.5 steps, default 4, live, TEMPORARY).** Full-3P body mode with native stereo on foot only: the eye alignment adds `nofoot::raiseSeated(h)` (seated y = -h; the camera rises by h), h = the slider x a 0.3 s ramp x (1 - g_adsW), so it fades as the ADS head-aim engages (the sight lock owns ADS). Outside full 3P it is zero at once and the ramp starts again from 0, so nothing drawn changes there. Pure half: `nofoot::eyeHeightM`, `eyeHeightClampCm`.
- **Marker (`fix.native_on_foot_marker_fix` on only).** (1) `kPatchGateNdc` 0.1 -> 0.4; the w > 0 and P-found tests are unchanged. (2) F62c's one-frame hold of the last good delta is removed (`drawtag::PatchHold` is gone): a held delta is wrong by the head's acceleration. (3) The eye's newest scene rows are used when they are at most one frame older than the draw (`drawtag::sceneRowsState`: fresh, newest, none); the delta is computed through them. Two or more frames old, or none, is refused as stale. The F50 measures and the bias EMA take this frame's rows only. The waypoint CSV's patch column reads `newest-rows` where it read `held`.
- **What it logs.** `native on foot eye height (F64)`: on, or off with the full3PWhy reason, at each change; once a second while on: the key, the ramp, the ADS weight, the raise applied (now and mean), the seated y, and the eye - neck up after the raise and before it (the F59b census residual; before = after less the raise at each census frame). `marker fix (F64): eye N: applied by head speed`: in seconds with a marker-visible eye-frame, the applied fraction (a patch bound at the issue) at 0..30, 30..90, >= 90 deg/s and unknown; the eye-frames fresh, newest rows and none; the unmatched eye-frames. `marker fix (F64): eye N: scene rows not this frame's`: per eye-frame whose rows are not this frame's, the rows' age (1 accepted, 2, 3..10, over 10, never), the cause in this frame's world draws of the eye (none yet this frame, a scene draw staged without CPU rows, the tries cap, all refused as scene rows, other), those refusals by kind, and the b1 Unmap stamp (the cb1 census's newest Unmap of the b1 the newest rows came from) against the marker draw's frame, with the last three stamps. The F62c line now reads `F62c/F64` and counts the newest-rows patches where it counted the hold.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test (248 checks): the eye height's seated sign (seated y -h, body +h), the 0.3 s ramp, the ADS fade, the clamp; the gate at 0.4 NDC (0.39 and the flight's 0.15 apply, 0.4 and 0.41 refused); no hold after a good frame; the newest rows (one frame older accepted, two refused, none and ahead refused); the success tally.
- **Not done.** Not flown, not installed. The ramp is in only; out is immediate (zero outside full 3P). The fade follows the ADS head-aim weight, which stays 0 with `_ads_head_aim` off, so with that key off the eye height does not fade in ADS. The "before" eye - neck up is derived, not measured a second time. The scene filter is unchanged: whether the stale rows come from it (the first-person body PS refusals) or from the frame's draw order is the stamps line's question. Temporary key added: `fix.native_on_foot_eye_height_cm`.

### 2026-10-16: F65a (the connection retry: renamed, gated by the state at the destroy; the left hand locked to the weapon)

- **Why (connection retry).** A crash dump and 27 load cycles in the Epic logs: the 3 real stalls had their CTSA destroyed in states 5, 3 and 5; all 24 normal loads (3 Solo, 21 Training) in state 10. The 07:29 crash: on a normal state-10 Solo load the recovery's StartPending forced LoadGameActivity to start during PlayEnterGameMovie, and the game's own LGA Run read a null request handle at exe+0x1D182B3. The logs also show false "attempt 1" arms 53 to 891 s after a destroy: g_f30Dead stayed set, and a later Deactivate (an exit to the menu) armed it.
- **What changed (connection retry).** The key `fix.training_connect_fix` is `fix.connection_retry` (on/off, default on, hidden, read at launch); edvr.ini's `# moved-from: fix.training_connect_fix` makes the runtime read the old name when the new one is absent and the installer migrate it. `fix.training_connect_probe` is unchanged. A destroy (the dtor, or the owner's Remove of a tracked CTSA) opens a cycle with the CTSA's state at [act+0x30] (SEH read, before the original runs). The Deactivate arms the recovery only for an open cycle whose state was not 10 and that came within 10 s of the destroy (`nofoot::retryDecision`); any other outcome clears g_f30Dead. A cycle also ends at 10 s with no Deactivate (the AM Update hook, or the next Deactivate), at a ctor before any Deactivate, at the next destroy, and when an armed recovery is recovered or gives up. The same CTSA's dtor after its Remove (within 10 s) stays in its cycle. The F30 log prefix "training connect fix" is now "connection retry".
- **What it logs (connection retry).** At launch: `connection retry (F65a): fix.connection_retry = on|off`. Per destroy cycle: `connection retry: CTSA ... destroyed (dtor | the owner's Remove) in state N; Deactivate X s after the destroy | none within 10 s; armed | not armed: reached sessions (state 10) | not armed: no Deactivate within 10 s | not armed: the state at the destroy was not read | not armed: the game built a new CTSA before a Deactivate | not armed: another CTSA was destroyed before a Deactivate | not armed: fix.connection_retry is off`.
- **Why (weapon).** F64 flight: the FP weapon is world-fixed (the FP root's world std 0.0 cm over 48 s) while the body follows the head (d ranged 18 cm right and 18 cm forward). The left hand missed its grip: the distance gate gave it w 0.64..0.89 (its animated hand 45..54 cm from the grip), leaving it about 11 cm short and sliding with the head, and its reach ratio ran 0.95..1.10 (shortfall median 5 cm, max 17 cm).
- **What changed (weapon).** (1) While a weapon is held (g_f60Weapon: the grips fresh and the grip gate raised; T_A sane), a hand on its grip solves at weight 1 (`nofoot::ikArmGate`); unarmed, the distance gate as before. The fit's raised factor still reads the distance gate. (2) The reach limit, a hard limit after the body align and the F60/F62a fit (full-3P body mode, arm IK on, a weapon held): the hook publishes per arm c = (the 3P upper arm U, after the square-up and before the assist, minus T_A) - d in body axes, and R = l1 + l2 - 2 cm. The frame thread moves the horizontal d by the smallest amount that keeps |c + d| <= R for both arms (`nofoot::reachLimitDelta`: each arm's horizontal disk; a single projection, else the nearer circle crossing; infeasible: midway between the disks), ramped over 0.2 s on arm and disarm. The filter's state is untouched; the published d is the filtered d plus the correction.
- **What it logs (weapon).** `native on foot reach limit (F65a)` once a second in full 3P with the arm IK on: the weight, ticks armed and measured, constrained and infeasible, the d correction mean and max (cm); per arm (L, R with the upper-arm joint index, l1 + l2 and the limit): the drawn attach against the real grip T_A (the written solve's attach, else the animated one) mean and max cm with the written count, the weight and both gates, and shoulder-to-grip / (l1 + l2) before and after the correction, mean and max.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test: the retry gate (state 5 and 3 within 10 s arm; state 10, a Deactivate after 10 s and an unread state do not); w = 1 armed, the distance gate unarmed; the projection is zero within reach; it brings a shoulder 17 cm past reach exactly to the limit, horizontal only; two arms out of reach both satisfied at the circles' crossing; one arm alone.
- **Ruled out:** eye height as the fix for the misfit, because at slider 4 the eye already sat at the 3P pov height standing (F64).
- **Not done.** Not flown, not installed. Whether the upper-arm joints are 40 and 41 is printed, not asserted. The reach limit moves the body, not the view; how much it pulls against the head follow standing armed is the flight's question. Sean's live `edvr.ini` keeps `training_connect_fix`; the runtime reads it through the moved-from map until the line is renamed.

### 2026-10-16: F65b (stand armed: the 3P body kept standing with a weapon drawn, the feet planted)

- **Why (F64 log edvr_gfx_20261016_074547).** With a weapon drawn the game's 3P weapon stance crouches the body about 21 cm (joint 7 drops; pov 1.699 -> 1.489 m, neck 1.543 -> 1.334 m; a 1.5 cm chest curl, a 4 cm head dip and +27 deg head pitch), starting about 0.5 s after the FP hands rise and settling in about 2 s, while the FP view and weapon stay at standing height. The 3P parents (RE): the hips 7 (children 8..15, 15 the povCamera), spine 9 -> 16 -> 21 -> 24, the pelvis 10 -> the thighs; L thigh 18, calf 23, foot 103 (def_l_foot_joint), toe 130; R 17, 22, 100, 129 inferred by mirror.
- **What changed.** New key `fix.native_on_foot_stand_armed` (on/off, default on, hidden, live, TEMPORARY). The joint probe adds def_l_foot_joint and def_r_foot_joint. The census builds the legs with the arm plan (`nofoot::ikLegChain`: F = parent(foot), U = parent(F), C = parent(U); gU, gF, gH as the arms'), requires the same pelvis C, parent(C) the anchor (the freeze's LCA of the neck and the left foot, 7) and the RE's indices with the R foot confirmed by FindJoint (`nofoot::ikLegPairWhy`; refused otherwise), and the upper body UB = subtree(7) less both thigh subtrees (`nofoot::ikUpperBody`, 152 joints). Stage 0 in the Animate post-hook (arm IK on), after the freeze and before M0: y15 low-passed (tau 0.25 s); the stand height is that low-pass captured hook-side only unarmed and idle (no locomotion, no ADS) for 1 s and held while armed; delta = clamp(stand - lp, 0, cap) x the armed ramp (0.3 s; the key on, g_f60Weapon, full-3P body mode, arm IK on), cap = min over the legs of sqrt((0.995 (l1 + l2))^2 - h^2) - v. Both legs solved first (`nofoot::legSolve`: S the raised hip, T the animated foot, the pole the animated knee's part across the original hip -> foot axis; the thigh group swung about S, the calf group about the knee, the helpers rolled by their share of the twist as the arm's), then UB raised by delta in model y (the freeze's member matrices too when it wrote) and the legs' solves, into the animated copy (the bend, the square-up and the arms build on the standing pose) and into the span (all of UB, so the mesh does not tear). The foot and toe are never written. g_f48Stand no longer captures while a weapon is held (F64 re-captured the crouched 1.489 m).
- **What it logs.** Once per X (census): `native on foot stand armed (F65b): X=... legs ok|REFUSED (why); L foot (FindJoint def_l_foot_joint) 103 ...; R foot (FindJoint def_r_foot_joint) 100 = the inferred 100: CONFIRMED ...; anchor; upper body N joints`. Once a second in scope: y15 and y7 as animated, the low-pass, the stand height and its captures, delta wanted and applied, the cap and how often it bound; per leg v, h, l1, l2, the reach before and after, the knee's animated and solved interior angles, the foot error (max mm; about 0 when the solve holds), limited solves; calls raised and refused (plan, no stand height, leg, write); the legs plan and the R foot index.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test: the chains on the real 3P parents (L 18/23/103, R 17/22/100, pelvis 10, anchor 7; a different R foot refuses); UB is 152 joints with 7, 10, 15, 34, 44 and no thigh-subtree joint; the leg solve keeps the foot exactly (under 0.001 mm), the bone lengths and the knee forward; the cap (raised by it, the reach is 0.995) and delta (held at the cap, half with the ramp at 0.5; 0 unarmed, key off, no stand height, or below the stand height).
- **Not done.** Not flown, not installed. The chest curl and the head dip and pitch are not undone (only the drop); the bend sees the raised torso. The stand height needs 1 s unarmed and idle in full 3P with the arm IK on before the first draw. Whether the skin follows the UB writes for every joint (the F49 risk) is the flight's question.

### 2026-10-16: F65c (the body align's neck source: the pre-bend neck, not the census's bent one)

- **Why (F64 log, standing unarmed, slider 4, 07:47:40-53).** Before the bend the 3P neck sat 11.4 cm left of its target (range 3 to 17); the spine bend rolled the torso a mean 15 deg (up to its 20 deg clamp) to close it, leaving +0.9 cm after, so the drawn torso stood tilted (likely the misfit Sean sees). The cause: the body align's neck source is the census neck, and the census reads the model matrices after the hook wrote them, the post-bend neck (F64: "hook post-bend neck 34 and census agree"). The align saw a neck already under the eye and left d alone; the bend saw the pre-bend neck 11 cm off every frame; the lateral offset was absorbed by roll instead of by the translation d.
- **What changed.** The Animate post-hook publishes the neck 34 and the head 44 (model space, the census's convention) as they stand after stage 0 (the F65b raise) and before the bend, the square-up and the arm writes (the freeze's matrix where it wrote), with a sequence and ms. The body align takes it while fresh (`nofoot::neckSourcePick`: at most 2 frame ticks since its sequence changed and under 100 ms) through the sample's matrix as the census neck goes, else the census neck, else none (the low-passed head, as before). The neck anchor's rest source, the F38 offsets and the walking shift's kind follow the chosen neck. The F59b eye - neck residual still reads the census (what is drawn). No key.
- **What it logs.** `native on foot neck source (F65c)` once a second in full 3P: the align ticks by source (pre-bend, census, none) and the freshness; the pre-bend minus census neck (right, up, forward, cm); the align's d right and forward (mean, |max|); the bend's mean |roll| and |pitch| with their ranges and the before-bend neck error (right, up, forward). BendStats adds the |roll| and |pitch| sums.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test: the pick (fresh at 2 ticks and 100 ms; 3 ticks or 150 ms fall back to the census; none without either); the align with the pre-bend neck converges d to +11.4 cm right and the bend's input error to 0, while the census's post-bend neck leaves d at 0 and the 11.4 cm for the roll.
- **Not done.** Not flown, not installed. The success test is the flight's: standing unarmed, the before-bend lateral |error| under 2 cm and the mean |roll| under 3 deg. A hook that does not run (the arm IK off and the bend off) leaves the census neck in use.

### 2026-10-16: F66 (one solve with exogenous inputs: armed from the game, QPC dt, the loops broken, the elbow's hinge, rendered eyes, the marker's own geometry, the frame-order instrument)

- **Why (reviews\native-onfoot-body-review-2026-10-16.md; the F65 flight edvr_gfx_20261016_092626, Epic).** Judder = a 2-frame limit cycle (the bend's limits switching on the fit weight and its discrete suspension, against a reach limit reading the bent shoulder), fed by a false "weapon held" flag from hand distance, GetTickCount64 dt = 0 on about 29% of 90 Hz ticks and d read three times a frame. The elbow lost its hinge (two shortest-arc swings). ADS was not decided by the log. The marker's e was a constant against perspective geometry. The architecture rule: every stage reads only samples the game made or an earlier stage's output in the same pass.
- **F66a.** `journalParseSelectedWeapon` reads `"SelectedWeapon":` beside Flags2: none (the fists, any case, or empty), item (a weapon or tool, name kept), unknown (key missing, split across a read, null buffer). Status.json is re-read every 100 ms while native stereo is on foot (it updates at about 1 Hz otherwise). The armed flag is item; unknown counts as unarmed. The ramp is 0.3 s in and out (`g_armedBlend`, published as `g_armedW`). The arm IK's weight is the ramp alone (0 unarmed whatever the distance gate says); the old gate is kept as a logged measure.
- **F66b.** `QpcDt` (QueryPerformanceCounter, capped 50 ms; 0 for the first tick or a clock that did not advance) feeds alignTick, the armed ramp and the arm IK tick. Millisecond stamps stay for staleness.
- **F66c.** The bend's limits are interpolated by the armed ramp (unarmed 45/15/20, armed 8/8/6 deg), never switched. The suspension is the weight 1 - smoothstep(0.30, 0.45, |T - N0|), N0 the neck before the bend. The reach limit stays as a post-filter and reads U*, the shoulder of the unbent pose turned about the vertical by the square-up's angle (published by the hook, `ReachPub`, torn reads keep the last); the full move of d into the hook is F67.
- **F66d.** Swing 1 turns the upper arm's subtree about the shoulder by the rotation taking the animated frame (u0, n0, u0 x n0) to the solved one; swing 2 turns the forearm's subtree about the elbow by the flexion difference about n1 alone (`nofoot::ikHingeSwings`). The animated hinge is the joints' cross. A nearly straight arm uses (source 3) the learned forearm-joint hinge row, else (1) the last solved hinge within 200 ms, else (2) the shortest arc; source 0 is the geometric cross. The row is learned from bent samples (animated flexion over 25 deg): 30 samples, 80% on one row, mean angle under 10 deg; it replaces the cross when they differ by more than 10 deg. The hand's twist is wrap-safe and limited to +-90 deg over the forearm helpers; the upper-arm helper roll by swing 2's twist is gone. The old method's hinge error is computed, not applied.
- **F66e.** The eye probe publishes each eye's rows (`EyeRowsPub`); the eye's world position is solved from its scene rows (the clip x, y, w vanish at the eye; asymmetric frusta, origin cb1[275]). The ADS sight lock uses the solved right eye when its rows are this frame's (at most one frame behind), else the F63 model. The forward target stays as F63 (F67).
- **F66f.** The projections are decomposed from rows alone (principal point and focal scale per axis); sigma = scale_M / scale_W per axis; the texture's optical axis is from the temporal pass's tangents (`temporalPassEyeTan`, noted in `edvrTemporalAa`); the TAA jitter the scene rows carry is their principal point less the axis. The target is the unjittered P through the scene rows; the rows are patched affine (r4' = sigma r4 + u r7 and likewise r5), which reduces to today's translation at sigma 1. e is the NDC difference between the anchor and the icon's centre vertex through the draw's rows (`iconEdrawNdc`: PACKEDVERTEXDATAA decoded as the vertex shader does; the record's quaternion four unorm16, scale word 1). The mesh is read once from the composite draw's vertex buffer (a 4 KB GPU copy through the staging ring), accepted at 3 to 64 vertices that all decode finite within 64 units; its layout is a validated guess (position at +0, 16, 32 or 48 of a vertex). `kIconPx` and `BiasEma` are removed.
- **F66g.** A QPC event ring (P1 probe, Animate, submit) with the d each consumer used: the order histogram, Animate calls per P1 frame, `submit d != G's d (over 1 mm)`, the threads. Added at the overseer's request: the parity split (low bit of the local P1 frame index): means of the bend's roll and pitch, d right and forward and the neck 34 position written over even and odd Animate calls, their even-minus-odd differences and alternating-sign counts, Animate calls per parity and the distinct span buffers written per parity.
- **Removed (Sean approved).** The weapon fit: `fix.native_on_foot_body_fit_weapon`, `..._body_fit_lateral`, `fitApply`, `fitDisengage`, `FitAdsLat`, `adsFitDetach`, `g_fitBendW`, the menu sliders and the config contract entries. The raise and drop: `..._body_raise_fp`, `..._body_raise_cm`, `..._fp_shoulder_drop_cm`, `g_dropWanted` and their code paths (the FP plan `g_fdX`/`FpDropPlan` stays for the arm IK's FP snapshot; the F54 MGMC census stays, read only). `pose_freeze` stays. The five keys are hidden `retired (F66): ignored.` rows in edvr.ini at their last shipping values, and `retiredKeysNote` names a differing value once.
- **What it logs.** `armed (F66a)`: on change `SelectedWeapon "<name>" -> armed|unarmed` and per second the frames armed, the unknown count, how often the distance gate said held while unarmed, and armed with the hands not at the grips (what a holster that wrote no fists reads like). `frame clock (F66b)`: ticks with dt 0 (expect 0; F65 had about 26 of 90). `body solve (F66c)`: d before and after the reach, constrained and infeasible frames, the bend's judder count (sign flips; F65 alternated on 45 of 90), per-call |delta roll| and |delta pitch| maxima, the post-bend shoulder minus U*. `arm IK (F66d)` per arm: hinge solves, the n0 source counts, the learned row, the old method's hinge error, plane change and flexion. `ADS sight check (F66e)`: eye rows this second (both, one, none), the eyes against the camera, IPD, each eye to the sight line, which E_R the lock used. `marker fix (F66f)` per eye: sigma x and y (sigma - 1 expected near +0.008 to +0.011, pulling the dot outward), the rows' translation tx, ty, the jitter removed from P, e in px (36.3 in F65) and its source, the residual of the anchor against its target read back; `icon geometry read` or `NOT read (reason)` with the raw words. `frame order (F66g)` as above.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test: 275 checks, 0 failures. F66a: the parser (fists, items, split reads, null/empty), the ramp, weight 0 unarmed. F66b: the 64 Hz ms clock against a 90 Hz QPC run. F66c: the interpolated limits, the weight, U*, a closed-loop model. F66d: swing 1 and 2 on random targets and a 160 deg plane change (hinge within 0.01 deg, hand within 0.001 mm, bone lengths), the +-180 deg twist continuity, the sources, the row picker. F66e: the eye from synthetic rows within 0.1 mm, IPD, the sight-line distances, the lock. F66g: the alternating count, the parity means, the order by QPC. F66f: axis and scale from rows, sigma, the jitter, the affine (anchor exact, sigma 1 = the translation), the position and quaternion decode, e at 1 m and 10 m.
- **Deviations and findings.** (1) F66f has no constant fallback: the mesh decode is a guess about the vertex layout, so when it is refused the offset comes from the head-still baseline (30 slow-head frames, affine-corrected), and failing that the patch is refused as `kPatchNoIcon`. (2) The closed-loop model with the F65 rules cycles with period 8 for every k from 0.25 to 1, not the flight's period 2 (the model's cap and delay differ); with the F66 rules there are no flips and d is identical at bend amount 0 and 1. The period mismatch is unexplained, so the flight's judder count is the real test. (3) F66a treats an unknown SelectedWeapon as unarmed. (4) The ADS forward target is left to F67.
- **Not done / verify first.** Not flown, not installed. Only a flight verifies: (a) that holstering writes the fists (`armed (F66a)` returns to unarmed within a second of the holster; if it does not, the flag stays up after a holster and the `armed with the hands not at the grips` count climbs; the fix would be to AND the flag with the grip gate, which is not built); (b) the forearm hinge row (`arm IK (F66d)` learned row, and the angle to the cross; fallback: the geometric cross, then the shortest arc); (c) judder count under 2 a second; (d) IPD near 6.0 to 6.1 cm and the right eye to the sight line under 0.5 cm; (e) sigma near +0.008 to +0.011, the series residual under 2 px with no trend against the offset from the axis, and the icon mesh decode; (f) the frame order, `submit d != G's d`, and the parity split, which decides a two-slot pose source from our loop.

### 2026-10-16: F67a, F67b (ADS aim without a seated cancel; the ADS instruments) - built with F67c to F67f, not flown

- **Why (the F66 flight, log `edvr_gfx_20261016_113909`; the review's sections 5 and 6).** The residual ratio of about -1 was our own F62b cancel: the game camera never takes the injected aim, so the view's only turn was the seated cancel, which held it still while the head turned; the residual grew until the runaway guard tripped (11:43:47.287, head 21 deg right) while the game's ADS stayed on for 1.7 s more. The trip latched (`adsAimTick` cleared `kAdsTripped` only on a key toggle, a sign key or an on-foot re-entry, and returned before the engage), so every later press printed `aiming down sights but not engaged`. The sights sat about 2 deg down-right of impact because the weapon followed the injected aim and the camera did not.
- **F67a.** Deleted: the seated yaw through the look total (`g_adsYawSeat`, `lookPublishTick` no longer adds it), the pitch overlay (`nativeFrameSetAdsPitch` is never written; version 10 stays inert), the 270 deg turn guard (`AdsTurnWindow`), the camera-minus-head residual guard (`AdsResidualGrowth` and its camera/head frames), the pitch clamp and the release unwind, and `kAdsTripped`. Kept: the position lock, the game-thread look hook (`adsUpdate`, the queue, the processor's applied deltas and the measured gain), the sign keys. `adsAimStep` (frame thread, after the sight lock in `alignTick`) reads the right eye's scene rows (`eyeRowsRead(1)`, fresh) and the FP root (`ikGRead`, fresh): the view's axes are `rotAxesFromClip`, the weapon's forward is G's `froot` row 2. `nofoot::adsAimError` gives view minus weapon (seat convention: yaw + left, pitch + up); `AdsAimRun::ask` returns k 0.3 x the ADS weight x (error less the sum of the last three asks); the weapon's yaw offset from the body (`rotFrameDelta` of the AMC rows against the forward) is held to 20 deg by `adsConeLimit` (inside the bound it clamps; past it only a move toward the body passes). The ask goes through the sign keys and the measured gain into the existing queue. Every engage resets the in-flight window, the log window, the sight lock's guard and the queue; release drops the queue at once and the weight ramps out over 0.3 s; the one hold-off left (`g_adsStoppedPress`, a look value that could not be restored) lasts until the ADS flag falls. Nothing is published to the runtime's look, so its base stays where look to aim leaves it.
- **Design finding.** The first loop subtracted the turn the game applied from the in-flight account when it landed. With a frame between landing and the animation showing it, the account was short by that turn and the model overshot the 20 deg bound to 31 deg. The account is now the window of the last three asks (kAdsAimFlightFrames), which the model keeps stable for the game's gain 0.85, a queue latency of 1 or 2 frames and 0 to 2 frames of lag in seeing the weapon (settle within 28 frames, overshoot under 1 deg, 20.0 at the bound).
- **F67b.** The sight check line's NDC points are now P14 + 0.5 m and + 10 m along the sight in each eye (`nofoot::sightPointNdc`; P14 is behind the right eye, so F66's NDC was all zeros); the two agree when the eye is on the line. `ADS aim (F67b)` per second: the angle between the FP root's forward, the right eye's view forward and camera C's forward (mean / largest, with C relative to the view), and the right eye's image-centre ray (NDC 0, 0) against its forward and the FP root's: on a canted-display headset (Pimax) the eye's forward is the lens axis, so these separate a cant from a real aim error. `ADS cone (F67b)` when the ADS flag falls: the weapon's yaw and pitch offset from the body at the last measurement, the press's largest |yaw|, and whether the bound had just cut the ask.
- **Logs.** `ADS head aim (F62b)`: `engaged (ADS), a clean press (F67a)`, the 1 s line (head turn, applied turn, gain, asks in flight, the lock, hook counters), `release`, `release complete`. `ADS aim (F67a)` per second: frames engaged and asked, the refusals by cause (no right-eye rows, no FP root, no body sample), view minus weapon (mean, largest), asked, queued, in flight, the view's turn against the head's (equal: the camera takes none of the injected aim), the weapon's offset from the body against the bound, frames cut by the bound. If `adsAimStep` never ran, the F62b engage line appears with no `ADS aim (F67a)` line.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test (scratch build, 279 checks, 0 failures; the repo build has not run): the error's sign and units, the cone, a plant that does not take the aim (converges within 0.1 deg in 40 frames for 0 to 2 frames of lag at game gain 0.85, follows a 20 deg/s view within 2 deg), the bound (19 to 20.5 deg at a 45 deg view, no push past it, a return inside the bound is followed), reset() (a press that ran to the bound converges like a fresh one), the axis/weight/NaN cases, `dirAngleDeg`, `sightPointNdc` (on the line the points agree; 3 cm beside it 0.0741 against 0.0030; P14 refused).
- **Not done / for the flight.** Not built, not flown. The cone (20 deg) is a stand-in until `ADS cone (F67b)` measures the game's. Risk: for a canted-display headset the right eye's forward is not the head's; read the F67b angles before trusting the 0.5 deg proof. The yaw and pitch sign keys are still ASSUMED (an inverted axis shows as view minus weapon growing while the ask is large).

### 2026-10-16: F67c to F67f (the body pinned to the camera, the marker without a refusal, the aim-jump instrument, both hands on the grips) - built, not flown

- **F67c, why (the F66 flight, log `edvr_gfx_20261016_113909`; the review's section 4; Sean chose option B: the view never moves, the body follows the camera in 3D).** The 3P head moves against the camera on every draw (a 21 cm drop in 0.6 s), run and jump (camera - head swings of -50 to +40 cm); F65b's captured stand height took back 1.1 cm because it was captured at 1.587 m mid-crouch (the armed flag came after the stance had dropped) and its leg cap (6.7 cm) was set by the armed stance's spread feet.
- **F67c, what.** `pinInPublish` (P1, `ikPublishG`): the job camera and the AMC matrix every game frame, whatever the FP root is doing. `pinStage0` replaces `standStage0` in `armIkAfterAnimate` (arm IK on, full-3P, `g_pinScope`): E = the left-eye camera plus half the IPD toward the right eye from the two eyes' solved scene rows (`pinCycloUpdate`, cached 1 s), T = `nofoot::pinNeckTarget` (0.155 m down, 0.09 m back along the eye's HEADING only: the body-axes offset of the review would put the 9 cm on the wrong side of a head turned 90 deg), N0 = the animated neck 34, `nofoot::pinMove` (horizontal cap 1.5 m, vertical 0.8 m). Armed: the reach rule (F66c's `reachLimitDelta`) runs here on U* (the unbent shoulder turned by the square-up angle, now phi itself) against the grips T_A = the FP handAttach through G at d = 0, the correction times the armed ramp; the bend then closes the neck's remaining gap. Applied: every joint's translation moves horizontally (animated copy, the freeze's chain, the span); the upper body UB (subtree 7 less both thighs) moves by the signed vertical delta; each leg is planted (`nofoot::legPlant`: in reach unchanged; past 0.995 (l1 + l2) the foot is re-planted toward under the hip keeping its animated height; lifted only when straight below the hip is still out of reach; closer than the knee's 30 deg fold re-planted outward; limits widened to the animated leg's own distance) and solved (`legSolveTo`: the F65b construction with any hip and foot target); the foot and toe carry the foot's move. The whole pose is written to the span. The bend takes its target from the pin (this frame's P1), the arms from G at d = 0. Frame thread: in scope the target machinery (F37 to F66 neck, pov, walking shift, filters), the reach limit and the F66c d window are skipped, d is zero, `f58BendFrame` needs no EyeSample. `fix.native_on_foot_stand_armed` is the switch (the name is F65b's; renaming it needs a decision).
- **The bend's period-2 judder (the overseer's finding 1; cause found before compensating).** The F66g parity line at 11:41:59 had flips on 10 of 15 pitch steps and 12 of 16 roll steps, with the even and odd means equal (diff 0.01 deg). A true period-2 limit cycle flips on every step; independent random-sign steps flip on 2/3 (67-75% observed), so the steps are noise, correlated with head speed (r 0.62, 0.3-0.75 deg a frame). The game ran 62 frames a second against the frame thread's 90 Hz tick: the bend's target was the frame thread's republication (`bendTargetPublish`, every tick) of the P1 sample, so the Animate call of game frame N read a target 0 to 2 game frames old at random (the sample is refreshed at 61 Hz, the target at 90 Hz, the hook consumes at 61 Hz); the error is head speed x the age jitter (0.3 m/s x 16 ms x lever 0.5 = 0.6 deg). Fix: no hop; the target is computed in the hook from the same game frame's P1. Instrument (the `body pin` line): the P1 input's age at the Animate call, mean and largest by P1 parity, and how many calls found a new publication (should be all of them, each parity).
- **F67c, the proof lines.** `body pin (F67c)` per state (standing, walking, running, jumping, armed beside them): the target less the neck as finally written (after the bend), the eye less the neck, right/up/forward mean and largest, 3D mean/max (within 3 cm), the vertical move min/mean/max, the horizontal move; the legs (re-planted, outward, lifted per foot, not closed, hip-to-foot reach before/after, foot error); the reach limit (evaluated, active, infeasible, correction); the refusals. If the 11:47:29-35 episode (the camera 1.2 m ahead of the head for 6 s) recurs, the pin follows it up to the caps and the line shows it.
- **F67d, why and what.** The F66 marker dumps (7 series): patched frames slip 0.13 px mean (0.33 max), unpatched 2.85 (13.1 max); 188 of 224 dump eye-frames were unpatched; the F66f lines show every decision in the dump windows refused as `no icon offset` (e.g. 11:41:56: 15 of 15, with no other refusal reason), the head-still baseline absent; outside the dumps the same outages run 10-20 s whenever the head moves (the baseline needs 30 slow-head frames: my tally of the eye-0 lines is 19,568 patched draws and 6,315 refused, 24%; the overseer's cut counted 2,672 of 41,880); the CSV's 'patched in frame 0 only' pattern (frames 1-15 refused) means the baseline was lost between the series' first and second frame: a reset or restart whose cause the F50 notes (capped at 8) no longer show, so the line now counts them; the dumps themselves add the eye run's 2.4-3.2 s synchronous writes (the missing F66f lines) and nothing else. The constant fallback makes the patch independent of the baseline in all of these. Now: `kIconFallbackPx` 36.38 px as the last fallback (the draw is always patched; e from the mesh when valid, else the baseline when seeded, else the constant); the mesh from the draw's own range (`nativeOnFootMarkerProbeDraw` passes count, start and base; `iconCapture` copies the index range (up to 4 KB) and a 12 KB vertex window from the base vertex into a ring, per distinct draw configuration, up to 6; `iconOnData` reads the indices, decodes `PACKEDVERTEXDATAA` at +0/16/32/48, `discCentre` picks a fan's centre vertex, else the mean of a small mesh, else of the far cluster); e per frame is `iconEdrawNdc` of that vertex through the draw's rows and must pass a 60 px gate; the 'after patch' figure is the decoded disc through the patched rows, displayed, against P unjittered (the CSV's residual columns carry it); the line names the decode status, vertices, stride and the disc vertex. Open: the vertex layout is still a validated guess; the first decode lines show whether it holds.
- **F67e (instrument).** The hook posts a record per call (`aimPost`: the FP root's yaw from G, the 3P root's, phi wanted and applied, the pin's horizontal move, per arm the state solved/HELD/REFUSED with the reason, w, reach ratio, the hand-to-grip error before and after the solve, the shortfall, the animated hand's move since the last call, measured before the pin); the frame thread (`f67eDrain`) watches the FP yaw rate and, armed and over 30 deg/s, prints 40 `aim jump (F67e)` lines (at most 5 events a minute). Candidates: (i) the 3P yaw lags the FP aim (fp - root grows, reach over 1), (ii) the IK held or refused (state), (iii) the game's aim-offset blend (the animated hand moves, the target does not). The F66 log already shows (ii) is real: 'held: the FP snapshot is older than 100 ms' and 'G is older than 100 ms' toggled the base weight to 0-0.5 on every stall and aim move (11:41:53, 11:41:56, 11:42:02, 11:42:36-40 ...); F67f removes it whichever candidate the instrument then blames.
- **F67f (Sean's requirement).** While armed, both hands stay locked to the grips on every frame. `armIkTick`'s gate no longer reads the snapshot's or G's age (`g_ikGateOk` is the gate with the age left out); in the hook the FP snapshot is used at any age up to 30 s (a record in the FP root's frame, valid with this frame's G) and G up to 1 s (`ikPublishG` accepts an FP root up to 1 s old); the solve weight is `nofoot::ikArmedSolveWeight` (1 once the armed ramp is complete, whatever the base weight); the hand target and the clavicle assist have no 5 cm cap while armed; the square-up applies phi itself (`nofoot::squareApplied`: no clamp, ramp or amount), flagged when it exceeds the old 50 deg. The reach rule runs in the pin on these frames; `armed arms (F67f)` logs per second the armed calls, sides solved and not (with the reason), the hand-to-grip error after the solve (the proof: 0.0-0.5 cm), the shortfall after the assist (calls over 1 cm are defects), the square-up's excess, and the stale inputs used.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test 292 checks, 0 failures (scratch build; the repo build is the commit's): the pin target and heading, the move and caps, the leg planting (in reach, re-planted, lifted, folded outward, animated limits kept), `legSolveTo` onto a planted foot and a 40 cm lift, the state bins, a 2 s old snapshot with this frame's G putting the hand where the aim says, phi squared in one frame, no armed path below weight 1, the yaw-rate trigger, the icon fallback and gate, `discCentre`, `readIndexRange`.
- **Removed/changed.** F65b's `standStage0`, `StandStats`, `f65bStandTick`/`Line`, `nofoot::standLegCap`, `standDelta` and their constants. `ikPublishG` takes the job pointer. No key added or renamed.

### F68 (2026-10-16; one build, one commit; log of the F67 flight `edvr_gfx_20261016_132256`)

- **F68a, ADS back to the head's turn as aim.** `nofoot::AdsAimMap` (core) is F62b's map: each frame-thread tick cancels exactly the turn the game applied since the last tick (the processor's `comp+0x5F4`/`+0x5F8`, our share), then asks for the head's step times the ADS weight (divided by the measured gain, signs from the keys). The cancel goes out as the seated yaw (`g_adsMap.yawSeat`, added by `lookPublishTick`) and the seated pitch (`nativeFrameSetAdsPitch`, clamped to 60 deg). The in-flight account (`AdsInFlight`, 8 ticks) takes the applied turn off the oldest asks, so it stays exact while the game answers within the window; a decaying account overshot the release's unwind by 0.5 deg in the model. Release: yaw and pitch unwind by injecting the opposite over 0.3 s (60 deg/s at most, 1.5 s at most, so the frame the cancel runs behind shows as under 1 deg), a remainder is chased for 0.2 s and then dropped (logged, a view jump of that size), and the reset serial returns the runtime's look base to 0. F62b kept the yaw; the review asked for the base back at 0 and the unwind is the way that does not jump the view. `AdsAimMap::releaseDone` waits for the last asks to land. The guard (`AdsResidual`, run by `adsAimStep` after the sight lock): the right eye's rows (`rotAxesFromClip`, fresh) against the LOCAL head's axes and the player's own aim turn since the base; trips only when the residual stays at or above 8 deg and half the head's turn, grown by 4 deg, for 0.5 s; a frame without fresh rows or a head sample changes nothing; a view step over 15 deg in a tick takes a new base. A trip stops the feature (the seated rotation returns to 0) and `AdsPressGate` holds it off until the ADS flag falls, so the next press engages. The 270 deg turn guard of F62b is not restored (the review: the residual alone). Removed: `AdsAimRun`, `adsAimError`, `adsConeLimit`, the 20 deg bound, the `g_adsStoppedPress` flag (now the gate), the F67a ask and its line. Kept: the position lock, the F67b instruments (`ADS aim (F67b)` three forwards, now with the weapon's offset from the body; `ADS cone (F67b)`). Lines: `ADS head aim (F62b)` (head, injected, queued, gain, applied, cancelled, seated, in flight), `ADS aim (F68a)` (the view's turn since the base, the head's, the residual and its largest, the ratio per axis, the guard's growth time, frames without rows, bases, rebases, trips, presses). Proof: the residual under 1 deg holding still; every press engages (`engaged (ADS), a clean press`); the weapon follows the head with no drift down; the release line says `unwound with the game's turn: the view did not move`.
- **F68b, the sight against the fire line.** The reticle composite is key 10 (the aim reticle, tagged in place by the marker probe, F52); `fixDecide` already projects the marker's world position P through the eye's scene rows (`nW`), so key 10's `nW` and its depth are published per eye (`retNdcPublish`, seqlock) at the decision. `adsSightTick` reads the right eye's and, beside the sight at 10 m (F67b's `sightPointNdc`), logs the angle from the reticle to the sight (`nofoot::ndcAngles`: right, up, total, through `ndcRay`, the cross of the clip x and y planes through the NDC point). `ADS reticle (F68b)` per second: frames found of the frames with a sight NDC, both NDC means, the reticle's depth, the sight minus the reticle in degrees (mean, largest). `NOT found` names what to check (the marker probe off, the reticle hidden in ADS, the tag not found). Proof: the two agree within 0.3 deg and Sean's shots land on the sights; the correction is not built: it needs the same figure across weapons first.
- **F68c, G from one game frame.** The F67 log: `G inputs ... root N ms older than G` read 0 ms in 51 samples, 15-16 in 35, 32-47 in 2 (the ms clock ticks at 15.6 ms, so the split is coarse; the call order is the evidence). `nofoot::P1FramePairing` (under `g_eyeMutex`) gives each P1 call of the FP AMC (`fpRootTrack`) and of the local 3P AMC (`eyeProbeHooked`) a game-frame index: a call of a kind already seen in the frame opens the next frame. The 3P call stores its half (`g_p1ThreeP`: M3P, the body offset d it read, the index, QPC) and builds G when the FP root of the same index is in; the FP call builds it when the 3P half is; once per frame (`take`), and a pair whose calls are over 10 ms apart is refused (an index shifted by a missing call). `g_f66gP1Seq` is now the frame index (parity splits are unchanged in meaning). `ikPublishG` split into `ikPublishG` (the 3P half and the pin's input, unchanged) and `ikPublishPair`. The F67f 1 s limit on F_root in G is gone (the pair is one frame by construction); the consumer's armed window on G's age (1 s, `ikGUsable`) stays for stalls: a stalled game has a stalled pose, not a skewed one. `G pairing (F68c)` per second: game frames, G built from a pair, frames without a pair, pairs refused as far apart, how many frames the 3P call came first (the frames where the F67 rule used the previous frame's root), the 3P AMC's speed. Check in the rig: a body at 4 m/s at 90 Hz with the calls in alternating order, grip error under 0.1 mm with the pairing and 4.4 cm on every frame the 3P call came first with the F67 rule. Proof: no frame without a pair, no pair refused, while running armed; Sean: no arm stutter.
- **F68d, the arms follow the FP hands.** `nofoot::FpHandsUp`, stepped once per Animate-hook call from the FP snapshot's right attach (joint 51, FP model space): the unarmed rest pose is captured when Status.json says fists (unknown counts as fists) and the hand has been under 10 cm/s for 0.4 s (and follows a slow drift while near it); up when more than 12 cm from it (down under 9 cm); up takes the armed weight (`armedW` = max of the Status.json ramp and the detector's) and the armed paths (`armedNow`) from that frame, with no ramp; the weight falls over 0.3 s when the signal ends. Authority: Status.json's flag arms as before; hands up with fists for over 1.5 s COUNTED FROM THE HANDS GOING UP are not armed (a long unarmed spell does not veto the next draw); a vetoed pose held still for 3 s becomes the new unarmed rest. The pin's `armed` is the effective flag (`pinStage0` takes it). Instrument: the hook posts one record per call (attach distance, flags, the FP AMC's count of parts with an instance, P1 frame); the frame thread prints each state change (`hands (F68d)`) and, a second after each Status.json change of the held item (5 a minute at most), the two seconds around it, one line per frame with the first frame at or after the flip marked, and a summary: when the hands first went up, the armed signal first held, the part count first changed, against the flip. Proof: the hands first by 0.3-1 s; Sean: the arms appear with the weapon.
- **F68e, the neck target from the animated head.** `nofoot::pinNeckTargetAnim`: T = E - Ry(heading(eye) - heading(head 44)) . (P44 + R44 e_h - N0), e_h the eye point in the head joint's frame, captured as the mean of 45 consecutive standing frames (`PinEyeCal`: unarmed, not ADS, under 0.35 m/s, the head 1.5-1.9 m up and level) so that the animated neck-to-eye vector in the head's heading axes is (0, 0.155, 0.09) there; the joint's forward and up rows are found at the same time (the signed rows most aligned with the body's forward and up), so the head joint's frame convention is not assumed. The head's heading comes from its forward row, or its up row when the head looks within 20 deg of vertical; unreadable: no turn. Standing reproduces the F67c target exactly; a 40 deg chest pitch moves the neck 7.9 cm further back (16.9 cm ahead of the neck against 9.0, 6.1 cm up against 15.5); looking straight down puts it 15.5 cm behind and 9 cm above the eye. A vector more than 35 cm from the rest vector (a rigid 90 deg lean is 25 cm) is clamped. Until the calibration completes, or when the head is unreadable, the F67c target stands. The bend's target is the pin's T, so it follows. The pin's state split gains `crouching` (priority after jumping): the animated legs' reach (hip to foot over thigh + calf, both legs) under 0.80 (standing 0.94-1.0; the armed stance about 0.9; a crouch 0.6-0.7: the threshold is a guess, the line prints the mean per state). `body pin (F68e)` per state: the neck-to-eye vector used (right, up, forward, cm), how many calls were the animated one, the torso lean (hips to neck 34, forward positive), the neck-to-head lean, the animated legs' reach, the head height; once: the calibration (`e_h`, the axes found, the head height). Proof from the dump 133113: the crouch's forward offset exceeds the standing 9 cm by about 9 cm (the target moves back), and the crouch has its own line.
- **F68f, the eye height is gone.** Removed: `fix.native_on_foot_eye_height_cm`, the slider, `nofoot::eyeHeightClampCm`/`eyeHeightM`/`kEyeHeight*`, the ramp, the ADS fade, the `eye height (F64)` lines and their sums; the eye alignment is the view run plus the ADS locks. `nofoot::raiseSeated` stays (the F51 sign test uses it). `edvr.ini`: a hidden row at the last shipping value 4 (`# ui: hidden -- retired (F68): ignored.`); `retiredKeysNote` names a different live value once. The config contract is unchanged (the row is a comment-keyed retired row like F66's).
- **F68g, the armed-move trace (instrument).** The dump 133206 (armed, moving, hands within 0.6 cm of the grips): the arm joints jitter 0.28 deg at the cuff, 0.39 forearm, 0.66 elbow, 0.94 upper arm (max 1.8), mostly vertical, on a 5-6 frame period while the FP gun sways smoothly; the hand leads the gun by about one frame of the gun's velocity (F68c's pairing); the upper arm's swing fits the pin's vertical motion (+21..+25 cm a second, target error 7.2 cm against a 12.5 cm reach correction). `nofoot::ArmMoveGate`: armed and the AMC over 1 m/s starts a 40-call event, 5 a minute at most, a second apart. Per frame (`armed move (F68g) ev N +i`): the P1 frame and the game dt; the pin's vertical delta and horizontal d; the neck 34, both upper arms (as finally written), both grips (T_A) and the FP root relative to the eye in body axes (cm); G's age in frames (0 with the pairing); the reach correction. It decides whether the swing is the grip moving against the eye (the game's FP weapon sway), the shoulder moving (the pin's vertical or the reach rule), or timing. Posted by the hook, printed by the frame thread; absent when the pin is off.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test 324 checks, 0 failures (scratch build; the repo build is the commit's): the ADS map against a plant whose camera takes the aim (gain 0.85, 1 and 2 frames of delay, a head up to 30 deg/s for 60 s: the view within 0.5 deg of the head, no accumulation, the aim within 0.05 deg of the head's weighted turn), the 120 deg/s sweep (within 1.4 deg), the first frames at the fallback gain, the pitch clamp, the release (view within 0.8 deg), the guard on a camera that takes the aim (never trips), one that does not (trips 0.5 s after 8 deg, ratio -1), stale rows (never trips), a view cut (rebased), the player's own aim, the press gate (a re-press engages); the ray and angle through an NDC point; the pairing; the hands-up detector (rest capture at 0.4 s, no ramp at the onset, the hysteresis, the 1.5 s veto, a draw after a minute of fists, a sway of 5 cm, the recapture); the head-based neck target (standing within 1 mm, 40 deg lean 7.9 cm back, looking down, another frame convention, the 45-frame run, the clamp, the crouch state); the armed-move gate. Removed with F64: the four eye-height checks.

### F69 (2026-10-16; one build, one commit, `F69`; the F68 flight log `edvr_gfx_20261016_152023`; reviews\native-onfoot-body-review-2026-10-16.md section 7, plus h and i from Sean's two answers) - built, not flown

- **The measurements behind h and i (the dump measurer, F68 flight).** Dump 152258 (rifle, hip) confirms the review's cause: the reach correction was 22.9 cm and shoved the neck 13.5 cm forward, 10.8 right and 4.2 up of its F68e target, so the eye sat about 11 cm behind the chest front; the error scales with the correction (rifle ADS 13.3 cm, pistol 4.8, tools 0-1.6), which F69a-c target. ADS (rifle 152230, pistol 152353): the right eye is on the sight line (0.3 / 0.0 cm), but the sight sits 18-20 deg off the right eye's lens axis (yaw about -9, pitch +16..19), set at the press and constant afterwards; the FP root is 19.2 deg from the right eye's view, the hip crosshair 21.7 deg off axis and the tool aim dots 13.5-22 deg. Grips: F67f's hand-to-grip error is 0.00 everywhere, so the defects are target pose and orientation: the Energylink's left hand twist is 171.9 deg raw (clamped at +-90 on 82 of 82 calls) and the hand hovers off the handle; the Arc Cutter's left hand rings empty space; the Profile Analyser's left fist is crossed under the right arm; the pistol's right arm is the most distorted (plane change 100.5 deg, twist 72, flexion 19 -> 69) and its left hand is open, holding nothing. Sean's answers: the off hand on a one-handed item is "the game's animation" (h); an ADS press brings the aim onto the head's forward (i).
- **F69a, the neck target follows the camera's crouch.** `PinEyeCal` also keeps the mean of the cyclopean eye's model height over its 45 standing frames (h_stand; a non-finite eye height is no standing frame). `pinNeckTargetAnim`: v = lerp(rest, v_anim, w_c), w_c = `pinCrouchWeight` = smoothstep(0.20, 0.45, h_stand - E_y), the animated vector still clamped to 35 cm from the rest offset before the blend. Exogenous and memoryless; no calibration (or an unreadable head) gives the rest offset; a jump (E_y up) gives the rest. Log `body pin (F69a)` per state: w_c mean/max, calls at 0.5 or more, E_y - h_stand (cm: mean, min, max) or "no calibration yet"; the vector used is the F68e line's. Proof: rifle standing eye minus neck up 15 cm or more (w_c about 0); a crouch w_c about 1 with the forward part over 9 cm.
- **F69b, the armed square-up by the weapon's yaw.** `nofoot::BarrelAxes::step` (one call per hook call, before the pin): the 3P animated right attach 66 (the freeze's chain where it wrote, else the animated copy) and the FP right attach 51 through G; each rig's barrel axis is the signed row most aligned with G's forward (`barrelRow`, at least 0.7, as `pinFindHeadAxes` finds the head's); the same pick in both rigs for 10 consecutive armed calls latches it for the item (`g_heldItemSerial`, bumped by the frame thread at every change of the held item, and the end of arming, drop it). `nofoot::squareYaw(armed, barrels, uL, uR, fwd)` is the ONE function: armed (the written turn's own condition: armed, mode 3, writing) with both barrels known and each with a horizontal part of 0.25 or more, phi = ikYawFromTo(3P barrel xz, FP barrel xz); else the shoulders' (ikSquareToForward, bit for bit what it was). Called by the reach rule's U* (stage 0, `pinStage0`), the stage-1 angle (`sqPhi0`) and, through it, the written turn; the post-turn yaw-left figure for the weapon source rotates the barrel by the applied angle. `square-up (F69b)` per second while armed: reach-rule calls, calls with the weapon's yaw readable / used / latched, phi (weapon) and the shoulders' phi (mean, largest), the rows picked, the reach correction while the weapon's yaw was used. Proof: the rifle's phi within 10 deg, the reach correction under 5 cm (F68: 16.5 mean). Risk: the left elbow crossing the chest (F56's reason); watch `arm IK (F57)` swivel clamps (46% already).
- **F69c, the bend never undoes the reach rule.** In the pin's scope the bend's target is T + the reach correction (`pinCall.reach`); the logged target minus neck stays relative to the pin's T (the correction is taken back off the bend's residual, so the proof figures are comparable with F68). Proof: `spine bend (F58)` "before" under 1 cm armed, clamp hits 0; `body pin` target minus neck up about 0 (F68 rifle -3.4).
- **F69d, head clearance and the state bins.** `head clearance (F69d)` per state: the eye minus the neck 34, chest 24, clavicles 35 and 36 and the upper arms (the plan's U) as finally written (the eye's heading axes: right, up, forward; mean and min), the frames "inside" (the eye under the clavicle line + 8 cm and under 12 cm ahead of the chest 24; proof 0), and the pre-pin both-feet-over-12-cm count as a counter. Bins: `pinState(speed, vy, crouched)`: jumping from |vy| over 0.8 m/s ALONE (the review's wording kept both a vy-alone rule and a pre-pin feet test; the task text said vertical speed only, so the feet test is a counter in the clearance line, not a bin input), crouching from w_c of 0.5 or more (32.5 cm down); `pinCrouched`/`kPinCrouchReach` are gone (the legs' reach is still logged).
- **F69e, the reticle can be tagged.** `drawtag::seedZeros` turns an exact +-0 component into a signed 2^-100 m before `encode` in `f52TagInPlace`; the F53 census line gains "posts whose exact-zero components were seeded (F69e)". Proof: `key 10: N, N`; `ADS reticle (F68b)` found over 0. If it is still not found in ADS, the reticle is hidden there and the measurer's bolt impacts are the reference.
- **F69f, F68d's margins.** Onset window +-2 s (the dump waits 2 s, the ring is 1024 records), `kHandsVetoSec` 3.0 s. The veto and the recapture (a vetoed pose held still for 3 s becomes the rest) now fall on the same frame for a hand held perfectly still: either way the arms stand down at 3 s. Proof: the onset summary's lead is no longer at the window's edge.
- **F69g, the grip instrument (D1).** The hook posts per armed call, per side, Gamma_fp = A_fp x inv(H_fp) (FP 51/52 in 44/37), Gamma_3p = L_A, the FP attach's place, the 51-52 separation and (written solves only) the wrist after the solve: `wristAngles` splits the hand's rotation beyond the forearm that carried it into twist (about the forearm axis), flexion (about the elbow's hinge normal) and deviation (about eT x n1). The frame thread (`gripTick`) prints `grip (F69g)` once per item (the held-item serial) and side after 0.5 s of an FP attach under 10 cm/s: both grip frames (translation in cm, rotation axis and angle), the difference 3P x inv(FP), the wrist, the separation mean and sd over the window, and the off hand's decision; a 5 s "waiting" line says how far a side is. Identity difference for every item = a rig convention (the awkwardness is the wrist and the off hand); a difference per item = the 3P hand must sit on the FP hand's frame, fingers copied (F70 in the review's D2).
- **F69h, the off hand on one-handed items.** `nofoot::OffHand`, stepped once per hook call from the FP snapshot: two-handed when the FP left handAttach (52) is within 40 cm of the right (51) AND its orientation relative to 51 changed under 5 deg over the last 10 frames; the weight follows the decision over 0.15 s either way (from 0 at the draw; unarmed forgets everything). The left (off) arm's solve weight is scaled by it (a 0 weight writes nothing: the game's own 3P arm, carried by the torso), and its shoulder's radius in the reach rule grows by (1 - weight) x 1 m so a free hand binds no reach. Decided from the FP rig, not a name list. Overrides F67f's both-hands rule for one-handed items only. `off hand (F69h)` per second, per item: the 51-52 distance (mean, min, max), the orientation change, calls two-handed / free / ramping, flips, the weight; the `armed arms (F67f)` line counts a free hand as "the off hand is free", not a defect. F69g's line records the decision too.
- **F69i, ADS centres the sight on the press.** `AdsCentre` rides `AdsAimMap::step` (F68a's own path, so the in-flight account and the exact cancel keep the view the head's, and the carried yaw and pitch include the snap: the release unwinds it). At each engage (and an engage from the release) the frame thread measures once (`adsCentreMeasure`): the sight forward (the FP root's, which P14 follows) against the head's forward, the mean of the two eyes' forwards from their scene rows (not the right eye's lens axis, which the Pimax cants 10.75 deg), read in the cyclopean view's screen axes with `rotFrameDelta`'s convention (yaw + left, pitch + up: the guard's), and injects the difference linearly over 0.2 s (longer, at most 100 deg/s, for a swing over 20 deg). Bound: the weapon's yaw offset from the body stays within 40 - 3 deg (the game's cone; `adsCentreYawBounded`); no snap under 0.5 deg or over 90 deg; if the game's ADS flag falls during the snap it is stopped and logged with the cone offset. `ADS centre (F69i)`: at the start (the sight to head yaw and pitch, the right eye's lens axis from the head as the convention check, the weapon's offset, the injection and its duration), 0.25 s after the snap began (its end plus the game's look update; the spec said 0.2 s) with the view's largest movement against the head during it, and 1 s on. Proof: under 1 deg after the snap, the view under 0.3 deg during it (a 19 deg snap in 0.2 s is 96 deg/s and the cancel runs a frame behind: the rig's plant shows about 1 deg, so the flight decides whether the rate cap must come down).
