- **Rig.** native_frame_test 347 checks, 0 failures in the repo build (324 before): the crouch blend (the stance's head at the rest offset within 1 mm, 60 cm down the animated vector exactly, monotonic from a jump to 70 cm, a half at 32.5 cm, no calibration or a NaN eye height = rest); the bins and the clearance's inside test and axes; the barrel pick (permuted and flipped rows, an aim 54.7 deg from every row has none), the latch (10 calls, holds, drops on reset); `squareYaw` (a 38 deg blade gives phi 0 against -38 for the shoulders, U*'s left shoulder 11 cm further forward; 30 deg off the aim gives -30 and the turn lands the barrel on the aim; a barrel pointing up or none falls back; unarmed bit for bit); the bend with T + a 20 cm reach correction (want 0, no clamp, height unchanged) against T alone (pitch back at the armed limit, +2.7 cm); key 10's (0, 0, 5) takes a tag and decodes its sentinel; Status.json 2.0 s late arms with no gap, the veto at 3 s; known Gamma recovered from A x inv(H) and the difference within 0.1 deg and 0.1 mm, the wrist's flexion, deviation and twist, the 0.5 s steady window; the off hand (a rifle's pair two-handed at frame 10 and weight 1 in 0.15 s, the 55 cm pistol never, the weight falling in 0.15 s, a turning hand one-handed, unarmed forgets); the snap converging within 0.3 deg at 0.23 s with the view under 1.5 deg and then exact, the release returning the look base to zero, a 60 deg swing taking 0.6 s, an ADS ending during the snap stopping it, the cone bound and the error convention. Removed with the feet bin: the F67c/F68e checks of `pinState(.., feetHigh)` and `pinCrouched`.
- **Only a flight can verify:** the rifle's eye minus neck up 15 cm or more with the reach correction under 5 cm and phi within 10 deg (b and a together), no `inside` frame in any state, the crouch's w_c and forward part (never flown), the left arm's swivel clamps with the weapon-yaw square-up, `key 10: N, N` and the reticle found in ADS, the rifle two-handed and every pistol and tool free (h) with the grips then looking right, the five `grip (F69g)` lines and whether the difference is a rig convention, and the snap: under 1 deg after it, the view under 0.3 deg during it, the cone never dropping ADS, and a clean release.

### F70 (2026-10-16; one build, one commit `F70`, after F69; the F68 flight log `edvr_gfx_20261016_152023`, dumps 152230 rifle and 152353 pistol) - built, not flown

- **The RE findings (a read-only agent).** There is no sight, scope or eye joint in the exe (the names are povCamera, handAttach, head, foot) and no per-weapon ADS camera table. The ADS pose is the FP animation's, with joint 14 (def_c_povCamera_joint) as the camera, still computed in VR (the F63 snapshot of P14 is fresh every frame). In both dumps the line through P14 along the FP root's forward passes through the optics: the rifle's lens dot within 0.6 deg, the pistol's rear sight within 1 deg, so P14 is the animator's eye point. The F63 lock (`adsSightErrorEye`) removed only the perpendicular part, so the rifle eye sat 7.3 cm AHEAD of P14 and the pistol eye 5.7 cm BEHIND it (the F66e along figure). The sights are 33.3 cm (rifle lens ring) and 40.8 cm (pistol rear sight) ahead of P14; the flat game narrows the FOV to make them look bigger, which VR cannot do, so "closer" is a forward offset k along the sight forward f. Sean wants the headset at the proper eye point behind the iron sights or scope with the head detached from the body's neck: the headset translation locked there, head rotation still aiming (F68a plus F69i).
- **a, the target.** `adsSightErrorEyeK(p14w, eye, f, rows, k)`: the full error to P14w + k f (perpendicular AND along: eFullW / eFullBody / seatedFull; the perpendicular figures and the old outputs are unchanged), k = 0 is P14 itself. `kAdsSightClampM` 0.12 -> 0.40 (k up to 30 cm plus the rifle's 7 cm perpendicular and 7.3 cm along must fit); the growth guard still reads the perpendicular part only.
- **b, k is a user setting.** `fix.native_on_foot_ads_eye_distance_cm`, menu slider "ADS eye distance" on the Native on foot page, 0-30 cm in 1 cm steps, default 15, live (edvr.ini; the ini comment says what the user gets). At 15 the rifle eye sits about 18 cm behind the lens ring and the pistol's about 26 cm behind the rear sight. Clearance guard (`adsEyeDistanceCap`): k is cut so the eye stays 6 cm behind any readable FP weapon point that is ahead of P14 and within 6 cm of the sight line; the points read are the FP attaches (51, 52) and hands (37, 44) of the snapshot through the whole F_root (no weapon geometry is readable). Usually none lies on the line and the setting's own 30 cm bound stands; the 1 s line says which.
- **c, the ramp.** `adsEyeStep`: the target shift is w x (the applied shift + the error measured on the rendered eye), clamped, followed by a critically damped step (`cdStep`, at most 8 cm a frame); w is g_adsW (0.15 s in, 0.3 s out), 0 when not engaged or the guard tripped (the shift returns to zero); a frame with no measurement holds the shift at w x applied (the F63 integrator decayed on every such frame). Deviations: tau 0.05 s, not the 0.07 s asked (a critically damped step is at 78% after 0.2 s and 95% after 0.33 s at 0.07; 0.05 gives 90% at 0.25 s with the 0.15 s weight ramp in front: the rig's plant with a 2-frame-late measurement has 24% of a 23 cm shift left at 0.2 s, 1.5% at 0.35 s and no overshoot); the game's own ADS fraction (exe string 0x51EDED8) is NOT read, g_adsW is used.
- **d, the headset translation lock** (`g_adsLock`, open loop) is unchanged.
- **e, the head detached from the body.** `adsShiftPublish(S)`, S = g_adsLock + g_adsSight.applied (seated metres), every alignTick (a seqlock). The camera the game reports moves by (-S0, -S1, +S2) in body axes (F40's plant, the same map adsSightTick's `lockBody` uses). `ikPublishG` (P1) publishes the pin's input camera as the job camera less that, mapped through the AMC rows (`adsUnlockedCam`; the raw camera is kept beside it); the frame thread's body consumers take the same (`camUnlockedFrom`): the head follow's eye (F59b), the legacy body target and the legacy bend target. The body stays pinned to the unlocked head and does not slide forward with the ADS eye. adsSightTick and the other eye-geometry code keep the raw camera (the rendered eyes include S).
- **f, order with F69i.** The centre-on-press snap turns the weapon onto the head's forward first; the eye placement follows P14w + k f every frame, so it tracks the snap.
- **Proof lines.** `ADS eye point (F70)` per second while engaged: k asked and after the cap (and why); the eye to the target after the lock, along mean and largest (target 0 +-0.5 cm) and perpendicular mean and largest (max 0.5); the applied shift norm and the clamp; w; the pin input camera minus the job camera (world) beside -S through the AMC rows and their difference (the body is pinned to the unlocked head); the 3P root's world drift since the press (now, largest; a walk counts); the sight's NDC in the right eye ahead of the target eye point at 0.3, 0.5 and 10 m (equal when the eye is on the line; measured from the target eye point rather than from P14 so a k of 30 cm does not put a sample at the eye). `ADS eye distance (F70)` once per change of the setting.
- **Rig.** native_frame_test 355 checks, 0 failures in the repo build (347 before): the target with k (k = 0 reaches P14, k = 15 cm reaches 15 cm forward, the guard's inputs unchanged); the setting's bounds; the clearance cap (a point 20 cm ahead limits k to 14 cm, points off the line or behind P14 and none leave it, a point 3 cm ahead takes it to 0); the ramp (24% left at 0.2 s, 1.5% at 0.35 s, 0 at 1 s, no overshoot; on release the shift follows the weight back: under 3 cm at 0.5 s, 0 at 1 s); the 40 cm clamp, zero at weight 0, hold without a measurement; the pin input excluding S (a turned AMC); k = 0 and k = 15 cm closed loops landing within 1 mm. The F63 clamp check now reads 40 cm.
- **Only a flight can verify:** that the camera the game reports really moves by (-S0, -S1, +S2) with the sight lock's along part (F63 only ever moved it perpendicular to the sight; the pin-minus-job line and the root drift say), the eye on the line at k with the NDCs equal, the comfort of 15 cm (the setting is live), the pistol's cap (its rear sight 40.8 cm ahead), and that the body does not slide in ADS.

### 2026-10-23: F85 (pin-step trace, weapon UI pin, reticle depth)

Built from `reviews\native-onfoot-hands-ui-reticle-review-2026-10-23.md` in one build, in the review's order, from HEAD `d29cc29a`. Full build green: `native_frame_test` 520 checks (460 before; 60 new, in `tools\native_frame_test\f85_checks.h`, which a desk driver also runs), and a new rig `reticle_depth_test` (build.bat `:rig_reticle_depth`, 34 checks: the production bytecode on WARP against the CPU march and the analytic answer). Not installed, not flown. Environment the work depends on: SteamVR/OpenXR (headset key `aapvr`), 4032 x 4032 per eye with scene rows at 2016 x 2016, Elite 334925; F85c reads the scene depth through the depth probe, so it needs `fix.temporal_aa`.

**RVAs.** Checked against `docs\native-onfoot-334925-address-table.txt` before use: the AMC submit job 0x3DA4A00 (table row 64), the AMC writer's return +0x3DA4E62 (row 65), the record writer 0x4C86270 (row 79), the instance submit 0x434FF80 and the weapon UI submit callers +0x1A16DFD/+0x1A16E6D are the retargeted values the code carries. Nothing new is hooked: F85a rides the F54 record-submit pre-hook and the body-shift job hook, which now brackets the job with a thread-local.

**F85a.** Sites: the 3P P1 call (`ikPublishG`: M3P translation and up row, the camera the pin follows, the camera in the model), the FP P1 call (`fpRootTrack`: F_root), the 0x4C86270 call from +0x3DA4E62 (first record's pointer, stride and count are left in a thread-local; the job's end reads record 0 and the spread of the first 16 records and the AMC's +0x2E0 origin, after the body shift, for the local 3P AMC and for the FP AMC), the MGMC post-hook (the FP weapon's record), `pinRecord` (demand = target minus animated neck, applied, the cap flags, w_c, E_y - h_stand) and the reach publication (the hand error after the solve). Each posts into a 64-slot ring under a sequence count; the frame thread joins the frames older than the newest 3P frame by the P1 index. If the code never ran, the 1 s line says so: `pin step (F85a): 0 events (triggers seen: ...) ... sites this run: 3P P1 0, FP P1 0, submit jobs 0 ...`; a run that works prints non-zero site counts and the drawn record minus M3P by speed bin. What decides F86 (review 4.1): record minus M3P under 1 cm through an excursion means the game moves the body (raise the vertical cap, take w_c from the FP AMC); the difference equal to the step means our M3P is not the renderer's; growth with speed means a timing skew; under 0.5 cm everywhere means the cause is after the pose.

**F85b.** The submit side (`f85bTagSubmit`, called from `weaponUiObserveSubmit` before the original packs the instance) tags only space-1 parts that have a getter row of the same render frame, item, renderer, part and instance (`weaponUiGetterJoinMatches`), with keys 0x70 + the part's slot (a second post of a frame carries bit 7), and publishes U (the 16 floats the getter read) and P_ui = t R_U + p_U in a 64-cell ring found by the tag's sentinel. The render side (`f85bDecide`, from `fixDecide` when the CPU identification names a weapon key; such a draw takes no marker key slot and no hold, and the read-back skips it): K from `nofoot::f85::solveK` with the display affine of F66f (rejected when K is not a rotation to 0.01), the two eyes' K compared once both are in (the latch; a frame's pairing serves that frame and the next), rows rewritten as B.(K^-1 U') with o_hud and the rows' origin folded in, rows 4 and 5 and row 7 written to the work buffer before the whole-buffer copy (the F47 bind path). The proof line `weapon UI pin (F85b) part slot` carries, per part and eye: found / matched / decided / bound / refused with the reason, the unpatched anchor's error in px and degrees beside the K-to-U angle and the head's yaw and pitch from the aim, the corrected residual (0.00 px by construction when the arithmetic is right), K's origin against U's, and the eyes' agreement. The unpatched error should equal the K-to-U angle: if it does not, the rows' frame is not the getter's frame and the origin distance says so (refused past 5 m). Risks recorded for the flight: draws of more than one instance are corrected whole (counted); rows one frame old (counted as newest) make the readout lag the scene by that frame's head motion. Reading the log: finds in the pool above zero with no `part slot` line at all means the composite draws are not being named by the CPU identification (the stream shadow), not that the tag is lost; `refused` with `no both-eyes pair yet` or `the eyes' K disagree` points at the display affine; `K's origin far from U's` points at the rows' frame.

*Which identification, and the fallback.* The review leaves open whether the packer copies the instance's translation verbatim into the pool record (the marker nodes' does). The TAG path is tried first. If 120 parts have been published, 150 unmaps have run and 3 s have passed with no weapon-keyed find, the render side says `identification path TAG failed` and switches to the VALUE path: the translation is left as the game wrote it, published, and the pool is searched for a record whose position is bit-equal to it (hints first, a full scan only when a published value has no hit; same scan budget and back-off as the tag scan). If that finds nothing in the next window it says `VALUE failed` and stands the submit side down. Deviation from the review's wording: the fallback named there is the F83 writer join (the game's record writer at helper 0x434FDA9, object to record). That join exists only as `ObjectRecordWriterProbe`, armed for an eye dump, with a CONTEXT capture and an unwind per lookup of 0x3699C00; making it always-on is a new game-code hook and was not built blind. The value path uses the same identification of the draw's record (the stream entry, F63) and needs no hook; if the packer rewrites the translation, neither path finds it and the log says exactly that, which is the evidence for the writer join.

**F85c.** `reticle_depth_shader.h` (entry `main`, its own source, built into `kReticleDepthBytecode` by `tools\temporal_shader_build`; no entry-point collision, the source is passed as the variant's own). One thread, 32 geometric steps over 0.5-50 m, ten bisection steps on the first step behind the surface, the depth linearized from the rows' own z column (`depth = A + B/w`, infinity at the far plane), a first step already behind a surface (a surface under 0.5 m) is status 2 and the caller holds. The dispatch is at eye 0's key-10 composite draw (K solved from that draw, O and u its origin and forward), once a frame, the compute stage saved and restored; a 3-deep staging ring read back with DO_NOT_WAIT two frames later, the sequence number checked. On the CPU, D_f is a critically damped step in 1/D; P' = O + D_f u is formed in `fixDecide` only for key 10 and only when the game's P is within 0.2 degrees of the aim line K gives (else refused, counted). The `reticle depth (F85c)` line carries D_hit mean/min/max, hit/sky/near/off-screen counts, how often the game's 5 m was behind a surface, |D_f - D_hit|, the readback age, and every refusal; with no key 10 draw it says so every 5 s. Deviation: "tau 40 ms" and "90% within 0.1 s" cannot both hold for a critically damped step with time constant 40 ms (90% at 156 ms), so the smoothing time is 40 ms in SmoothDamp's sense (omega = 2 / 0.04, closed form, 90% at 78 ms, no overshoot, independent of the frame time); the rig checks the 0.1 s bound.

**Not changed.** The pin, w_c, the caps, F62c for keys other than 10, F68a/F69i ADS, F84's companion capture. No new key. Temporary keys in play: the existing `fix.native_on_foot_marker_fix` (and `_marker_probe`), which gate b and c. Nothing was written to `analysis\`.

### 2026-10-23: F86 (pose carry, trace, stream windows, weapon UI depth and size, prompt snap, vertical demand, dump counts)

Built from `reviews\native-onfoot-f86-review-2026-10-23.md` (the F85 flight, log `edvr_gfx_20261023_202639`) in one commit, parts a to h, from HEAD `112d1d2f`. No new key, no new hook: c, d and f ride `fix.native_on_foot_marker_fix`; a rides the body-shift job hook and the Animate post-hook. Full build green (`build\F86-full-build.log`, 240 s, receipt `d4f05e8b`): `native_frame_test` 598 checks (520 before; +78: the F85a tracker's checks changed by +4, 74 new in `run86` in `tools\native_frame_test\f85_checks.h`, which a desk driver also runs), `reticle_depth_test` 54 checks (34 before, unchanged and run first; +20 for the multi-line contract on WARP against `nofoot::f86::marchRangeCpu`). Not installed, not flown. Environment: SteamVR/OpenXR (`aapvr`), 90 Hz, 4032 output with scene rows at 2016, DLSS, Elite 334925. New pure header `src\d3d11\native_on_foot_f86.h`.

**a, pose carry.** The Animate post-hook (`armIkAfterAnimate`, after the optic capture) publishes `PoseIn` under a sequence count: G's game frame, M3P_p, F_p, the body shift d_p, each written hand joint (3P model) and the pin's eye. The FP P1 call posts F_i whole (`g_f86Fi`, by frame), the 3P P1 call posts M3P_i whole (`F85P1.m3p`). `bodyShiftHooked` gathers the local AMC's present records before the body shift (the bodyrec blocks, as `bodyShiftApply` finds them), runs the shift as before, then `f86CarryEnd` computes and writes: want = (a M3P_p + d_p) inv(F_p) F_i, drawn = a R(M3P_i) + the first record's translation, delta = want - drawn at a = the mean of the two written wrists, else the eye; every present record's position (+0x10) += delta. Refused and counted: no pose (also: no M3P_i or F_i for the frame, busy), pose over 3 frames, |delta| over 0.5 m, out of the pin's scope. The previous copy (+0x124) is classified against last frame's positions as the game left them (before the body shift): bit-equal to the game's own last position, then last frame's delta is added; bit-equal to the position we wrote, left alone; neither, left alone and counted. `pose_arm_drawn` (the wrists in world with and without the carry, the carry, the state, the pose age) joins the optic record by the pose's game frame. Lines: `pose carry (F86a) ev ...` after each frame of an F85a event, and the second's line always on foot (`submits 0, carried 0` if it never ran).
*Deviations.* (1) The body shift d rides both sides: want carries d_p and the drawn point uses the record's actual translation (M3P's translation plus d_i), not M3P_i's alone; identical at d = 0 (the rig checks a nonzero d). (2) A frame with no M3P_i or F_i, and a busy submit, count under `no pose`; a frame with no present record counts under `out of scope`; the second's line carries a suffix `also: no M3P_i or F_i for the frame, no body records, busy, write faults` after the specified text. (3) The wrist error by speed bin and `|carry|` are joined from the carry ring by the frame thread with the F85a tracker's speed (a missed slot drops a frame from them; the counts before them are exact). (4) Errors with no written wrist print -1.00. "After" is the carry's own prediction (the proof is `pose_arm_drawn`).

**b, the F85a trace.** `vy` is the up-step over dt; `cam-move` is no longer a trigger (its figure stays in the line) and `hand-lag` (the carry's error before the carry over 2 cm, the larger hand) takes its place; the cap is 8 events a minute per trigger and the second's line gives the frames each cap held back; the FP half's fields, rings and sites are gone and the log says so once.

**c, stream windows.** `planWindows` (pure, rig-checked): the entries a draw read last frame, per eye, sorted, clustered at a gap of 2048, one 4096-entry window about each cluster (end to end past 4094 entries), at most 4 per eye, even starts. The Unmap copies every window; the entries are the eye's draws of the newest two frames (the Unmap may land inside the newest one's draws); with none yet, one window about the newest entry as before. The hold logic is untouched. `marker fix (F86c): eye N: ...` once a second per eye. *Deviation:* in `last-frame entries %u clusters (min %u max %u), windows %u` the first number is the cluster count and min / max are the lowest and highest entry; `nearest window` is the smallest distance, in entries, of an outside draw to its own eye's windows.

**d, weapon UI depth.** `reticle_depth_shader.h` takes Dim.w = n lines, one thread each, line 0 from b0 as before, lines 1..31 from a second constant buffer (origin, direction, range), 8 values a line (status, distance, the hit pixel, the four neighbours' view depths). Dim.w = 0 is the old contract, answered unchanged (`reticle_depth_test`'s old checks pass, and run first). A part's eye 0 decision files five rays (the mesh's centre and four corners, else the anchor) as world points; the next dispatch marches them over 0.12-0.8 m, the nearest hit is smoothed in 1/D (40 ms, clamped 0.12-0.8 m), a miss holds the last value, and both eyes scale the correction by s = (D_s - 0.045) / D_panel about the aim camera's origin (M3' = s M3, m4' = s m4 - (1 - s) o_hud, which the rig shows equal to scaling U about the pivot; eye 0's image within 0.01 px and eye 1's disparity IPD f / 0.35 m for a plane at 0.395 m under a panel at 0.30 m). Key 10 keeps line 0 and owns the frame's dispatch while it is drawn; with no reticle (ADS) the first eye 0 consumer dispatches with line 0 unused. *Deviations.* The lines start at eye 0's origin from its scene rows (K's origin, which the latch holds to a millimetre of it, is the pivot of the scale); an item change resets a part's smoother; the F85b proof's target is scaled with the panel.

**e, weapon UI size.** Per part, eye 0, hip and ADS apart, the second's latest: the quad in K space (the decoded mesh's two largest extents x the record's scale), at the panel's distance, |U rows|, the texture bound at t0, the angular size. At an ADS press (the weight past 0.9 after under 0.1) the record words that differ from the last hip frame's are listed; the record is read whole (84 words, stream loads under SEH) at each weapon-key find.

**f, prompt snap.** Keys 1 and 45-50: eye 0's decision files the anchor P; the march over [|P| - 0.15, |P| + 0.01] from eye 0's origin gives the hit and, from the four neighbours, the surface normal; a facing surface (n . -u over 0.2) puts P' = P + n ((H - P) . n); the offset is cached per key (EMA 0.25 while P holds within a millimetre) and both eyes apply it in `fixDecide`. A hit with a grazing or untrustworthy normal, or no surface in reach, leaves P. The label instrument (`prompt labels (F86f)`): the untagged marker composite draws read back in an eye-frame in which a prompt key was patched, with whose b0 they drew with (our private copy or the game's) and their anchor's NDC (the record's position at the pool's next Unmap, through the draw's rows) against the nearest drawn key's patched NDC. The rig: a 34-degree plate at 0.94 m with P 5 cm behind its centre snaps to the centre within 0.1 mm (a narrow camera, 0.07 mm a pixel; a wide one gives the normal within 1 degree), a wall 2 m in front of a waypoint and an 80-degree plane leave P alone.

**g, vertical demand.** `vertical demand parts (F86g)` is appended to the F69a line of each bin: the headset's LOCAL height (and generation), the cyclopean eye over F_root and F_root's height in the 3P model, the animated neck's height, the aim pitch (F_root's forward against the model's up), and the floor under the origin from `floor probe (F86g)`: two rays from eye 0 toward the model points (+-0.3, 0, 0.7), a hit counted 0.4-1.2 m from the origin horizontally, the second's mean in cm. A separate `floor probe (F86g)` line gives the accepted hits, the rays without a surface and the hits outside the ring.

**h, dump counts.** `eye_*_decisions.json` frames carry `render_frame`, `marker_patched` and `marker_bound` per eye (the draws decided and bound at the issue, kept in a 256-frame ring by render frame), `marker_both_eyes_bound`, and the file `first_frame_both_eyes_bound`; null where the fix saw no draw. The log says `eye capture (F86h)` with the count.

**Not changed.** The pin caps, w_c, F85b's K, F85c's key 10 path, the ADS head aim. **For the flight beyond the plan:** the `also:` counters of the carry's line (an F_i that does not reach the submit shows as `no M3P_i or F_i`), `prev` not `neither`, the carry's cost in the frame (one gather of 216 records and as many writes), the dispatch every frame now (up to 32 lines), and the F63 line's Unmap copy time with up to four windows an eye.

### 2026-10-24: F87 (back-outs, standing-height hold, previous copy, stream history, scene rows fallback, prompt and label probes, hand residual)
Design: `reviews\native-onfoot-f87-review-2026-10-24.md` (parts h, p with appendix A, a, c, f and the two back-outs; appendix B, the label move, is F88 and is not in this build). Evidence read: the F86 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_023335`), the prompt-anchor RE. The RE is filed as `analysis\onfoot_prompt_anchor_334925.txt` (build 334925): the interaction prompt's point is the interactable model's `Root!Ui_interact` node, or the centre of the model's bounding box when the node is missing; the game hands it to the marker interface (vtable 0x520B2E8) with SetMarker (+0x50) from three call sites and UpdateMarker (+0x60), and the label's indicator with UpdateIndicator (+0x48), which carries no position. One commit; no new user key (the one hidden temporary key below is the pilot's).

**Back-outs.** F86d: `scale` is no longer assigned in `f85bDecide`; its two `scale != 1.0` guards skip, so the rows are bit-identical to F85's. `scaleCorrection` and `scaleUFrameAbout` stay (dormant, with their checks) for F88; the line `weapon UI depth (F86d, measure only)` carries D_s, the centre ray's own D, the panel's distance and the scale it would take, and keeps the ray counters after it. F86f: `isPromptKey`, `PromptCache`, `promptSnap`, `f86PromptApply`, the prompt rays and lines (`prompt snap`, `prompt labels (F86f)`), the `Untag` label fields and their `run86` checks are gone. `surfaceNormal` and `unprojectPixel` stay, because the multi-line shader still returns the neighbours (`reticle_depth_test` uses them).

**h, the hold.** `HeightHold` (`native_on_foot_f87.h`): dev = the render head's seated height + the eye alignment published for it (the hold, the ADS locks and the weapon view offset are all part of that space; the runtime lowers the view by what it moves the origin up) less the neck pivot's drop at the LOCAL pitch. The sustained part is a critically damped low pass (95% in the settle time, the rate capped at 4 cm/s); camera raise = - sustained, published as the eye alignment's y = - raise (the sign of `weaponViewOffsetSeated`). It starts at dev on entering the pin's scope, so a head already 9 cm down is up 9 cm within the 0.5 s ramp and does not drift for 3 s. The pin takes the camera less the transient (dev + raise) along the model's up, and its standing calibration waits until the transient has been under 1 cm for 1 s. `fix.native_on_foot_standing_height_settle_s` (hidden, live, TEMPORARY, default 3.0, clamped 0..10; 0 holds the neck hard) is read in the config tick and added to the shipped `edvr.ini` (the contract gate requires a documented default; no live `edvr.ini` was touched). Lines: `view height hold (F87h)` and `eye to weapon root (F87h)` (the scene's right eye less the FP root, in the AMC's right, up and forward) on foot every second, and in the runtime trace `head tracking (F87h, runtime)` (VIEW and LOCAL in the natural space, tracked counts, space-change events, the largest step of y).

**a.** The record's previous copy: classified at the submit's start (`with pos` when it equals the position bit for bit), and moved by the carry then; `lastRaw/lastFinal/lastDelta` are gone. `pose_arm_drawn` joins `g_f86Carry.get(gGameFrame)` (the submit of the pose's own frame; `g_f86Drawn` is removed). `kCarryMaxM` 1.5 m (the line says `over 1.5 m`). The floor probe tests the 12 affine entries (`affineFinite`). `hand residual (F87a)`: per hand |(target_i - w_{i-1}) R(M3P_i) - the carry written|, frame i's own Animate target against the wrist frame i-1's Animate wrote, by speed bin; the body shift d_p is not in it (zero in the pin's scope); the FP-frame bucket counts the newest FP post against the submit's frame.

**c.** `ClusterHist` keeps the entries each eye drew in the last 64 frames as intervals (a gap of 2048 starts another, 16 at most); the plan is F85's 8192-entry window about the newest entry, then the intervals newest first in windows of 4096 (at most 6, a wide interval in consecutive windows), then, while an escalation runs (8 frames after a draw outside every window), the whole stream in one copy. Copy cost: 256 KB an eye at most, the whole stream once. `marker fix (F87c)` prints windows (F85, history), the Unmap copies of the second that were escalated, named, outside and the nearest miss; the eye-1 ring is eye 1's first and last stream entry of the frame before (eye 0's line prints `-`).

**f.** `worldDraw` keeps the depth prepass's rows (`kFbVs`/`kFbPs`, CB shadow only) once per eye-frame; the first marker composite draw of an eye-frame that has no scene rows adopts them (`g_cpuWorld`, the published eye rows, the eye's scene size); where both exist `rowsMaxDiff` over the 16 rows and the origin row is compared and one value over 1e-5 latches the fallback off for the session (said once).

**p.** All gated on native stereo on foot and `fix.native_on_foot_marker_fix`. Install (`f87p::tick`): identity, all 15 pins, the three vtable slots (each must hold the game's function), the three getter calls' targets, then the page of stubs (`emitTrampoline`, `emitGetterStub`: the bytes are in the pure header and the rig runs them), the three rel32 swaps and the three slot writes, rolled back on a failed write, restored when the state ends. `prompt anchor (F87p) 1 s` counts the calls by role (the return address), the focus key as the game's focus marker call passes it each frame (the hidden call clears it), its point and the range of its points; `prompt getter (F87p)` once per entity and site: the point before and after the game's getter, the branch (node, box, no model component) from the game's own sequence (entity reference, component table, FindNode "Root!Ui_interact", bounds, world matrix; under SEH), the local box and the front-face point P' = P + n ((C + h n - P) . n) with the face latched at first sight. `camera path (F87p)`: the standard deviation, over the second, of eye 0's pitch in the scene rows minus the pitch of the render head the runtime handed the game (both over the character's up; the aim's pitch is a common offset), and of eye 0's height against the job camera's.

**Appendix A, the label.** `labelIdentify` (at `fixDecide`'s untagged return): VS B10B032BDFD46700 with PS DB899F4BD577F2E5, kind X, one instance, a CPU-named record; the record is read, whole, at the pool's next Unmap (`labRecRead`), so a draw is counted once its record is known to be camera-local (0, 0, 0.01); per draw the cell keeps the eye, order, index count, start, base, start instance, the record's position, scale, quaternion words and +0x50, the viewport as issued, and at the issue (`nativeOnFootLabelIssue`, called from vscreen.cpp right before the game's own draw) whether the layer bracket began, the viewport as mapped, the b0 bound (the game's, `g_privCb`, other) and its rows 4/5/7 (the game's from its shadow, ours as last written). Once a second per eye the small draw's index and vertex ranges are copied (the icon capture's way, a 28 KB vertex window) and decoded; the quad clusters are grouped by shared vertices and the smallest is the leader's dot; its centre through the bound rows and the record is `leader NDC`. `prompt label draws (F87p) eye %d 1 s` joins that to the focus key's patched target and the game's own NDC for the same eye-frame, counting only eye-frames in which the focused interactable's key was matched to a marker composite draw. `label_draws` is in each frame of `eye_*_decisions.json` (null where the instrument saw none). Extra diagnostic lines (not in the review): one `mesh decoded` line per eye and at most three `mesh was NOT decoded` lines.

**Deviations and readings.** (1) `seat_meter` in the hold line is the render head's height plus the published eye alignment (the runtime's meter, recomputed); compare it with the runtime trace's `seat_meter,...height_cm`. (2) `camera path`: the job camera has no orientation in what the d3d11 half reads, so the pitch is the scene's against the runtime's render head, as above. (3) The `prompt getter` line has no `node (...)` field (the amended review's format); the node's world point is computed and not printed. (4) `weapon UI depth (F86d, measure only)` keeps `rays: hits ..., misses ..., held ..., no depth view ..., no hit yet ...` after the specified text. (5) `marker fix (F87c)`: `escalated` is the second's count of whole-stream Unmap copies. (6) The record cache means the first sight of a label record is `unverified` and not counted; the label's records are expected to keep their pool slot. (7) `head tracking (F87h, runtime)` samples VIEW at every WaitGetPoses and LOCAL ten times a second, once the native frame provider is acquired; LOCAL in STAGE reads zeros when the natural space is LOCAL. (8) The hold's raise is quantised before the transient is formed, so the pin's eye and the view agree to the millimetre.

**Rig (`run87`, `f85_checks.h`).** native_frame_test 598 -> 639 checks: run86 loses F86c's 11 stream-window checks and F86f's 8 (snap, cache, wall, grazing, key list) and its prev-copy checks go from 7 to 5; the new run87 adds 62: the hold including the 10 cm slump, the -60 deg nod, the 0.3 s bob, the crouch, ADS, the ramp and the millimetre steps, the stillness gate, the previous copy, the join by submit frame, 1.2 m carried and 1.6 m refused, the affine test, the hand residual, the history windows with three cycling layouts, the escalation, the fallback guard, the 15 pins and the three getter targets, the roles, the box geometry, the label census, and the emitted trampoline and getter stub run on stand-in game functions: arguments in registers and on the stack, the return value, xmm0-3, 16-byte alignment). The stub rig found an off-by-8 in the getter stub's alignment before any flight; fixed. reticle_depth_test 54 -> 54 (unchanged, the multi-line shader is kept). The 15 pins were read against the 334925 executable with capstone before the build (the vtable entries, the three entry prologues, the call sites, the getter calls and the four model pins at 0x191F190, 0x191F1C7, 0x191F382, 0x191F3A3; the type id 0x5F05D38 and the node name string at 0x51F3500).

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock`: green, receipt `5f8163673c58...` over the uncommitted tree, 189.8 s (test rigs 153.7 s, d3d11.dll 12.3 s); d3d11.dll 29,731,328 bytes, openvr_api.dll 833,536, edvr-installer.exe 170,470,912. Not installed, not flown, `--dll-only` not run (a branch build). **For the flight beyond the review's plan:** `prompt anchor (F87p)` must read SetMarker above 0 (else the slots did not hook and every label figure is blank), `prompt getter` must follow once per entity (else the stubs never ran), `groups 0` while the focus key is above 0 means the label identification failed, the hold's `state` should read `holding` (not `no pose`), and the first seconds of the F87c line show whether the history needs its first cycle.

### 2026-10-24: F88 (the weapon eye offset: live eye offsets relative to the held weapon)
From the F87 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_050524`): the pilot judged only the rifle pose. At the hip the right eye less F_root (`eye to weapon root (F87h)`, the character's right, up and forward) read +3.3, -1.0, -3.9 cm by default and +2.6, -1.8, +7.6 cm in the pose he set by hand, and he raised his head about 2.5 cm, which the hold cancelled. He says the view is too far back from the weapon and has to be higher. The game places the first-person weapon relative to the character and not the headset, so the eye moves against the weapon only through the eye alignment; F88 makes that a setting he can tune in the headset. The other F87 probes (prompt anchor, label, hand residual, stream windows) are not read yet. The label move (appendix B of the F87 review, once called F88) is not in this build.

**The keys** (temporary: they go when the pilot picks values, the winning offset becomes the behaviour and the keys are proposed for removal, quoted). `fix.native_on_foot_weapon_eye_forward_cm` (-10..25), `_up_cm` (-10..15), `_right_cm` (-10..10), step 0.5, default 0, and `_body` (`follow` | `stay`, default `follow`), read live in `readKey`'s config tick with a line on each change (`native on foot weapon eye offset (F88, TEMPORARY)`): clamped to the range, a value that is not finite reads 0, any other body value reads `follow` and the line says so. Menu rows "Weapon eye forward", "Weapon eye up", "Weapon eye right" and "Body follows weapon eye offset" on the Native on foot page, right after "Weapon view offset" (file order in `edvr.ini`, where each has its own comment block and the contract gate's row). They add to F75's Weapon view offset (default right 2.5 cm), which is untouched: the readout's +3.3 right already contains it.

**The offset.** `native_on_foot_f88.h` (pure, shared with the rig). O is in the character's axes, so it turns with the body's yaw and not with the head. It rides the eye alignment's seated translation through the plant the ADS lock already uses (F40, psi 0): the camera moves by (-S0, -S1, +S2) in the AMC's right, up and forward for a seated shift S, so the seated shift for a camera move O is (-O0, -O1, +O2). `alignTick` adds w O to each axis after the F87 sum (the hold's term included), so with every key 0 or w 0 the addend is an exact positive zero and the sum is F87's bit for bit.

**The weight.** `f88::Weight`, linear, 0.25 s from 0 to 1 and back (22 ticks at 90 Hz are short of 1, 23 reach it). Target: the pin's scope and a weapon or tool drawn (`g_armed`, the F66a flag: Status.json SelectedWeapon and the hands' state, not its 0.3 s blend) and not ADS (the journal's flag or the resolved ADS state, the pair the ADS lock reads); native on foot must be active. So 0 unarmed, 0 in ADS and in the cockpit, and a draw, a holster, ADS in and ADS out are each a 0.25 s ramp with no step larger than a tick of it.

**The body.** The pin takes the camera less the ADS shift S (`adsUnlockedCam`); F88 reuses that: the share of w O the pin ignores is added to S, which `alignTick` publishes with `adsShiftPublish` (and the ADS eye point line's expected shift includes it, so its difference column stays a check). `stay`: all of w O, so the body stays where it would be and only the view moves. `follow`: the up part only, so the body moves with the eye forward, back and sideways, as when the pilot leans, and never stands taller than its standing height; the up offset always moves only the view.

**The hold.** The hold's input is the head's posture: the render head plus the eye alignment published for it (`devOf`), and w O is in both, so it cancels. The runtime applies a published value a frame or two later (`eyePoll` at the next WaitGetPoses), so while O moves the render head misses the change for that long and dev would read the difference as the head moving (the rig's control, without the wait, moves the hold's sustained part by more than a millimetre from one draw at up 10 cm and two ticks of lag). The hold therefore waits (`HoldIn.frozen`, as in ADS) while the published up of w O is changing and 0.3 s after (`f88::Settle`); it is never true while that up is 0, so with no up offset the hold is F87's. The hold's state reads `frozen (weapon eye offset moving)` then. The hold's output enters the sum before w O does and w O never enters the hold.

**Lines.** `weapon eye offset (F88) 1 s: keys right %+.1f up %+.1f forward %+.1f cm, body %s, w %.2f (armed %d ADS %d), applied seated (%+.2f %+.2f %+.2f) cm, pin ignores (%+.1f %+.1f %+.1f) cm`, once a second on foot while any of the three offsets is non-zero or w is above 0 (so a set key shows `w 0.00` unarmed: the code ran and nothing was drawn). "Applied seated" is w O's seated image added to the eye alignment, in cm; "pin ignores" is the part added to S, the same convention (seated, cm): `stay` equals applied seated, `follow` has only the middle number. `eye to weapon root (F87h)` then moves by w O in the character's axes.

**Rig (`run88`, `f85_checks.h`).** native_frame_test 639 -> 653 checks (14 new, none lost); hotkey_capture_test 657 -> 657 (its Native on foot page row count 33 -> 37); reticle_depth_test 54 -> 54. The new checks: the key ranges, NaN and the body key (2); the defaults and a weight of 0 are exact positive zeros and the sum is F87's bit for bit (1); the weight's target table, its 0.25 s ramp and the draw / ADS in / ADS out / holster sequence (3); O in the character's axes for five yaws, its heading turning with the yaw, 10 cm forward at 90 degrees along world x (1); the hold's input equal to the head's posture for up offsets of 0, 3, -10 and 15 cm at three weights (1); the hold waiting for every tick on which two ticks of runtime lag leave a difference, its sustained part unmoved, and the control without the wait moving by more than a millimetre (1); no wait with no up offset (1); `stay` leaves the pin's demand unchanged with an ADS shift beside it, for five yaws and two weights (1); `follow` moves it by w x (4, 0, 10) cm in the body's axes and an up offset alone does not move it (1); the F87h readout from (+3.3, -1.0, -3.9) moving by 0.6 x (4, 3, 10) cm at five yaws (1); the seated shift through a draw, ADS in, ADS out and a holster at (-10, 15, 25) cm stepping by at most 1.2 cm a tick, at (+10, -15, +25) cm drawn and exactly 0 in ADS and holstered (1).

**Deviations from the specification.** (1) The hold's wait (above) is an addition: O must never feed the hold, and the cancellation in `devOf` is exact only when the render head and the published value agree. (2) The ADS flag of the weight is the journal's OR the resolved ADS state (`g_adsState == kAdsActive`), not the journal alone, so a press by the bound action takes w down with the ADS lock. (3) Slider steps are not ramped (0.5 cm each); only w is. (4) The seated-to-character map is the ADS lock's psi 0, so O's direction is off by the carried look yaw only while an ADS release is still unwinding it (0.3 s, w ramping up). (5) The F87 bullet in the Status block now carries the flight's verdict and points to the F87 journal for its build contents.

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock`: green, receipt `959bbc8b5d8d...` over the uncommitted tree, 163.7 s (test rigs 138.6 s); d3d11.dll 29,737,984 bytes, openvr_api.dll 833,536, edvr-installer.exe 170,482,688. The first pass failed one new check (the yaw check's 1e-9 tolerance against the hook's float rows, loosened to 1e-7) and the second the hotkey rig's page row count (33 -> 37, edited with the four rows); no gate was bypassed. Not installed, not flown, `--dll-only` not run (a branch build). **For the flight:** set `forward` first (about +11 cm), read `eye to weapon root (F87h)` at the hip against the starting numbers above, then `up`; watch `w` in the 1 s line (0 unarmed and in ADS, 1 drawn), `pin ignores` against the body setting, and the hold's `state` (`holding`, with a short `frozen (weapon eye offset moving)` after a draw if `up` is set).

### 2026-10-24: F89 (the ADS release, the left eye closed in ADS, the hold's open loop)
From the F88 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_054818`): the pilot set forward 5.0, up 6.0, right 0.5 cm, body follow and a standing height settle of 0.1 s, and reported two things. Releasing ADS "goes back to the non-offset position and then returns to the offset after a pause". And with the settle at 0 (a hard lock) the body and view "freak out"; at 0.1 s it is better. He asked for one addition: in ADS, close the left eye. F89 does all three and makes his values the built-in defaults of the five temporary keys (`weapon_eye_forward_cm` 5.0, `_up_cm` 6.0, `_right_cm` 0.5, `_body` follow, `standing_height_settle_s` 0.1: code defaults and the shipped `edvr.ini` rows; the keys stay temporary and stay in the menu).

**The release: what the ADS lock does with O.** The F70 sight lock (`adsSightTick`, `nofoot::adsEyeStep`) is absolute. On an active tick with a valid right-eye measurement its target is its own position plus the error between the rendered right eye (solved from the eye's scene rows, so everything in the eye alignment's sum is in it, O included, as the F74 weapon view offset already is) and P14w + k f; one critically damped filter (tau 0.05 s) follows it. On release the target is zero and the filter runs down. So with O in the sum the lock finds a shift that is the sight's less O's, and the eye lands on P14w + k f with or without O. The pin takes the camera less S, the lock's shift plus the share of w O it ignores (`f88::Out::ignore`), so with the lock's shift the sight needs and O both in the camera, the pin's demand is what it is at the hip. F88 had w at 0 whenever the journal's flag or the resolved ADS state (the bound action) said ADS. On release the state falls first and the lock runs down to the un-offset eye at once; the journal's flag lags the bound action's release and kept w at 0 until it cleared, and then w ramped up over 0.25 s: the dip, the pause and the return the pilot described. The fix is at the root: `f88::weightOn(pin, armed)` has no ADS term (the 1 s line still prints ADS). Nothing else in the lock changes; its shift stays inside the 40 cm clamp with O in the sum (the rig checks it).

**Rig: the ADS eye and the release path** (`run89`, `tools\native_frame_test\f89_checks.h`). On the production functions (`adsSightErrorEyeK`, `adsEyeStep`, `adsCamShiftWorld`) with the measurement one and two ticks late, at four yaws: with O applied (the pilot's values, w 1) the eye lands on P14w + k f within 1 mm and exactly where it lands without O. The release goes from the sight to the offset eye with its distance to the offset eye never growing, arrives within 1 mm, never comes nearer to the un-offset eye than the offset eye itself (7.8 cm) and never pauses (no 10 ticks of under 0.3 mm a tick while more than 2 cm away). The control is F88's gate (w 0 in the sights and until a journal flag that clears 0.6 s after the release, then a 0.25 s ramp): the eye comes within 1.5 cm of the un-offset eye and stops for more than 10 ticks, so the checks have teeth. The pin's demand in the sights equals the hip's for stay and follow, five yaws.

**The trace.** `ads transition (F89) press|release frame %u: t %+.3f s, journal ADS %d, ads state %s, lock weight %.2f, offset w %.2f, eye - F_root right %+.1f up %+.1f forward %+.1f cm, eye step %.2f cm`: one line per frame for 1.5 s after each edge of the resolved ADS state (`g_adsState == kAdsActive`: the bound action, else the journal), at most 4 events in any minute (the rest are counted; a note says so at most every 5 s). The edge frame is t +0.000. The journal flag, the state and w are separate fields, so a flight with the old gating would show w 0 against a journal flag still 1 and a state of `releasing`. The eye is the right eye less F_root in the character's axes (the `eye to weapon root (F87h)` figure) and the step is its motion since the last new eye rows (a frame without a sample prints zeros and says so). In the new build the step should show the sight lock running down without a dip or a stall and arriving at the offset eye, with `offset w` at 1.00 throughout.

**Close left eye.** User key `fix.native_on_foot_ads_close_left_eye` (`on` | `off`, default `on`, live), menu toggle "ADS: close left eye" on the Native on foot page after "ADS eye distance" (file order in `edvr.ini`; the hotkey rig's row count for the page is 38, was 37). The level is `f89::darken(key, onFoot, armed, g_adsW)`: the ADS lock's weight (the head-aim ramp: 0.15 s in, 0.3 s out), exact 0 outside ADS and with the key off; a weight above 0 with no weapon drawn or off foot is the guard: it forces 0 and logs `ads close left eye (F89): GUARD TRIPPED`. **Where the eye images are made.** The d3d11 half composites the UI layer and the menu into the game's eye images before the game submits them; the runtime then blits each captured image into the swapchain image of an OpenXR projection layer. It submits one projection layer and no other (there is no quad layer in `src\openxr`), so there are no separate UI layers to hide. The last point that covers everything the left eye shows is `D3D11Stereo::renderCaptured`, after the comfort fade and the plugins' overlay, and that is where the pass is. **How the level gets there.** `left_eye_darken.h` (the comfort fade's packing and staleness rule: a level not republished within 0.2 s decays to 0 over 0.1 s, NaN and negative read 0, above 1 reads 1) carries it from the frame thread to the provider's `beginFrame`, which writes the float's bits into the retired `reservedProjectionChannel` slot of a version 10 answer (the same move F62b made with `reservedYaw`; no new version, no change in size). The runtime reads the slot for a version 10 answer only (the ladder zeroes it otherwise), and `compose` passes it to `renderCaptured(..., leftEyeDarken)` for a fresh pair and a replayed one alike, as an argument of each draw. Below 1 the pass rebinds the whole state (plugins may have changed any), blends black over the left image with the comfort fade's blend state and the level as the factor (out = in x (1 - level); in an sRGB swapchain the blend runs in linear light), over the whole image so a trimmed placement's black border stays black; at 1 or more it clears the image to black. Eye 1 never reads it. A pair of DLLs from different builds darkens nothing. **Lines.** `ads close left eye (F89): closed|open (key %s, lock weight %.2f)` on each change; `ads close left eye (F89) 1 s: darken %.2f ...` once a second while closed, naming the path; the runtime's trace has `left_eye_darken,level=...` at each edge and once a second while darkened; the key reads with a line on each change (`native on foot ADS close left eye (F89)`).

**Rig.** `openxr_stereo_test` (WARP, `leftDarkenSelfTest`, deferred and owned contexts, sRGB and UNORM swapchains): level 0, -0, negative and NaN are today's image bit for bit in both eyes; 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75 darken the left eye by that factor and leave the right eye's pixels equal to the baseline's; 1 and above (to infinity) make the left eye exactly 0xff000000 at every sampled pixel with the right eye untouched; with the comfort fade at 0.5 and the level at 0.5 the left eye is x 0.25 and the right x 0.5, and a fade of 1 is black in both eyes; a trimmed placement keeps its black border and darkens the placed image on the left only; a render after a darkened one is plain again. `native_frame_test`: the level follows the lock weight, is exact 0 with the key off and in ADS's absence, the guard forces 0 and reports a trip when unarmed or off foot, a weight that is not a number reads 0, the real ramp rises to 1 in 0.15 s and falls to 0 in 0.3 s monotone, the key off in the sights opens the eye at once; the signal reads 0 until published, decays when stale, and the wire form round-trips; the provider's version 10 answer carries the level and a version 9 answer does not. `openxr_native_test`: the ladder believes the slot only from a version 10 answer.

**The hold at settle 0.** Hypothesis from the pilot: the hold's input contains its own output, applied late. Checked in the code. `f87HoldTick` measured dev = `np.render[7]` (the render head of the last frame the runtime handed over) + `g_eyeAlignPub[1]` (the alignment published last tick) - the pivot's drop. The render head carries the alignment `eyePoll` applied at the start of that frame's WaitGetPoses, which is what the provider handed over at the previous frame's `beginFrame`: a tick or more older than the one subtracted. With d ticks of extra delay dev = h + A(i-1) - A(i-1-d), where A is the published y. At settle 0 the hold's output is dev (a quantised millimetre), so A(i) = h + A(i-1) - A(i-1-d): d = 0 settles in one step; d = 1 swings between 0 and -14 cm for a 7 cm slump (a period of six ticks, 15 Hz at 90 Hz) and never settles; d = 2 grows to the +-50 cm clamp. At 0.1 s the low pass damps the loop (roughly, its damping falls by 0.53 d of 2 at 90 Hz) and it rings at d = 2 instead of running away, which is "better at 0.1". The rig reproduces all three (the control in `run89`: the last published alignment taken out, d = 1 and 2: the output moves by more than 5 mm in the last second; d = 0 settles). No flight has measured d yet; the new instrument does.

**The fix: match by serial.** The runtime keeps `eyeAppliedSerial`, the serial of the alignment `eyeApplied` is (0 after a new origin, the wanted serial once `eyePoll` has applied it, unchanged when an apply is refused or put off) and says it with each pose: the last word of `EdvrNativeFrameInput` version 3 (was `localReserved`, always 0), serial + 1, 0 meaning not reported. The provider keeps it with the frame (`nativeFrameNowPose().eyeAlignApplied`). The frame thread keeps the y of every alignment it publishes under its serial (`AlignHistory`, 256 slots; `f89AlignPublish` replaces the two `nativeFrameSetEyeAlign` calls) and the hold's input (`HoldFeed`) takes out of the pose the y of the serial the pose carried, not the one published last. The input is then the head's posture alone and the loop is open at any delay. A runtime that does not report (older than F89) gets the last published alignment, F88's measurement; a reported serial the history no longer has makes the filter wait on its last good input. **F88's wait.** The rig proves it unnecessary when the serial is reported: with O's up ramping over a weapon draw and the runtime two ticks late, the hold's sustained part is, tick for tick, the one it has with no O at all (difference under 1e-12), with no wait. It stays for the unreported case only (there the rig still shows it holding the part unmoved, and the control without it moving by more than a millimetre). **Rig:** settle 0 with the runtime applying 0, 1 and 2 ticks late publishes a 7 cm slump in full on the first tick that sees it and stays within half a millimetre of it, no overshoot or ringing; settle 0.1 gives the filter on the posture alone to 1e-9 at the same three delays, and for a 2 mm step (under the 4 cm/s cap) 1 - (1 + wt) exp(-wt) of the step at every tick (the 7 cm slump is the rate-limited response, equal to the undelayed reference); the history finds a serial, reads serial 0 as the zero alignment and 0 as not reported, and misses an overwritten one; the feed's three sources; the second's counters. **The line.** The `view height hold (F87h) 1 s` line now ends with `applied align (F89): serial lag mean %.1f max %u frames (publications behind the newest), matched %u of %u (serial not in the history %u, runtime does not report %u), residual of the input after taking the applied align out %.2f mm (taking the last published out instead: %.2f mm; 0 = the input is the head's posture alone)`. The residual is the range over the second of (render head + alignment - LOCAL head height): the seated origin's height when the input is posture alone, so its range is 0 when the right alignment is out and the size of the hold's own motion when the wrong one is. The second figure is the old input, so one flight shows both.

**Deviations from the request.** (1) The release trace's `lock weight` is the head-aim ramp `g_adsW`, which is also the darkening's weight; the sight lock has no weight of its own. (2) The darkening is a separate pass in `renderCaptured` and not in the d3d11 half because the runtime's blit is the last point that covers the left eye's whole content, and it is where the comfort fade already lives; it travels in a retired slot of the version 10 answer instead of a new ladder step. (3) The applied-serial word reuses the version 3 input's reserved word (no new input version), so a pair from different builds falls back to F88's measurement and F88's wait. (4) F88's wait is kept for that fallback instead of removed. (5) `kSettleDefaultS` is 0.1, so the three `run87` cases that depended on the slow default (the slump, the bob, the ADS freeze) now set 3 s explicitly. (6) The `hotkey_capture_test` row count and `tools\slow_regime_test\mutants.py` (the compose line it anchors on) change with the code.

**Rig counts.** native_frame_test 653 -> 683 (30 new: the built-in values (2), w through the press and release (1), the ADS eye with O at two measurement delays (2), the release path, its dip and pause, and the control (3), the pin in the sights (2), the hold's input in the sights (1), settle 0 open loop and its control (2), settle 0.1 (2), O's up with no wait (1), the history, the feed, the second and the generation reset (4), the darkening: level, guard and ramp (3), the signal (2), the trace and the eye step (3), and the provider's slot and the applied word (2); the run88 checks that gated on ADS were rewritten in place, none lost); hotkey_capture_test 657 -> 657 (the Native on foot page has 38 rows, was 37); reticle_depth_test 54 -> 54; openxr_native_test 5278 -> 5281; openxr_stereo_test 24431 -> 33479 (the new WARP pass; every pixel read counts as a check).

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock`: green on the second pass (the first failed one new check, the provider rig reading sequence numbers out of order, fixed in the rig), receipt `38ac9d1ee079...` over the uncommitted tree, 179.5 s (test rigs 143.9 s); d3d11.dll 29,748,736 bytes, openvr_api.dll 835,072, edvr-installer.exe 170,497,024. Not installed, not flown, `--dll-only` not run (a branch build). **For the flight:** read `weapon eye offset (F88) 1 s` for w 1.00 in ADS (armed 1, ADS 1), then the trace at each press and release (offset w 1.00 on every line, eye step smooth, `ads state` going `engaged` -> `releasing` -> `idle` while the journal flag clears later); `ads close left eye (F89): closed` on the press and `open` after the release; at settle 0 the F87h line should read `matched` for every frame with a residual near 0 and the old figure beside it showing the size of the loop; both eyes open outside ADS.

### 2026-10-24: F90 (the rifle's ADS readout at the hip's size, the pistol's readout in the weapon's frame, the pilot's F89 values)
From the F89 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_064508`, Epic, build 30cc64e6, Pimax OpenXR `pimax-openxr/pimax-crystal-super`, 4032 x 4034 output, 2016 x 2017 render, DLSS): the ADS release is clean and the pilot likes the left eye closing. His values: weapon eye forward 9.0, up 7.5, right 0.0 cm, weapon view offset 2.0 cm. Two things remain: the rifle's ADS readout still shrinks, and the pistol's readout sits over the rear sight. The review (`reviews\native-onfoot-f90-review-2026-10-24.md`) read the eye dumps and found both; F90 builds its sections 2 to 5.

**A: the rifle's ADS readout.** Every weapon UI part samples one 3525 x 3133 texture, redrawn every frame, through its own UV rectangle (b2 row 6). At the hip the readout's content is in part 29501's rectangle, in ADS in part 29500's, at one texel size; 29500's quad maps 16/9 as many texels per cm, so the ADS digits were 9/16 = 0.5625 of the hip's size (F85 measured 0.55-0.57). F90 scales 29500 by texels/m(29500) / texels/m(29501) about its content centre: the digits get the hip's world size at the game's own ADS place. 29498 (the pistol's ADS part, inferred from the rifle's ADS texture; there is no pistol ADS dump) gets its own ratio, 4/7. `native_on_foot_f90.h` holds the table (name, mesh extents, record scale, |b2 row 6 .xy|, the content centre in mesh units) and computes the ratios from it: 29500 1.77769 in u and 1.77768 in v, 29498 0.57142 in both; the factor applied is their mean (x1.7777, x0.5714). A draw is the table's part only when its icon mesh was read and its x and z extents and the record's scale are each within 1% of the table's; otherwise it is skipped and counted. The scale has no ADS gate and no ramp: 29500's rectangle is empty at the hip in every frame of both hip dumps, so per-part scaling is right on every frame, the game's switch frame included. In `f85bDecide` the map is applied to the getter frame U right after it is formed and ahead of F86d's panel, so its rays probe the final place; the correction's angle, yaw and pitch figures come from the game's U (a scaled M3 is not a rotation), and the proof's target moves with the same map.

**B: the pistol's readout.** Pistol only: the held item's name starts `$wpn_s_pistol_` and its serial is the held item's; a pistol name with another serial, or an item that cannot be read, is refused and counted, and the rifle and the tools keep F85b's rows bit for bit. The weapon frame R_w: the FP right attach (joint 51, snapshot slot `kIkFpSlotAttach[1]`) taken to the world through F_root; b is its row most aligned with F_root's forward, signed (`barrelRow`, within 45 degrees); u the remaining row most aligned with F_root's up, made orthogonal to b; l = b x u, signed against F_root's right. A snapshot older than `kIkFreshMs` takes F_root's own rows (counted as "F_root only"); no fresh F_root gives no frame and no move (counted as "none"). The target T = T0 + toward unit(E - T0), T0 = C501 + forward b + left l + up u, with C501 the game's content centre and E the deciding eye's origin from its scene rows. T is latched in absolute world once a render frame by whichever eye decides 29501 first; the other eye and 29498 reuse it. 29501 moves by T - C501; 29498, scaled by A, moves by T - C498, so the ADS content lands on the hip spot at the hip size. A 29498 draw ahead of its frame's latch takes the previous frame's T (counted); with no T of this frame or the last it is only scaled (refused, counted). The facing is the game's: a translation leaves U3 unchanged.

**The keys** (TEMPORARY, live, read in `readKey`'s config tick with a line on each change, `native on foot pistol readout (F90, TEMPORARY)`): `fix.native_on_foot_pistol_readout_forward_cm` (-10..30, default 13), `_left_cm` (-15..15, 6), `_up_cm` (-10..10, -3), `_toward_eye_cm` (-5..10, 2); step 0.5 cm, clamped, a value that is not finite reads 0. Menu sliders "Pistol readout forward", "Pistol readout left", "Pistol readout up" and "Pistol readout toward you" on the Native on foot page, after "Body follows weapon eye offset" (file order in `edvr.ini`, each with its own comment block). All four 0 leaves F85b's rows bit for bit in both eyes. The pilot's values become the behaviour.

**Item 3: the pilot's values are the built-in defaults.** `f88::kForwardDefaultCm` 9.0, `kUpDefaultCm` 7.5, `kRightDefaultCm` 0.0, `kWeaponViewOffsetDefaultCm` 2.0 (body stays `follow`, the settle stays 0.1 s), and the shipped `edvr.ini` rows (value and `recommended`). The live game ini already holds these values.

**Lines.** `weapon readout size (F90) 1 s: part %u (slot %u) x%.4f about mesh (%+.2f, %+.2f): decided %llu, scaled %llu (eye 0 %llu, eye 1 %llu), table mismatch %llu (mesh %.2f x %.2f scale %.5f); ADS weight at the scaled draws min %.2f max %.2f; content centre %.3f m from eye 0`, once a second for each table part other than 29501 that was decided (the mesh is the last mismatch's, else the last decision's); when parts were decided and none of them is such a part, `weapon readout size (F90) 1 s: no table part among the decided parts (names %s)`. No F90 line while F85b decides parts means the decision never ran. `pistol readout (F90) 1 s: item %s serial %u; keys forward %+.1f left %+.1f up %+.1f toward eye %+.1f cm (TEMPORARY); frame: attach 51 %llu, F_root only %llu, none %llu (barrel vs aim mean %.1f max %.1f deg); target latched %llu frames; moved: 29501 %llu, 29498 %llu (x%.4f), previous-frame target %llu, refused %llu [%s]; content shift mean %.1f cm; content %.3f m from eye 0, nearest surface on its rays %.3f m (%s)`, once a second while a pistol is held on foot (zeros then mean nothing ran) or while any of its counters moved; the last field is `in front by` or `behind a surface by` (F86d's D_s for the moved panel against the content's distance), `no surface hit yet` or `no eye 0 placement this second`. Distances are from eye 0's origin, not K's (the F86d/F86e bias the review found).

**Deviations from the review.** (1) `texelRatio` is texels/m(part) / texels/m(29501); the review's formula text has it inverted (that would give 0.5625 for 29500), while its values (1.77768, 0.5714) are this one's. The table carries |b2 row 6 .xy| times a common 0.9995 (the packed span, which cancels), so the printed factor for 29500 is 1.7777, not the flight plan's 1.7778 (16/9 within 1e-4). (2) A scales 29500 and 29498 for any item; B then only translates 29498 (the review's B lists its scale again; it is applied once). (3) 29501's content centre comes from the table, so B also requires 29501's mesh and scale to match (refusal `table mismatch`). (4) An attach with no row within 45 degrees of F_root's forward also takes F_root's rows, counted as "F_root only". (5) With all four keys 0, 29501 is not moved at all, rather than by T - C501, which in the second eye is a float rounding away from zero; 29498 still moves onto T = C501. (6) The previous frame's T is taken only from the frame immediately before. (7) The mesh check reads the icon mesh's x and z extents (the panel's right and up); an unread mesh counts as a table mismatch (mesh 0.00 x 0.00). (8) The pistol line's `(x0.5714)` is printed from the table, not as a literal. (9) For a moved or scaled part, F85b's `unpatched anchor error` is measured against the F90 target, so it includes the move; the corrected residual is the proof. F86e's quad figures stay the record's unscaled size, measured from K's origin as before. (10) The F89 rig check of the follow body read the F89 pilot values literally; it now reads the built-in values.

**Rig** (`run90`, `tools\native_frame_test\f90_checks.h`, on the F85 two-eye rig: 6.06 cm, 10.75 degree cant, U the head turned 30 degrees). The texel ratios from the table: 29500 16/9 and 29498 4/7 in u and v (1e-3), 29501 exactly 1, the review's 10022 and 17816 texels/m. Part 29500 (record words `0x81892657`/`0xDB557FE1`, scale 0.0501, mesh 3x1): its content centre's world point moves by 4.6e-16 m, the corner-to-centre distances grow by the factor (1.1e-14), both eyes' corners land 0.00067 px from W_e(U' v) through the stored float rows and the proof residual is 0.00058 px; the scaled correction is no rotation while the game's U still gives the head's 30 degrees. ADS weight 0 and 1 give the same 29500 rows bit for bit; the rifle's 29501, a part outside the table, and a pistol with all four keys 0 (either eye first) keep F85b's rows bit for bit. Pistol keys (13, 6, -3, 2): 29501's content lands on T (1e-6 m) with U3 untouched; the other eye reuses T and the weapon frame is read once a frame; 29498 lands on T at the hip's texels per metre (1e-3); T is 13 cm along the barrel, 6 cm left, 3 cm down and then 2 cm toward the deciding eye; eye 1 first latches T for both eyes; a 29498 ahead of the latch takes the previous frame's T; two frames old, another item or none yet is refused and only scaled; a pistol name whose serial is not the held item's is refused. The weapon frame: an attach 7 degrees off F_root gives b on its signed barrel row, l against F_root's right, orthonormal to 1e-9; a stale snapshot and an attach with no row within 45 degrees take F_root's rows; no fresh F_root gives none; in the decision the stale case still moves and the none case leaves F85b's rows. A table mismatch (scale 0.06, a 2x1 mesh for 29500, an unread mesh) is skipped, counted and leaves the rows; the pistol's is refused. The keys' ranges, NaN and the defaults.

**Rig counts.** native_frame_test 683 -> 715 (32 new, none lost; the F89 follow check reads the built-in values now); hotkey_capture_test 657 -> 657 (the Native on foot page has 42 rows, was 38); reticle_depth_test 54 -> 54.

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock`: green on the second pass, receipt `2d92f9b36dff...` over the uncommitted tree, 182.9 s (test rigs 157.3 s); d3d11.dll 29,765,632 bytes, openvr_api.dll 835,072, edvr-installer.exe 170,518,016. The first pass failed two checks: the F89 follow check (the old pilot values written into it) and F90's corner check, whose first record put the scaled 29500 panel about 70 degrees off the eyes' axes, where the projection itself blows up; the record now sits 0.52 m out, within 23 degrees of both eyes. No gate was bypassed. Not installed, not flown, `--dll-only` not run (a branch build). **For the flight,** beyond the review's section 6: F85b's `draws of more than one instance` should be 0 for the slots of 29500, 29501 and 29498 (the rows are per draw, the F90 map per part); the A line for 29500 should read `table mismatch 0` with mesh 3.00 x 1.00 and scale 0.05010 (anything else means A never applied, and the mesh it prints is the one to compare); at the hip 29500 is decided too (its rectangle is empty), so `scaled` should equal `decided` there as well; the pistol line should read `attach 51` on every frame (`F_root only` is a stale snapshot), and its keys line should show the four built-in values unless the live ini says otherwise.

### 2026-10-24: F91a (the weapon readout parts known by mesh and scale, not by name)
From the F90 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_080707`, Epic, build eff7b9de, Pimax OpenXR, Pimax Crystal Super): F90 applied nothing. All 148 `weapon readout size (F90)` lines read `no table part among the decided parts (names 29510, 29526)` and all 79 `pistol readout (F90)` lines were zero (attach 51 0, moved 0). F90's table was keyed on the F89 session's name ids, and the game interns them per session: the same four parts were 29501/29500/29498/29495 in F89 and 29510/29526/29524/29553 in F90. The slots follow the first-submit order, so they matched only because the rifle came first in both flights. The F91 review (`reviews\native-onfoot-f91-review-2026-10-24.md`, section 1) found what is stable: each part's layout (width, height, base scale), the record's scale and mesh, b2 row 6, the texture and the content counts are bit-identical in both sessions. F91a is that review's part A; parts B (the ADS vibration instrument) and C (the ADS item gate) are built separately.

**The identity.** `native_on_foot_f90.h`: the table's `Part.name` is now `Part.role` (`kRoleHip`, `kRoleRifleAds`, `kRolePistolAds`, `kRoleTool`), with the measured record scales (hip 2x1 0.072070308, rifle ADS 3x1 0.0500976555, pistol ADS 2x1 0.166796863) and a tool row (2x1 0.0939941406) that is never scaled or moved and exists so the log names it; each row also carries the layout's base scale (0.400000006, 0.224999994, 0.699999988, 0.699999988). `partOf(meshRead, ext, recScale)` returns the unique row whose mesh x and z extents and record scale are each within 1% (`kMatchTol`), else none; the four scales are at least 1.3x apart. `decide()` looks the part up that way, so F90's mismatch guard is now the lookup, and A's scale and B's condition key on the role; `In.name` stays, for the lines only. The factor is unchanged in kind (the UV-derived ratio to the hip row): with the measured scales it is x1.7778 for the rifle's ADS readout and x0.5714 for the pistol's, the layouts' base ratios 0.4/0.225 and 0.4/0.7 within 1e-5. F90's A map and proof and B's frame, latch, keys and line run as built.

**The lines.** At a slot's first decision after the held item changes, and whenever its role changes: `weapon readout part (F91): item %s serial %u, slot %u name %u: mesh %.2f x %.2f, record scale %.6f, layout %.6f x %.6f base %.6f -> %s`, with `hip readout (x1.0000)`, `rifle ADS readout (x1.7778)`, `pistol ADS readout (x0.5714)`, `tool readout (not scaled)` or `NO TABLE PART: not scaled, not moved`. A role that keeps changing for one item is named at most 8 times; the ninth change prints once that further changes are counted, not logged. The layout is the slot's first valid submit sample (width, height, base scale), kept in `f85bTagSubmit` right after the slot is assigned, before the U is published; zeros mean no valid sample yet. The 1 s line is `weapon readout size (F90) 1 s: %s (slot %u name %u) x%.4f about mesh ...`, one for each decided part whose role is not the hip's (the rifle's and the pistol's ADS readouts, and the tool's at x1.0000 with `scaled 0`); its `table mismatch` now counts that slot's decisions that matched no row. When parts were decided and none matched a row: `weapon readout size (F91) 1 s: no table part among the decided parts (slot %u name %u mesh %.2f x %.2f scale %.6f; ...)`. The pistol line's `moved: 29501 %llu, 29498 %llu` reads `moved: hip readout %llu, pistol ADS readout %llu`, and its refusal `table mismatch` is gone (a record that matches no row is no B part).

- ruled out: the part name id as an identity, because the same parts are 29501/29500/29498/29495 in F89 and 29510/29526/29524/29553 in F90.
- ruled out: the slot as an identity, because `f85bSlotOf` assigns slots in first-submit order, which matched only because the rifle came first in both flights.
- ruled out: F90 changing the rifle's ADS readout, because A applied to 0 parts and the readout box is 85x75 px in dump 080918297001 against 85x74 in 064843396001.

**Deviations from the review.** (1) A's rig checks live in `run90` (`tools\native_frame_test\f90_checks.h`, rewritten to roles, none lost) rather than a new `f91_checks.h`/`run91`, which parts B and C use. (2) The rifle's factor prints x1.7778, not the review's x1.7777: with the measured record scales the UV-derived ratio is 1.777778. (3) The 1 s size line keeps its `(F90)` prefix and prints the role first; the tool's role gets that line too (x1.0000, scaled 0), so a tool held shows the part was recognised; the idle form prints only when no decided part matched a row. (4) The role line is capped at 8 changes per held item and slot, so a mesh read on some frames only cannot flood the log. (5) The pistol line's part labels are roles, not the F89 names.

**Rig.** native_frame_test 715 -> 723 (8 new, none lost): the four measured parts give their roles with F89's names, F90's, 0 and garbage; each matches exactly one row; the same draws named by either session give bit-identical rows (29526 x1.7778; with a pistol held 29510 moved to T, 29524 x0.5714 and moved), and with the pistol's parts drawn first under garbage names the rows are the same (its ADS readout takes the previous frame's T, counted); the role line comes once per held item and slot in either slot order, again on an item change, and a flipping role is capped and counted; the factor equals the base-scale ratio within 1e-3; the tool's readout is named and keeps F85b's rows bit for bit; a scale 2% off each row (either way), a 3x1 mesh at 0.0721, F90's two mismatch cases and an unread mesh match no row and leave the rows. F90's A rig now reads factor 1.777778, corners 0.00029 px, proof 0.00023 px. hotkey_capture_test 657 -> 657 (no new row); reticle_depth_test 54 -> 54.

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock`: green on the first pass, receipt `72d49d172bce...` over the uncommitted tree, 186.5 s (test rigs 145.1 s); d3d11.dll 29,768,192 bytes, openvr_api.dll 835,072, edvr-installer.exe 170,520,576. Not installed, not flown, `--dll-only` not run (a branch build). **For the flight:** every weapon part's `weapon readout part (F91)` line should name a role (no `NO TABLE PART`) and print the layout beside the mesh; the rifle's ADS readout should read `scaled` equal to `decided`; with the pistol, the pistol line should read `attach 51` and `moved: hip readout` above 0, and `pistol ADS readout` above 0 in ADS.

### 2026-10-24: F91bc (the ADS vibration instrument, the ADS item gate) and the F91 integration
Parts B and C of the F91 review (`reviews\native-onfoot-f91-review-2026-10-24.md`, sections 2 and 3), built and validated on their own branch (`F91bc`, native_frame_test 715 -> 740 there) and merged here with F91a and the `edvr_log --health` check.

**B (instrument only).** Each press is now traced for 4 s (a release still 1.5 s, still at most 4 events a minute). Every `ads transition (F89) ... frame` line ends in `; view (F91): r ... (rows frame, head seq), applied, head step, view step, cancel total; sight along/perp; asked, cancel this frame, cancel in the pose (N frames old), rifle in view (+step)`. It reads `r -` when the head aim is not engaged. Every 0.5 s of a press gives one `ads transition (F89) press fit (F91) window N` line: STILL (net head turn under 2 deg/s) or MOVING, the tremor and rifle-jitter rms, the correlation of the head's step with r's step and the rifle's step at lags 0, 1 and 2, and a verdict H1 (d frames late), LOCK or H3. A trace line without `; view (F91)` means the fields never ran. Deviations: the sign of each axis is fitted and r's offset removed per residual base; H3's sight error under 0.1 mm is read as its frame-to-frame step (absolute perp is 0.3-0.5 mm still); view step = head step uses 0.01 deg rms; an overshoot is a still window that moves the error back by 0.2 mm or more after a moving window; one reversal suffices for LOCK; the cancel's frame lag comes from the eye-alignment serial (exact only while the alignment is republished every tick, else an upper bound); adsAimTick's state edges now come from the pure adsStateAfter (behaviour unchanged).

**C.** Our ADS engages only for F83's native primary or a held item named `$wpn_*` (any case). A refused press logs `native on foot ADS item gate (F91): refused: the held item "%s" is not a weapon (tool|fists|unknown); request, source, journal`, with `stopped` and `allowed` lines; the F70b 1 s line gains `item gate refused %u ticks`. A weapon switched to a tool mid-press releases through the normal 0.3 s unwind (the gate drops `ads`, it does not call adsStop, which would drop the carried yaw at once).

**Integration.** `claude/onfoot-ads-gate` (F91bc) and `claude/onfoot-health-check` (`edvr_log --health`) merged with `--no-ff` onto F91a, one at a time; git merged both with no conflict. The places where the two sides meet were checked by hand: native_frame_test runs `run90` (F90 and F91a) and then `run91bc`, both included; `native_on_foot.cpp` includes `native_on_foot_f90.h` and `native_on_foot_f91.h` (separate namespaces `nofoot::f90` and `nofoot::f91`); F91bc's `f89_checks.h` edits sit on F90's defaults; the hotkey rig's Native on foot page stays at 42 rows (F91 adds no key). **Health rules** (`tools\edvr_log.py`, `HEALTH_RULES`): the size rule reads the F91 lines (`%s (slot %u name %u)`, the role first; the idle form `weapon readout size (F91) 1 s: no table part among the decided parts (slot .. name .. mesh .. scale ..)`) and still the F90 build's; only the ADS readouts decide its status, the tool's readout alone is IDLE. New `weapon readout part (F91)`: the last role line of each (item serial, slot) decides; `NO TABLE PART` there is NEVER APPLIED for that item, a miss the slot later matched is PARTIAL. The pistol rule reads `moved: hip readout N, pistol ADS readout N` and the F90 build's numbered labels.
