New `ADS press fit (F91)`: windows, STILL and MOVING, verdict counts (H1 by d, LOCK, H3, none, too few r frames), each split STILL/MOVING; NEVER APPLIED when no window had enough r frames. The F89 trace rule ignores the fit lines (they carry its label) and counts the frame lines with `; view (F91)` fields and with r shown. New `ADS item gate (F91)`: refused (by item and reason), stopped, allowed, and the F70b line's refused ticks; IDLE when nothing was refused. The self-test still renders every fixture from the C format strings (native_on_foot.cpp, and native_on_foot_f91.h for the press fit's pieces), plus one fixture of the F90 flight's own lines for the older format: 80 -> 97 checks, all pass.

**Rig counts.** native_frame_test 723 (F91a) + 25 (F91bc) = 748, 0 failures; hotkey_capture_test 657; reticle_depth_test 54; openxr_native_test 5281; `edvr_log --health` self-test 97.

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock` of the merged tree: green on the first pass, receipt `1fe41f83116d...`, 195.5 s (test rigs 152.0 s); d3d11.dll 29,793,280 bytes, openvr_api.dll 835,072, edvr-installer.exe 170,545,664. Not installed, not flown, `--dll-only` not run (a branch build). **For the flight:** run `python tools\edvr_log.py --file <the gfx log> --health` first; then the F91 review's section 5.

### 2026-10-24: F92a (the pilot's pistol values, the rifle's ADS readout placed, each placement only for its class)
From the F91 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_092147`, Epic, build e0975724, Pimax OpenXR; dumps 092535205001 rifle ADS, 092548037002 pistol, 092739381003 and 092748651004 charge port; `edvr_log --health` summarises it). F91a works: the ADS readouts scaled 36685 of 36689 decisions, the pistol readout moved 41940 draws. The pilot: "Pistol ui looks good with these offsets" (forward 13.0, left 3.5, up -3.0, toward eye 3.0 cm), and "The rifle UI when ADS clips into the gun itself, could we configure its location as well?"

**The pistol's values.** `f90::kForwardDefaultCm` 13, `kLeftDefaultCm` 3.5, `kUpDefaultCm` -3, `kTowardDefaultCm` 3, and the shipped `edvr.ini` rows (value and `recommended`). The keys stay temporary.

**The rifle's ADS readout: what clips.** Dump 092535205001 is the rifle in ADS (the log: the rifle ADS readout, slot 0 name 29626, scaled at ADS weight 1.00 from 09:25:30 to 09:25:35). The F90 size line puts its content 0.302 m from eye 0, and F86d's rays for that slot find the nearest surface at 0.172 m (the centre ray 0.172 m): the readout lies about 13 cm behind gun surfaces along eye 0's rays. In the right eye (the sighting eye; `FinalCrisp_R00`) the content sits on the receiver's top centre, behind the scope mount, about 0.3 cm left of the receiver's centre, with its second line half hidden under the rim of the receiver's recess: the game draws it into the gun. At that row the receiver is about 350 native px wide, 5.5 cm at 0.30 m (f = 1894 px), and the content about 145 x 105 px, 2.3 x 1.7 cm (the hip's size after F90's scale). **The defaults:** left 5 cm (forward, up and toward eye 0): the content's right edge then lies about 1 cm past the receiver's left edge, beside the gun at the receiver's height, below and to the side of the sight line, with nothing of the gun between it and the right eye. These numbers are from one dump and the image; the new line measures the result.

**The placement.** `native_on_foot_f90.h`: the pistol's machinery for the rifle's ADS readout role. `decide()`'s placement step (`place`) serves both: the first decision of a render frame reads the weapon frame (the FP right attach through F_root, as for the pistol) and latches T = T0 + toward unit(E - T0), T0 = C + forward b + left l + up u; the other eye reuses T. The rifle has its own latch (`Latch::rifle`) and keys (`In::rifleKeys`, `fix.native_on_foot_rifle_ads_readout_forward_cm`, `_left_cm`, `_up_cm`, `_toward_eye_cm`: TEMPORARY, live, the pistol's ranges, step 0.5, menu sliders "Rifle ADS readout forward / left / up / toward you" on the Native on foot page after the pistol's four, a line on each change `native on foot rifle ADS readout (F92, TEMPORARY)`). The move is a translation on top of A's scale about the content centre, so the content lands on T at the hip's size and U3 stays the scaled, unturned U3. All four 0 leave F91's rows bit for bit. It applies only with a `$wpn_m_` item held (serial checked); at the hip the part's rectangle is empty, so the move shows only in ADS. Line: `rifle ADS readout placement (F92) 1 s: item %s serial %u; keys forward %+.1f left %+.1f up %+.1f toward eye %+.1f cm (TEMPORARY); frame: attach 51 %llu, F_root only %llu, none %llu (barrel vs aim mean %.1f max %.1f deg); target latched %llu frames; moved %llu (ADS weight at the moved draws min %.2f max %.2f), refused %llu [%s]; content shift mean %.1f cm; content %.3f m from eye 0, nearest surface on its rays %.3f m (%s)`, once a second while a primary is held on foot (zeros then mean nothing ran) or while a counter moved.

**The role check.** The `weapon readout part (F91)` lines the health check flagged (the pistol's ADS readout on the rifle, the rifle's on the pistol, the tool's on the pistol and the repair tool, the rifle's on the fists) are switch windows, not wrong roles: the game draws the next item's parts 0.1 to 0.9 s before Status.json names it (09:23:54.705 the pistol's ADS part with the rifle held, 09:23:55.195 the pistol; 09:26:29.174 the rifle's ADS part with the pistol held, 09:26:30.063 the rifle), and the line names the item held at the slot's first decision. The A scale is per part and right in any frame. The pistol placement is not: the hip readout is one part (one mesh, scale and name) for the rifle and the pistol, and the pistol line's seconds that ended with the rifle held show moves in the switch window before Status.json changed: 09:25:20 (13 latched frames), 09:26:30 (46) and 09:27:06 (60), the rifle's hip readout moved by the pistol's offsets for up to 0.9 s (and 09:24:00, 28 frames, the pistol's own readout while a tool was drawn). It never ran with the rifle named as held. **Fix:** each class's own part is evidence of what the game draws (the rifle's ADS readout, the pistol's ADS readout, the tool's readout; each drawn every frame its item is out, the pistol's ADS part at the hip too); a placement is refused (`kBRefClass`, `another class's part drawn: a switch`, counted) while another class's part was decided this render frame or the last, so the readout stays where the game draws it until the switch settles. The A scale stays per part. **The role line** now says what the decision applied against what it only matched: `rifle ADS readout (x1.7778): applied: scaled, rifle placement`, `hip readout (x1.0000): matched only (no placement for the held item)`, `hip readout (x1.0000): matched only; pistol placement refused (another class's part is drawn: a switch)`, `rifle ADS readout (x1.7778): applied: scaled` (a pistol held); a change of what was applied is a new line (the same cap of 8 per held item and slot).

**`--health`.** New rule `rifle ADS readout (F92)`: held seconds, the weapon frame, latched, moved (and the seconds in ADS), refusals, and the last ADS second's content against the surface on its rays; NEVER APPLIED when nothing moved. The role rule names, per item, the roles applied, `[matched only]` or `[<placement> refused: <why>]`. The self-test renders the new fixtures from the C formats (the role text from `native_on_foot_f90.h`'s `roleText`): 97 -> 103 checks.

**Deviations from the request.** (1) "While a `$wpn_m_` weapon is held" is the held item's name (serial checked), as for the pistol; F83's native primary is not used here. (2) The class gate uses the parts drawn in the last two render frames, not a time window. (3) The pistol placement is also refused while a tool's readout is drawn (the pistol's readout then stays where the game draws it for the rest of the holster). (4) The defaults come from the image and one dump; no depth map was read for them.

**Rig.** native_frame_test 748 -> 757 (9 new; the F90 default and T checks rewritten for the new pistol values, none lost): the rifle keys' defaults; keys 0 with a primary held give F91's rows bit for bit in both eyes, either eye first; the defaults put the content on T, 5 cm along the weapon's left, one T and one frame read per frame; the move leaves A's scaled U3; the rifle placement never with a pistol held and the pistol's never with a primary; the F91 switch (a pistol held while the rifle's ADS part is drawn) leaves the hip readout as F85b's and counts it; a primary with the pistol's or a tool's part drawn, and a pistol with a tool's, are refused; a pistol with only its own parts is placed as in F90; the role text for applied, matched only, refused and no table part, and a change of what was applied is a new line. hotkey_capture_test 657 -> 657 (the Native on foot page has 46 rows, was 42); reticle_depth_test 54 -> 54; `edvr_log --health` self-test 97 -> 103.

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock`: green on the first pass, receipt `dfade0d1535c...` over the uncommitted tree, 200.3 s (test rigs 158.2 s); d3d11.dll 29,800,960 bytes, openvr_api.dll 835,072, edvr-installer.exe 170,557,952. Not installed, not flown, `--dll-only` not run (a branch build).

### 2026-10-24: F92b (the still-head ADS vibration: the head aim's cancel published ahead; "ADS: aim with head")
With the head still, the head aim's cancel landed one frame after the game turned the camera: in all 22 STILL windows r (view less head, on the previous frame's rows) equals the game's look step applied that frame (fit 0.0003-0.0008 deg rms against r's 0.007-0.025 deg; head tremor 0.008-0.027 deg a frame); the game applies each ask exactly one tick later (0.0002-0.0028 deg rms). So each tremor step showed for one frame as a turn the head did not make. The F91 fit compared r with steps ending at the tick, not at r's rows, which gave the 23 'none' verdicts. Fix: publish each ask's cancel when the ask is queued, so it reaches the frame where the game renders the turn; asks unchanged (deliberate turns 1:1); the weapon is now one frame off the eye (the game's input latency, the floor). Toggle 'ADS: aim with head' (user key, live): off mid-press unwinds like a release while the press carries on; on engages fresh. Deviation: with the key off the press still runs (before, the key turned it all off); the look hooks install in VR regardless. Open: a slow 0.4 deg yaw offset with ~0.02 deg rms wander in r on pistol presses. The 16-frame dumps are unusable for per-frame motion (the capture stalls the frame thread).

### 2026-10-24: F92c (the prompt label census keyed on the type-1000 indicator owner)
F91 flight 092147: the F87p census never ran: the focus-prompt function 0x1AA99C0 hid its marker every frame, which reset the key. The labels' owner is 0x1AA9830, which calls UpdateIndicator then SetMarker(key [this+0x10C], P, visible 1, offset 0); caller 0x1AA9778 passes type 1000, 0x1AAE644 and 0x1AB67CB pass 1001. F92c keys the focus on the type-1000 owner and logs indicators by type and the HUD placement. A CPU replay of B10B on the captured vertices (dumps 092739381003, 092748651004) shows the 3600- and 624-index camera-local draws are the HUD dome, not the label; no 6-index quad covers the label. In unlayered eye-frames the label sits by its dot as in flat; layered, it is displaced (-346, -237) px. Open: whether EDVR's on-foot HUD placement causes it; next flight A/B with the HUD settings neutral.

### 2026-10-24: F93a (the pilot's rifle value; the readout class from the parts the game draws, so a swap places from its first frame)
From the F92 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_102707`, Epic, build ed903ba3; `--health` summarises it). The pilot: "Rifle ADS ui looks good, set the default" (left 3.5 cm, the rest 0), and "When swapping to the pistol from the rifle, the pistol weapon ui briefly shows at its original and incorrect position before jumping to the correct offset".

**The default.** `f90::kRifleLeftDefaultCm` 3.5 (forward, up and toward eye 0) and the shipped `edvr.ini` row (value and `recommended`). The keys stay temporary.

**The swap, from the log.** One rifle-to-pistol swap in the flight. The rifle's ADS readout part (slot 1, name 29625) was decided 38 frames in the second to 10:29:40.922 and 10 in the second to 10:29:41.927, with the rifle placement applied on all of them (latched 10, two of them in F_root's frame as the rifle went away). The pistol's ADS readout part (slot 2, name 29623), which only the pistol draws, was first decided at 10:29:41.057 (role line: `pistol ADS readout (x0.5714): applied: scaled`, item serial 2, the rifle) and in 79 frames of that second; 10 + 79 = 89 frames, so the two items' parts did not overlap. Status.json named the pistol at 10:29:42.072 (`SelectedWeapon "$wpn_s_pistol_kinetic_sauto_name;"`), and the first placed frame was 10:29:42.082 (role lines: `hip readout (x1.0000): applied: pistol placement`, `pistol ADS readout (x0.5714): applied: scaled, pistol placement`). Between 41.057 and 42.082 (about 1.03 s, some 92 frames at 89 Hz) the held item read the rifle, the pistol placement was not tried (the hip readout's state stayed `matched only (no placement for the held item)`, no role line changed), and F92a's class guard never refused: the pistol readout sat at the game's spot, over the rear sight, until Status.json caught up. The F91 flight's three pistol-to-rifle swaps had the same lag the other way (0.1 to 0.9 s).

**The signal.** Each class draws a readout part only it has, every frame its item is out: the rifle's ADS readout (at the hip too), the pistol's ADS readout (at the hip too), the tool's readout. Their first and last frames are the game's draw itself, so the class is decided from them (`f90::DrawnTracker`): the class whose own part was decided most recently, within this render frame or the last, is the class the game draws; two classes' parts in the newest frame is a switch in progress (`mixed`), refused as before (`another class's part drawn: a switch`), since the hip readout is one part for the rifle and the pistol. The class part counts for its own decision (it is seen before it is decided). The pistol placement runs while the drawn class is the pistol, the rifle placement while it is the primary; Status.json's item no longer gates them, it only vetoes (`Status.json names the other weapon class`, counted) once the drawn class has stood longer than 3 s (`kClassGraceS`; the longest lag seen is 1.0 s) and names the other weapon class, which would mean a weapon this table does not know shares a part. The placements' latches are keyed on the drawn class's epoch instead of Status.json's serial. Other signals considered: F83's native read changed about 0.15 s before the pistol's first part (`primary=0` at 10:29:40.902), but it says only "not the primary" (tools and fists too); the FP right attach (joint 51) is present for both weapons. The class parts are the only signal that is both first and specific. **The draw order** within a frame is the game's and unknown (the F91 and F92 sessions differ), so at the first frame of a swap the shared hip readout may be decided before the new class's part: rifle to pistol, the hip readout is then left at the game's spot for that one draw (the other eye places it); pistol to rifle, that one draw can still carry the pistol's offsets. Every later draw follows the parts.

**The line.** `readout swap (F93a): item %s -> %s, first draw frame %u, first placed frame %u, gap %u; Status.json named "%s" at the first draw`, the items being the drawn classes (`primary`, `pistol`, `tool`, `none`), from the first frame a weapon class's own part appears after two frames without it to the first frame a placement of that class is applied; `... NOT PLACED in 3 s; ...` when none was. `--health`: new rule `readout swap (F93a)`: OK when every gap is 0, PARTIAL for a gap, NEVER APPLIED for a swap never placed; self-test 114 -> 117 checks. The F92 flight's log still parses (no UNPARSED rule; the swap rule is not present there).

### 2026-10-24: F93b (the still ADS vibration: the ADS eye hold's late cancel; the hold off, the sight lock fed from the drawn alignment)
The vibration that stays with head aim off is the ADS eye hold (F62b's position lock): it cancels the head's translation through the eye alignment, which reaches the pose two frames late (serial lag 2 on every frame of the F92 log), so each frame of head tremor shows for two frames as the eye moving against the weapon. Flight: every still rifle window's sight-error step is 0.080-0.086 mm rms with autocorrelation ~0 at lag 1 and -0.41 at lag 2; a plant with the flight's timing reproduces it only with that mechanism (0.057 mm/frame head translation -> 0.086 mm and -0.44). With the hold off the plant gives 0.014 mm/frame. F93b turns the hold off by default (fix.native_on_foot_ads_eye_hold, TEMPORARY, hidden, latched per press; the eye follows the head's own translation and the sight lock keeps it on the sight line; a 2 cm lean returns within 0.5 s) and feeds the sight lock from the drawn alignment (fix.native_on_foot_ads_eye_steady, TEMPORARY, default on; the plant's 3.6 mm entry overshoot goes to none). Open: the pistol 0.4 deg yaw offset (seen in F91 right after the F69i centring snap); next flight one pistol press with head aim on.

F93c reads that offset per key and moves a marker that has one by its nearest plain marker's correction. ### 2026-10-24: F94a (the neck as the source of truth for the view's height; the body at its animated height)
From the F93 flight (`edvr_gfx_20261024_124854`, Epic, the right build): the pilot, after flying: with the knees bent even with nothing drawn "it feels a bit like I have no neck"; the neck height should decide how far the headset sits above the torso, the weapon eye offset only applies with something out. The `stance height (F93e)` lines show F93e worked as built and was the wrong cure: fists standing, aim -31 deg, the game's camera 1.553 m, the animated neck 1.542 m, the rest target lowered the neck 13.7 cm and the hidden head took all of it (pitched 52 deg, the body 7.9 cm further back), legs 0.996 written and animated. The view sat at the game's own height, a few centimetres over the neck. With the rifle and pistol the leaning stances (neck 1.30-1.39 m) were raised 2-11 cm (`raised in 89 (by 10.4 cm mean)`, legs 0.980 written against 1.003). The tool counts as armed (F88's w 1.00 with the Energylink, up then 15 cm), so with a tool the view was F88's.

**The measurement.** The skeleton's natural neck-to-eye height, standing level, from the animated head: the pin's calibration (12:50:10) has the head joint 44 at 1.660 m with the neck joint 34 at 1.543 m (11.7 cm), and the calibrated eye point 3.9 cm up and 3.8 cm forward of it in the head's frame (`e_h` 0.0033, 0.0391, 0.0381 m): 15.5-15.6 cm, the rest offset's 15.5 (the calibration is built on that rest vector, so the head joint's 11.7 cm is the independent part of the figure). The game's own camera, head pitch taken out (`eye height raw/norm` in the F69a lines, 12:50:13-18), stands 3.7-4.2 cm over the same neck (1.577-1.585 m). The pilot's old F64 value (11.5 cm over the game's camera) put his eye 15.2-15.7 cm over the neck. Default 15.5.

**The body.** `nofoot::f94::bodyKeepsNeckHeight` (`native_on_foot_f94.h`): the pin's neck target keeps the animated neck's height in every stance, after the ADS detach and F74's shift, and in F82's laser-rifle physical-rest target; the horizontal target is the rest offset's as before. No lowering, no raise: the legs are the animation's (the leg solve sees a vertical move of 0). F93e's call, its `NeckTake`, its stats bins and its `stance height (F93e)` line and health rule are removed (nothing is left for the head to take).

**The view.** `fix.native_on_foot_eye_height_cm` is a live user key again with a new meaning: how far the eyes sit above the commander's animated neck with the head level (0..30 cm, step 0.5, default 15.5; menu "Eye height" on the Native on foot page before "Weapon view offset"; `retired-default: 4`, so the installer's merge replaces a stock file's old 4). The Animate post-hook forms the raise the view needs, R = neck + h - (the game's camera with the head's pitch taken out, `pinEyeHeightNormalized`), and publishes it; the frame thread (`f94Tick`, after F88's weight) follows it critically damped (95% in 0.2 s), ramps it in and out of the pin's scope over 0.5 s like the hold, weights it by 1 - w and adds its seated y (-R) to the eye alignment after the hold's term and F88's. The head's pitch still pivots the eye about the neck as before (the hold's `drop`).

**The loop.** R is measured on a camera that already carries the R the runtime applied. The frame thread keeps R's seated y by alignment serial (`SeatedHistory`, one atomic word a slot, because the reader is the P1 hook); `ikPublishG` looks up the serial the runtime reported with the pose (F89's word) and adds that y to the ADS shift S it takes out of the job camera, so the pin's camera, its calibration, the crouch weight and R's own target see the game's camera and R never sees itself. A runtime that reports no serial gets the newest published; the 0.2 s filter keeps that loop settled at 1-3 frames of delay (the rig; the same loop unfiltered runs away at 2). The hold is untouched: its input takes out of the pose the alignment the pose carried, R included (F89); with an unreporting runtime it also waits while R moves, as for F88's up.

**F88.** Armed (w 1) the neck term is an exact zero and the view is the game's camera plus w O, F88's armed view bit for bit; the pilot's values keep his armed view (`weapon_eye_up_cm` 10, `forward` 9, `right` 0 need no change). Drawing and holstering blend the two over F88's 0.25 s; while w is above 0 R's target is frozen, so the leaning stance under a draw does not pull the blend down. With up 10 the drawn rifle view is the game's 1.56 m + 10 cm against the unarmed 1.543 + 15.5 cm: the draw lowers the view about 3 cm (tools about 0); `weapon_eye_up_cm` 13 would make them agree and move his armed view up by 3 cm.

**F87h.** Unchanged: it still holds the head's lasting posture out of the view (the view now stands at the neck reference instead of the game's height) and its transient still goes to the pin. Nothing else needs a lowering: a crouch and a jump's dip are in the animated neck, and the view follows them through R (vertical only, so no fore-aft sway; F93e's 6-9 cm backward shift of upright stances is gone).

**Lines.** `native on foot eye height (F94a): fix.native_on_foot_eye_height_cm = ...` at each change; `native on foot neck height (F94a) 1 s` per held item (standing, else every state) from the pin's line: the animated neck (mean, min, max), the key, the view as drawn over the neck (head level; unarmed it should read the key), the game's camera and the skeleton's eye over it, R's target and the R the drawn camera carried (found by serial / last published / not found), the frame thread's weight 1 - w, raise, applied, frozen, stale and clamped ticks, the rest target's asked vertical move, the lowering written (mean and max |.|, 0) and the legs written against animated. `--health` rule `neck height (F94a)`: OK when unarmed standing seconds read the view within 1.5 cm of the key with no lowering and no bent knees; NEVER APPLIED when the raise never reaches the drawn camera, or the key was read and the hold held the view on foot with no 1 s line (the code never ran); IDLE when only armed or never standing; a build without F94a has no such lines (the rule reads "not present").

**Rig.** native_frame_test 814 -> 829 (`f94_checks.h`, `run94`: 25 new; F93e's 10 removed with it): the key, R on the F93 flight's figures, the body on the F92 stances (the Energylink's 9.4 cm lowering and the rifle's 11.7 cm raise asked and not applied), the filter (ramp in, no overshoot, the draw with the stance leaning under it, armed exactly 0, a holster in another stance, a stale target, the clamp, the ramp out), the history, the loop on a plant (matched: R identical tick for tick at 0, 1 and 2 frames late, the eye 15.5 cm over the neck within 1 mm standing and crouched, within 3.5 cm through a 0.4 s crouch; legacy settled at 1-3 frames; the unfiltered control runs away), F88's composition with the pilot's values (unarmed 15.5 over the neck, drawn the camera + 10 cm exactly, no step over 0.75 cm a tick through the draw) and the hold's input with R in the alignment. hotkey_capture_test 657 (the page's rows 47 -> 48); reticle_depth_test 54; openxr_native_test 5281; `edvr_log --health` self-test 138 -> 143. No other key became dead.

**Build.** Full `build.bat --wait-lock`: green on the first pass, receipt `67891b100174...`, 334.2 s (test rigs 246.5 s). Not installed, not flown. **For the flight:** first `--health`; standing with fists, nothing and the Energylink, the `neck height (F94a)` line's view over the neck near the key with weight 1.00, the lowering 0.00 and the legs written equal to animated; looking down, shoulders and legs where they belong; a draw and a holster with the rifle (no jump; the drawn view as in F93); a crouch and jumps (the view follows the neck, no fore-aft sway); "found by the alignment serial" near all calls.
